The orchestrator updates this file after every step. Status values: `todo`, `in progress`, `done` (code merged into `tauri-1.2.0`), `verified` (acceptance tests from PLAN.md passed). Dates are filled in when a status changes.

Branch: `tauri-1.2.0`. Specs: ARCHITECTURE.md, PLAN.md, INVENTORY.md. Plan: ALL AT ONCE (one release, 1.2.0, fully Rust + Tauri v2, no Node sidecar).

Now the page stores the sentinel `mc:webview-import` = import id after the entries (also after an error), the report writes an unconfirmed marker (`id`, `confirmed: false`), and an unconfirmed marker hands the entries out again with the id to look for; a later page that finds the sentinel confirms the marker (`mc_webview_import_done` with `confirmed`) and only then removes the sentinel; markers without the new fields or unreadable ones count as done; an unconfirmed import whose old database is gone becomes final. (minor) `last_drop` expiry is a pure `fresh(at, now)` / `recent_at(now)` and the test ages a record past MAX_AGE. (minor) verify-sftp-ui.mjs comments name `pathsForFiles`. Review fixes (2026-11-07; 2 major, 4 minor, all fixed): `Inflight::shared` spawns the work as its own tokio task and a drop guard removes the entry however it ends, so a panic is `WorkPanicked` (a `failed` answer through `Git::shared`) for that run's callers and a timed-out or never-polled caller leaves no stale entry (the next call runs afresh); `paths::resolve` is a lexical port of Node's `path.win32.resolve` / `path.posix.resolve` with `normalizeString` (no GetFullPathNameW: trailing dots, spaces and separators like Node; drive cwds from the `=C:` env names), checked by the new golden `git-resolve.jsonl` (618 rows: UNC, `\\?\`, drive-relative, trailing dots / spaces / separators, `..` above the root, DOS device names); `path_exists` is `fs.access` (GetFileAttributesW on the link itself on Windows, access semantics elsewhere), so a dangling junction is not-repo like 1.1.4; `RunOptions::default()` is a read with READ_TIMEOUT (never 0 s); the `ExecOptions::stop_at_cap` merge step for H3 is recorded below (item 8). After the fixes: cargo test -p unimux-git 34 (20 unit, 1 canonical-path TS comparison, 3 golden, 10 real git; new: panicking key, dropped / never-polled key, default RunOptions, host resolve, resolve golden, dangling junction), unimux-exec 8 of 9 (the known load-sensitive cap test, item 6 / 9), clippy -D warnings, fmt, dump-goldens --check, typecheck. |

Index saved to `conversation-index-v2.bin` (own binary format v3; 1.1.4's v8 `conversation-index.bin` never read or written). conv:search / conv:transcript / conv:export registered in commands/agents.rs (blocking work off the async runtime, main's fallback answers), index saved on exit (`commands::agents::on_exit` in the RunEvent::Exit hook). Bench `node scripts/bench-convsearch.mjs` (Rust vs the 1.1.4 TS indexer in Node on the same folders): faster on every measure of the 4-file fixture set and of a generated 600-file / 160 MB set, same hits (numbers below). Real app: `node scripts/verify-tauri-convsearch.mjs` 27/27 (searches, transcripts, exports equal to 1.1.4 on a scratch home, bad requests, v2 file saved on quit, old file untouched, real profile untouched); smoke-tauri 34/34. Review fixes (2026-11-07, 2 critical findings, both the same deadlock, fixed): a search of a big index (1M+ folded code units, 8+ candidates) held the entries read lock while it waited on the pass workers, whose jobs blocked on the write lock (parking_lot's fair lock then also parks new readers), so on 4 to 6 CPUs (read phase) or with files vanishing mid-pass (stat phase, also on 16 CPUs) the pass and every later search / transcript / export hung for good. Now the search takes a snapshot of the visible documents (`Arc<DocState>`) under a short read lock and matches without the lock, on its own search workers (`search_workers()`, never the pass workers, so a search also does not wait for the pass jobs ahead of it); IndexerOptions::pass_threads gives an index its own pass workers (tests force a 4-CPU machine). New tests/conv_pass_search.rs: big index + 4 pass workers, searches in a loop while a pass reads 2000 new / grown files (3 passes) and while a pass stats 6000 files with a third deleted mid-pass (3 passes), each under a 60 s limit; both fail with DEADLOCK on the old locking (checked by putting it back) and pass now, natively and in Docker rust:1.99-slim with --cpuset-cpus 0-3 (4-CPU pool sizes). See "H5 open issues" |

| P2 | Pty output engine and Channel data plane | done (Windows; acceptance partly met: the SP5 echo target under a 19-cell flood is not proven within 1.1x at p95 on this machine at 100 percent CPU; macOS compile-checked for unimux-pty / unimux-ipc only) | 2026-11-07 | 2026-11-07 | | branch `tauri-pkg-P2`. unimux-pty: `flow` (TailBuffer 512 KiB in bytes, cut forward to a UTF-8 character start, size history max 512 with the replay offsets in bytes; FlowGate 1 MiB / 256 KiB; EchoClock EWMA 0.25, 150..1000 ms; coalesce_delay 4 / 12 / 33 ms, aligned_delay), `output` (OutputChannel: 128 KiB flush now, echo flush at the end of the read within ECHO_FLUSHES 4, 3 s pause watchdog renewed by acks, 80 ms exit drain, rearm on focus / typing), `timers` (one timer thread for every channel, ManualScheduler fake clock), `batch` (WindowBatcher: one message per window, 16 ms bulk / 4 ms focus leading-edge windows, echo / exit / replay at once, 256 KiB at once, gated transports at most 2 messages posted and not released, 3 with an echo), `routes` (WindowRoutes, early sizes LRU 200), `engine` (PtyEngine between PtyManager and the windows: replay tails on attach / route, flow control on with the main window, accepts messages only from the owner window, host-side DA1 answer while the owner window has no page, timeBeginPeriod(1) while terminals exist, exit after the drained output), `frame` kinds replay (sizes header + bytes) and json. unimux-ipc PortSink: `post_frames(seq)`, `gated`, `transport`, `close`. unimux-app: commands/pty.rs registers the `pty` port owner (static engine), pty_transport.rs (WebView2 shared buffers with `{"mcPort":"pty","n":seq}` and the UI-thread wait / stall count, pull transport `mc_port_pull` long poll, Channel fallback; `MC_PTY_TRANSPORT` in dev / check builds), `mc_port_attach` answers `{transport}`, test bridge op `pty` (spawn / kill / killAll / write / route / replay / stats). Renderer: src/shared/ptyFrames.ts (frame decoder, streaming UTF-8 per terminal, replay byte offsets to character offsets, `bytes` on data messages), bridge/ptyPort.ts (port object handed to TerminalManager synchronously, release acks, shared-buffer copy + releaseBuffer, pull loop), mc.ts attachPtyPort, TerminalManager accepts the port object and Uint8Array frames and acks `bytes ?? data.length`. Verified: see "P2: verification, measurements and open issues". Review fixes 2026-11-07 (2 critical, 2 major, 5 minor, all fixed; branch `tauri-pkg-P2-fixes`, see "Review fixes" in the P2 section): timer generation guard (coalescing timer / watchdog firing before its id was stored), page releases by the host number with an attach id in the shared buffer and drops buffers of an earlier attach, failed posts released at their number and a 3 s cap timeout, resize during a spawn kept and applied, reader opens at the child exit (last output of a paused terminal), replay queued inside the channel lock, `reset` frame on spawn + decoder forget on dispose + ignoreBOM, deterministic batch tests, stats_json lock order, duplicate capability. Re-verified: fmt, clippy -D warnings (default, `dev-overrides,test-bridge`, unimux-pty for aarch64-apple-darwin); cargo test unimux-pty lib 89, conpty 14, engine 7, manager 16, plane 2 (UNIMUX_P1_FLOOD_MB=5), unimux-app 83, unimux-ipc 31; npm run typecheck; vitest 267 files / 4,031 tests (pty-frames 17); real app `verify-tauri-pty.mjs replay reload grid`: replay ok, 8 reloads under a 4 x 2 MB/s flood all kept posting with the host queue bounded and no cap timeout (1 stale buffer of an old attach dropped), grid 20 x 1 MB/s at 156 fps, in flight page 1 / host 3, unacked max 69,795 bytes. |

Patterns run on regress 0.12.0 (`utf16`) instead of a fancy-regex translation (ARCHITECTURE / PLAN updated): an ECMAScript engine over UTF-16 code units, so offsets, `i` / `u` folding, `.` on surrogate pairs, lookbehind, named groups and modifiers equal V8; vendored in `src-tauri/vendor/regress` with one patch (Annex B case-insensitive classes without `u`, so `/[a-z]/i` never matches the Kelvin sign); `jsregex.rs` checks the Annex B grammar in V8's order for V8's own error text (`Invalid regular expression: /(/gi: Unterminated group`). Commands `fs:searchProject` / `fs:replaceInProject` in unimux-app commands/files.rs on the blocking pool. Review fixes (2026-11-07, branch `tauri-pkg-G7-fixes` on top of `tauri-pkg-G7`): (1) case-insensitive `[\W]`, `[\W\d]`, `[^\W]`, `[\Ws]` with `u` no longer match k, s, U+017F, U+212A (regress patch: a negated class escape inside a class is the complement of the folded positive set, like V8's WordCharacters); (2) a query that is no string throws only in literal mode, a regex search compiles `String(value)` like 1.1.4's `new RegExp`; (3) the non-`u` fallback accepts literal astral characters in group names (regress patch joins the surrogate pair) and patterns nest up to 10,000 groups (regress limit raised from 256, deep patterns compile on a 512 MiB-stack thread); (4) unimux-app tests for the per-window cancel (`SearchRuns`: a new search of a window answers the previous one cancelled, another window's search and a GONE call leave it alone, the map forgets only the current id). search-pattern goldens +75 rows (1,357), all byte-identical; tests/search.rs 28; unimux-app 74; cancel test 25/25 repeats; clippy, fmt, macOS check, typecheck, vitest 4,014, `dump-goldens --check`, real app `verify-tauri-search` 12/12 after `npm run build:check`. |

## Wave 0 report (2026-11-06)

### Layout

- `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`: workspace (resolver 3, edition 2024, pins in `[workspace.dependencies]`, release profile lto thin / codegen-units 1 / strip / panic unwind), `rustfmt.toml` (width 130). All 19 crates exist; the domain crates are documented skeletons.
- `src-tauri/crates/unimux-app`: `tauri.conf.json`, `tauri.macos.conf.json`, `capabilities/`, `permissions/`, `build.rs` (app-command permissions, `dev_overrides` cfg), `src/main.rs` (start order), `context.rs` (AppContext), `ipc.rs` (mc_invoke, mc_send, mc_invoke_raw, mc_port_attach, mc_port_post, update_splash_*), `windows.rs`, `startup.rs`, `test_bridge.rs`, `commands/<domain>.rs` (one file per domain: app, state, window, os, pty, agents, mcp, files, git, github, ssh, sftp, db, accounts, update, plus fallbacks).
- How a package adds a command: in its `commands/<domain>.rs`, `r.invoke("git:status", move |call| async move { ... })` (or `r.send`, or `r.invoke_reply` for raw bytes). The router checks every registration against the contract, applies the guard, and a real handler replaces a wave-0 fallback. Events: `ctx.events.emit(Scope::Cell(id), "cell:hook", args)`. Ports: `ctx.ports.register("pty", owner)`. State: `ctx.state.change(...)` and `ctx.state.on_change(...)`.
- Generated: `src-tauri/gen/ipc-contract.json`, `src/renderer/src/bridge/contract.gen.ts`, `src-tauri/gen/messages.json`, `test/golden/*` (`npm run gen:check` fails on drift).
- Renderer: `src/preload/mcApi.ts` (the method table, used by the Electron preload AND the Tauri bridge), `src/renderer/src/bridge/transport.ts` + `mc.ts`, `vite.config.tauri.ts` (out/tauri-renderer, dev server 5174).

### Review fixes (2026-11-06)

All 14 confirmed findings of the wave 0 review are fixed; none was skipped.

| finding | fix | checked by |
|---|---|---|
| major: a state.json with a lone UTF-16 surrogate (JSON.stringify writes `\udXXX`, JSON.parse reads it) was treated as corrupt, renamed and replaced by defaults | `unimux_core::js_json::parse`: serde_json first, on failure every unpaired `\uD800-\uDFFF` escape becomes `\ufffd` (a Rust string cannot hold a lone surrogate; byte parity is kept for every other string). `read_state` uses it. The bridge makes every renderer payload well-formed (`wellFormed` in transport.ts, values and keys), because Tauri's own IPC parse would refuse the whole call | golden `test/golden/state-load.jsonl` (9 texts written by Node, lone high / low in values and keys, valid pairs, an escaped backslash) through parse and the whole load_state; store test; vitest; smoke: a fixture with half an emoji loads with no `state.json.bak-*`, a renderer edit with a lone surrogate is saved |
| major: a send refused before it was queued (not yet / refused / unknown) used a sequence number that never reached the SendQueue, so the next send waited the 2 s gap timeout (renderer-boot was 3074 ms) | `SendQueue::skip(label, session, seq)` fills the slot (pending items are `Option<T>`); `mc_send` calls it before returning the error | cargo `a_refused_send_does_not_hold_the_next_one`; smoke: reportError after two not-yet sends reaches crash.log in 35 ms; startup.log now shows renderer-boot 871-1062 ms |
| minor: `app_data_dir()` honoured %APPDATA% (Electron ignores it) | Windows: `dirs::data_dir()` (SHGetKnownFolderPath FOLDERID_RoamingAppData) only; doc fixed. Scratch runs keep using MC_DATA_DIR | code; smoke isolation check |
| minor: startup race when 1.1.x and 1.2.0 start at the same moment | under Electron's named startup mutex we look for the window again, take `<data dir>/lockfile` like Chromium (write, share read, delete on close; a held one means Electron owns the dir) and create our window before releasing it. The mutex name is NOT Chromium's `ChromeProcessSingletonStartup!`: Electron 45 uses `Local\<app name>ProcessSingletonStartup`, verified by holding candidate names while starting a scratch Electron app named unimux (only `Local\unimuxProcessSingletonStartup` made `requestSingleInstanceLock()` wait, 2.1 s) | check-electron-compat: 14 simultaneous-start offsets, always exactly one instance |
| minor: JS event listeners leaked in Rust on every reload | the transport tracks every subscription; mc.ts disposes them all on `pagehide` (not for a persisted bfcache page) | vitest (late listens too); smoke: 46 subscriptions before a reload, 0 at pagehide, events reach the reloaded page. Not measured: Tauri's internal listener table (pub(crate)) |
| minor: pty port posts could be reordered | port messages are batched per task and numbered per page load (`createPortPoster`); `mc_port_post(session, seq, kind, messages)` goes through a second SendQueue with lanes `<label>\0port:<kind>` | vitest batching/numbering; the SendQueue ordering tests |
| minor: SendQueue::forget / Ports::detach_window never called | `forget_on_destroy` (WindowEvent::Destroyed) on the main window builder (W5 adds it to detached windows) calls `AppContext::window_destroyed`: send and port lanes (forget also drops `<label>\0...` sub-lanes, so the worker task ends), port sinks, detached registration | cargo `forget_removes_the_window_and_its_sub_lanes`; smoke: test-bridge `lanes` = 1 send lane |
| minor: Uint8Array never used the raw path; nested binary results had no defined form | the transport sends a Uint8Array / ArrayBuffer argument as the raw body of `mc_invoke_raw` (`mc-cmd`, ASCII-escaped `mc-args`, `mc-raw-index` headers). Binary results: `Reply::Binary { value, parts }` and `Reply::Bytes` leave as one frame (`unimux_ipc::binary`: `MCB1`, header length, `{value, parts}` with `{"$mcBytes": i}` placeholders, the parts), decoded by `decodeBinaryReply`; ARCHITECTURE section 3 documents it | cargo binary tests; vitest (notes.saveImage headers, a nested frame); smoke: notes.saveImage reaches the router through mc_invoke_raw |
| minor: release capability granted test_bridge | `allow-test-bridge` moved to `capabilities-check/test-bridge.json`; build.rs picks `./capabilities*/*.json` only with the test-bridge feature, else `./capabilities/*.json`. `allow-mc-invoke-raw` stays: the bridge uses it now | release binary has no `test-bridge` capability string; the check build has it and the smoke uses it |
| minor: macOS reused Electron's entitlements | own `crates/unimux-app/entitlements.mac.plist`, empty dict with the reason; packages add keys when they prove the need | config (macOS not built yet) |
| minor: CI release check only grepped `--remote-debugging-port` | fails on `MC_DATA_DIR`, `MC_DEBUG_PORT`, `test_bridge` on every OS; `--remote-debugging-port` on Windows only (built in cfg(windows) only) | local grep counts above |
| minor: CI never clippy'd / tested the release-only code of unimux-app | `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --release -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace --release` replace the core/state-only release test | run locally: clean, 123 passed |
| minor: Tauri's drag-drop handler broke HTML5 drag and drop on Windows | `.disable_drag_drop_handler()` on the main window builder (OS file paths come with W3) | code; HTML5 drags are not exercised by a script yet (W3 real-app list) |
| minor: the guard's "top-level frame only" rule was never enforced | `Caller.main_frame` removed. The guard now also compares the IPC request's `Origin` header (from `tauri::ipc::Request`) with the page origin, which refuses a cross-origin frame; a same-origin iframe cannot be told apart by a Tauri command, so the docs (guard.rs, ARCHITECTURE, PLAN F2) say it rests on the CSP and on the renderer creating no iframes (it creates none) | cargo `a_request_from_another_origin_is_refused`; smoke: real requests carry `Origin: http://tauri.localhost` and are allowed, a probe with another origin is refused |

### Open issues after wave 0

1. ~~F8 not started~~ Done on `tauri-pkg-F8` (see the F8 row). What is left of F8:
   - F8a: the corpus has no anonymized real user state files (the rules forbid reading %APPDATA%\unimux); the realistic inputs are the 118 states the vitest suite hands to normalizeState, a hand-made 1.1.4 state using every domain and their damage. A user who wants to can drop anonymized files into the seeds.
   - F8b: an optional field the TS code sets to `undefined` (sshStatusBarItems, sidePanels, a cell's profileId `constructor`) is absent in Rust. On disk that is identical; in memory a later `{...settings, x}` puts the key at the end in Rust where JS keeps its old slot, so the next save can order that one key differently from 1.1.4. Not seen in the corpus (after-edit rows are byte-identical).
   - ~~F8c~~ fixed: the inbox retention sorts with a port of V8's TimSort (normalize/timsort.rs), so NaN timestamps order like 1.1.4 and never panic (golden js-sort.jsonl and the retention rows of normalize-state.jsonl).
   - F8d: `linux` is not a Platform (only win32 / darwin are shipped); 1.1.4 never ran there.
   - F8e: the corpus is 11 MB (normalize-state 7.3 MB, after-edit 3.8 MB, seeds 0.2 MB); `npm run gen:check` regenerates it in about 10 s.
2. ~~Windows SDK missing~~ installed 2026-11-06 (see install status).
3. **CI never ran** (branch not pushed). macOS was never compiled; the Unix code paths (instance socket, process groups, setsid) are untested.
4. F11: no StartupGate trait / update-gate window yet, the native title bar is kept (W1), Ctrl+R / F5 / zoom / minimize behaviour is not checked by a script, renderer crash handling is W6.
5. Quit is the plain Tauri exit plus a final state flush: no quit confirmation, wind-down registry or session-end handling (W6).
6. Cells show "cell:spawn is not available yet" and the status bar shows the update:get not-yet error: expected until P4 / U1.
7. `pathForFile` returns '' in Tauri (an HTML5 File has no path in WebView2) until W3 adds `pathsForFiles`.
8. The installer file name is Tauri's default (`unimux_1.2.0_x64-setup.exe`); U4 renames it to `unimux-Setup-X.exe`.
9. The golden corpus covers state merge/diff, translate and temp names only; normalize, routeMessage, launch lines and the update progress model come with their packages.
10. Detached windows, the gate and the update window are not created yet, so the guard's detached registration and the destroy cleanup (`forget_on_destroy`) are only unit-tested and wired on the main window.
11. HTML5 drag and drop (tab reorder / detach, snippets, notes, tree, SFTP) is enabled again by `disable_drag_drop_handler()` but no script drags yet; W3 adds the real-app drag checks with the OS file drop.
12. The macOS half of the startup race (Electron's SingletonLock / SingletonSocket) is still R3; the Windows half (startup mutex + lockfile) is done.

### Open issues after SP1 (2026-11-06)

0. **User decision needed: the SP1 acceptance substitute.** As written ("Channel p50 < 2 ms at 20 x 1 MB/s", into 20 xterm) it is not met by any transport. Proposed: transport-only p50 + clock bound < 2 ms with the WebView2 shared buffer (met: 0.31 +-0.81 ms), the into-xterm criterion moves to SP5, the WKWebView item to W2. Until the user agrees SP1 stays "done (acceptance substituted)", not verified.
1. **WKWebView IPC not checked (open blocker for macOS)**: the SP1 scope item "WKWebView in CI uses the same IPC" was not run (no Mac, CI never ran, no CDP on macOS; needs the W2 eval bridge). Tauri 2.12.5 uses `ipc://localhost` there by default, which the CSP allows.
2. **macOS data plane not measured**: WKWebView has no shared buffer; the plan is the pull transport (raw invoke long poll, 2.8 ms p50 transport-only on WebView2, best into xterm) until SP5 / W2 measure it on a Mac.
3. **Renderer limit**: 20 xterm parse 20 x 1 MB/s with no IPC at 109 fps / 22.8 ms p50 on a moderately loaded dev box, but only 20 fps at 94-100 percent machine CPU; the FlowGate (P2) must hold producers back, and SP5's 60 fps target has to be compared with Electron 1.1.4 on the same machine and load.
4. **No renderer backpressure in the shared-buffer sender**: "one post in flight" only waits for the WebView2 UI thread, not the page; into a saturated renderer the posts queue with about one frame each (18,369 posts for 19,200 frames, 39 ms arrive p50, 36 fps, worse than pull's 26.6 ms / 133 fps). P2 adds a page-side release ack capping the buffers in flight per window plus the FlowGate byte acks; its acceptance now checks that the posts in flight stay bounded. SP5 compares the gated sender with pull into xterm.
5. The shared-buffer sender exists only as the check-build bench (`channel_bench.rs`); P2 builds the production one (per-window sender, renderer gate as in 4, fallback to Channel when `ICoreWebView2_17` is missing, bridge `sharedbufferreceived` -> fake port, a UI thread slower than 2 s waited for and counted, never answered with a second post).
5a. Harness: in offscreen runs tao's `minimize()` / `hide()` act on tao's VISIBLE flag, which the raw `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` show leaves unset (`minimize()` then hides the window, `hide()` does nothing). Real-app checks that need a user-like window state use the test_bridge `window` actions `sysMinimize` / `sysRestore` / `sysHide` and gate on the read-back state.
6. `unimux-exec` `output_over_the_cap_is_truncated_and_the_tree_ends` failed once (20 s timeout) in a full workspace run at 100 percent machine CPU and passed on rerun; not touched by SP1, looks load-sensitive.

### SP2 open issues

1. **Mac half not run** (no Mac): the Keychain round trip with 1.1.4 on macOS, the one "Always Allow" prompt for the re-signed binary, and the legacy-account migration. The macOS code is compiled and clippy clean for aarch64-apple-darwin and its crypto matches Node vectors byte for byte, nothing more. The spike script is Windows-only and needs a small launcher port for the Mac run.
2. Resolved (review 2026-11-06): the earlier expectation ("Electron stays unavailable, no new key") was wrong. Chromium's OSCryptImpl::Init generates and stores a new key on kKeyDoesNotExist and kDecryptionFailed, and real 1.1.4 does so on a DPAPI blob nobody can unwrap (spike check F) and on a non-JSON file (moved to `Local State.bad`, check H). Rust now does the same (`Origin::Replaced`, checks G/H; 1.1.4 keeps the key Rust stored). Not measured with a real second Windows account: the foreign key is simulated with a DPAPI blob that does not unwrap (CryptUnprotectData 0x8007000D), which is the same code path.
3. B2: when to touch the Keychain first on macOS (`Cipher::open` is eager today), the callers and IPC, `MC_SSH_NO_SAFESTORAGE` through DevOverrides, `Origin` in startup.log.

### SP5 open issues

1. Echo under a 19-cell flood is slower than 1.1.4 in the median (13 to 28 ms vs 9 to 13 ms): the renderer main thread works through xterm's write queues of the other terminals (1 MiB unacked each). P2: smaller unacked window for unfocused terminals or pausing them while the focused one is typed into; re-measure with `scripts/sp5/run.mjs latency --load=19` vs `scripts/sp5/electron-grid.mjs 20 1000000 5 --latency=60`.
2. "20 cells at 1 MB/s keep 60 fps" is not met when ConPTY passes 13 to 20 MB/s to xterm (the renderer saturates near 20 MB/s of parsing, in 1.1.4 as well); what ConPTY passes varies from 1 to 24 MB/s between runs (it drops lines that scroll past between its frames).
3. OpenConsole 1.23 holds a new terminal's first output until DA1 is answered (3 s without a renderer): P2 answers it host-side.
4. conpty.dll / OpenConsole.exe come from node-pty's third_party copy (node_modules) for tests and the check run (`UNIMUX_CONPTY_DIR`); P1 commits them under src-tauri/resources/conpty/x64 and wires the resource dir.
5. One unreproduced flake in about 15 runs of the conpty suite: `job_object_kills_the_whole_tree` saw no exit 5 s after kill once under heavy machine load (the assertion now prints the process exit and close state).
6. Renders compared by screenshot and scripted checks, not pixel-diffed; no pwsh on this machine (Windows PowerShell used, as 1.1.4's resolveShell would).
7. Unix pty and the macOS run are P1.

How to run SP5: `npm run build:check`, `cd src-tauri && node ../scripts/msvc-run.mjs cargo build --release -p unimux-pty --bins`, then `node scripts/sp5/run.mjs [render] [latency] [flood] [close]` (options in the file header); 1.1.4 references after `npm run build`: `node scripts/sp5/electron-ref.mjs`, `node scripts/sp5/electron-grid.mjs`, `node scripts/perf-latency.mjs 60 --json`; cargo: `node ../scripts/msvc-run.mjs cargo test -p unimux-pty`.

### SP6 open issues (2026-11-06)

Review fixes 2026-11-06 (branch rebased on tauri-1.2.0 at 6868341; details in spikes/SP6.md "Review fixes"): never always-on-top, shown inactive with a taskbar flash on every show (first show and error card), raw SW_HIDE (tao's hide() was a no-op after the raw show); installer through ShellExecuteExW (owner HWND, `runas` when the install dir is not writable, AllowSetForegroundWindow(ASFW_ANY) first); supervision starts with the process, independent of WebView2 (headless fallback, 3 s exit watchdog); supervision goes on behind the timeout card and still relaunches (1.1.4 `--force-run` outcome), closing that card only hides; own failed-install text; `runtime-<ver>.partial-<pid>` staging; per-hand-over `webview2-<id>` and a lock-guarded runtime cleanup.

Verified on Windows after the fixes: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy -p unimux-exec -p unimux-update -p unimux-app --all-targets -- -D warnings` with and without `dev-overrides,test-bridge`; `cargo test` of those crates (exec 8, update 12, app 6); `npm run typecheck`; `npm run gen:check`; `npm test` (3951 passed); harness 88/88 in three consecutive full runs, `--visible --case=main,guard` 51/51, `--prestage --case=main` 32/32.

1. macOS half of SP6 not done (no Mac): bundle swap from a temp-dir copy, `codesign --verify`; the window mode was never compiled on macOS (CI not run). Goes with U5 / R3.
2. The UAC path is tested for the decision only (icacls deny ACL); a real per-machine install with the consent prompt in front, and a real full-screen app staying in front, need the VM (U2/U4). The harness proves: never foreground, never WS_EX_TOPMOST, no activation.
3. The positive path with a different target version needs a second payload build (1.2.0 -> 1.2.0-test.1, U2); the guard case covers "an old build started mid-install writes no ready".
4. `work/shown` later than the 4 s SHOWN_WAIT still happens on the loaded machine (2 of 7 full runs); the update no longer depends on it.
5. A WebView2 whose environment fails keeps its own processes for minutes (WebView2 behaviour, no update file held); stale `webview2-<id>` folders of a killed window are not swept.
6. Session end (logout / shutdown) does not yet let the window close at once (W6).
7. `UpdateProgressModel` port has unit tests only; TS goldens are U2.
8. `signal_update_ready` is the minimal form (no heartbeat wait, no clearGateFailureIfUpdated): U3.
9. The real productName `unimux` installer was not run on this machine on purpose (second Apps & features entry, the user's real app runs); U2/U4 in the VM.
10. Run it: `node scripts/spike-update-window.mjs --build` (builds the spike installer into `src-tauri/target/sp6`, then runs the cases main, guard, failed, headless, each installing into its own scratch dir and uninstalling); later runs without `--build`; `--case=main,guard` picks cases; the headless case (broken WebView2 folder, shows an Edge dialog on the desktop) runs only with `UNIMUX_SPIKE_BROKEN_WEBVIEW=1`; `--visible` keeps the main case's window on screen (MC_UPDATE_VISIBLE=1, never takes the focus); `--prestage` stages and warms the copy first; `--keep` keeps the scratch dir. Screenshots and timings in artifacts/tauri-sp6/.
11. Observation for W6: after `quit` the main app's WebView2 processes took 3-30 s to end on the loaded machine.

### Review fixes: SP7 (2026-11-06)

All 8 confirmed findings of the SP7 review are fixed (the two timing findings were the same defect); none was skipped.

| finding | fix | checked by |
|---|---|---|
| major: `legacy_pem` refused DEK-Info AES-128/192/256-CTR and BF-CBC, which 1.1.4 (ssh2 under Electron's BoringSSL) loads | CTR (ctr 0.10.1, Ctr128BE) and BF-CBC (blowfish 0.10.0) added with ssh2's key derivation and IV lengths; the cipher list is the ssh2 CIPHER_INFO x Electron getCiphers() intersection that decrypts (GCM / ECB / RC4 have no working path in ssh2); golden-keys.mjs now runs under Electron (Node 26's OpenSSL cannot decrypt bf-cbc) | 7 new fixtures (RSA AES-128/192/256-CTR, BF-CBC; EC AES-128/256-CTR, BF-CBC) load identically; 8 refused-cipher fixtures (camellia, CFB, OFB, DES-CBC, DES-EDE-CBC, hand-made GCM / ECB / DES-EDE3) answer like 1.1.4; SP7.md and PLAN S2 corrected |
| major: the key test compared one input per file; an unencrypted SEC1 EC key given a passphrase loads in 1.1.4 but not through russh | keys.json holds the 1.1.4 answer for no / the right / a wrong passphrase; `port_parse` (the S2 prototype of parsePrivateKey) must match all of them (ok-ness, error code, `encrypted`, metadata); a passphrase reaches the key reader only when `isEncrypted` says so (traditional PEM always goes through `legacy_pem`, which ignores it without DEK-Info) | 348/348 answers equal; PLAN S2 lists the rule |
| minor: unencrypted PPK v2 + passphrase is notAKey in 1.1.4 but loads in russh | refused before russh in `s2_load` (ssh2's MAC key includes the passphrase); SP7.md names all three callers that pass a user passphrase | ppk2-rsa*.ppk with the right and a wrong passphrase -> notAKey; a mutation that drops the check fails the test |
| minor: `clean_comment` cut at 200 scalar values and used Rust `trim()` | 200 UTF-16 units, a split pair's lone high surrogate becomes U+FFFD (as `js_json::parse` reads 1.1.4's JSON), the JS whitespace set (U+FEFF yes, U+0085 no); keys.json is read with `js_json::parse` | 4 comment fixtures from 1.1.4's generateKey (150 emoji, `x` + 150 emoji, BOM / NEL / U+3000, control + U+2028/2029) and a unit test |
| minor: a non-ASCII DEK-Info IV panicked (`&str` byte slicing), the IV length was not checked | IV parsed as bytes like `Buffer.from(hex)` (stops at the first non-hex pair), checked against the cipher's IV length (16 AES, 8 DES-EDE3 / Blowfish) before the passphrase; a header without a comma is skipped like ssh2 does | 7 fixtures (IV short, bad hex, `é`, no IV, trailing junk, odd length, lower-case name) match 1.1.4; `malformed_dek_info_headers_never_panic` (179 hand-made values, no / wrong passphrase) |
| minor: the matrix compared only the ok boolean and the russh probe ran a fixed step list | the probe chooses methods like SshLogin.nextAuth from the server's lists (`AuthOrder`) and maps errors to SshErrorCode (`error_code`, the S3 table); the matrix compares outcome + code, the methods sent and the algorithms | full rerun: 104/104 logins same outcome, code, method sequence and algorithms (Dropbear: `password, false` on both sides); 21/21 host keys |
| minor (twice): russh timing included the disconnect | probe records `ms.authenticated` right after the auth success; the matrix uses it against ssh2's 'ready' | rerun: p50 81 ms (ssh2) vs 80 ms (russh); SP7.md explains the earlier 87 vs 100 |

### Open issues: SP7

1. russh pins ssh-key `=0.7.0-rc.11` (a release candidate); a russh update moves it, rerun `cargo test -p unimux-ssh --test sp7_keys` and the matrix.
2. S2 ports `port_parse` / `s2_load` / `legacy_pem` / `clean_comment` from tests/sp7_keys.rs (all refusals are prototyped there now) and keeps the keys.json comparison; ssh2's anchoring of the PEM regex to the whole text is not prototyped yet. RC4 DEK-Info (ssh2 throws) answers badPassphrase by decision.
3. The matrix compared the 1.1.4 modules (SshLogin, KnownHostsStore) under this tree's Electron 45 runtime, not an installed 1.1.4 app; macOS not run.
4. OpenSSH 9.8+ `PerSourcePenalties` blocks repeated failing test logins; the SP7 configs turn it off and S4's CI matrix must too. CentOS 6 (OpenSSH 5.3) does not run on WSL2 (vsyscall), so the oldest real build is OpenSSH 5.9.
5. The unimux-ssh dev-dependencies (russh, ssh-key, aes, cbc, ctr, des, blowfish, md5) become normal dependencies in S2/S3; the prototype `legacy_pem` moves from tests/sp7_keys.rs into the crate.

### Open issues after SP8 (2026-11-06)

Verification: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy -p unimux-mcp --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p unimux-mcp` (10 sp8_wire tests + 2 unit tests), `node scripts/spike-mcp-wire.mjs` (ts / custom / rmcp: 52 protocol cases + 8 CLI scenarios each; ts and custom all PASS, custom 52 / 52 identical, rmcp gaps reported). Run: see spikes/SP8.md "How to run".

1. Codex `accept` of an elicitation is not exercised: `codex exec` always answers `decline`; the accept path needs the Codex TUI in a pty (M4 real-app checks). Accept is proven with Claude (Elicitation hook).
2. Long waits through the Rust server (Claude 600 s, Codex 170 s, `MCP_CLI_WAIT`) not run; the 15 s SSE keepalive is in the prototype (M4, verify-mcp-cli).
3. The 10 s header / 30 s body timeouts, the 120 / 60 per s bucket and the 256-session cap are in the prototype but no test drives them (M1 ports the mcp-protocol cases).
4. No CLI declares or uses MCP tasks; the 2025-11-25 task wire is proven only at HTTP level against the TS server.
5. macOS not run (hyper's drop-on-disconnect behaviour to be confirmed in CI / R3).
6. The spike's `cap_result` uses `String::from_utf16_lossy`; 1.1.4 can emit a lone surrogate escape when the cut splits a pair (M2, with the F7 lone-surrogate handling).
7. rmcp 3.9 stays a dev-dependency of the spike only (examples, tests); M1 removes it together with `examples/sp8/rmcp_impl.rs` when it moves the dispatcher into the crate.
8. Deliberate deviation: after a client's `notifications/cancelled` for a session call, 1.1.4 sends no answer and keeps that call's SSE stream open until the client closes it (the in-flight slot stays taken meanwhile); the prototype also sends no answer but ends the stream once nothing else is pending on it. Same frames on the wire (protocol case "session tools/call cancelled by notifications/cancelled gets no answer").
9. The elicitation timeout (120 s) and the ttl expiry (clamp 60 s) are driven only by cargo tests with a shortened `Config` (`elicit_timeout`, `min_task_ttl`); the TS server has the constants built in, so these two are not compared case by case with 1.1.4 (the abort path of the elicitation is).

### Open issues after SP9 (DB wire fidelity)

1. TLS was not part of the spike for any of the three clients (rustls over the same generic streams): D4 / D7 / D9 cover it in their Docker matrices. MySQL 9 and Valkey are not in the SP9 matrix yet.
2. D4 / D7 / D12 must send DB port messages through a JSON writer that can emit a lone `\udXXX`: a 64K cell cut inside a surrogate pair, and every `DB_LIMITS.serverText` (4,000) cut too (PG error fields and notices, MySQL errors and warnings, Redis error replies). `unimux_db::wire::value` (`DbValue::write_json`, `server_text` + `write_head`) is the template, serde_json alone cannot.
3. D7 decisions: MySQL connection attributes (`_client_name` is `Node-MySQL-2` in 1.1.4, `unimux` in the spike, visible only in performance_schema), keeping the `LOCAL_FILES` client flag that mysql2 advertises (1.1.4 and the spike answer LOAD DATA LOCAL with an empty file and fail the statement with mysql2's streamFactory message, reported as `dbClosed`), and whether to add MariaDB client_ed25519 (1.1.4 / mysql2 cannot log in with it; PLAN D7 lists it).
4. D4 note: node-postgres takes `PGOPTIONS` (and other PG* variables) from the environment; the Rust client does not. Recommended to keep it that way and mention it in the 1.2.0 notes.
5. The server checks need Docker: they are `#[ignore]`d in a plain `cargo test` (shown as ignored, not passed); `node scripts/spike-db-wire.mjs` runs them with `--include-ignored` and fails a server test that has no server of its kind. CI does not start the containers yet.
6. The redis crate stays a dev-dependency of unimux-db only to keep the measured gaps reproducible; D9 can drop it (and the workspace pin) once its own tests replace that comparison.
7. D7: after `SET NAMES` / `SET CHARACTER SET` mysql2 encodes queries and decodes column names in the session charset; `wire::mycharset` does it for utf8, latin1 / binary, ascii, ucs2 / utf16-le and cesu8 and refuses queries in the iconv-lite-only encodings (cp1250, koi8r, sjis, big5 ...). D7 adds iconv-lite's tables for those.
8. D9: the reconnect handshake (`auth`, `client setname unimux`, `select` of the previously selected DB) is taken from the ioredis source (`wire::redis::reconnect_sequence`); D9 adds a golden for it through RedisSession.

### How to run

- Dev: `npm run tauri dev` (Vite on 5174 + a debug build; MC_* overrides are on in debug builds). Not exercised in wave 0.
- Check flavour (what the scripts drive): `npm run build:check`, then `npm run smoke:tauri` (`--keep` keeps the scratch dir) and `node scripts/check-electron-compat.mjs` (after `npm run build`).
- Manual run on scratch data: `MC_DATA_DIR=<scratch> MC_DEBUG_PORT=9333 src-tauri/target/check/release/unimux.exe` (add `MC_OFFSCREEN=1` for a window that never takes focus); DevTools at http://127.0.0.1:9333.
- W1 caption check: `npm run build:check`, `(cd src-tauri && node ../scripts/msvc-run.mjs cargo build -p unimux-platform --example wincap --release)`, then `npm run verify:caption` (`--scale=100,125`, `--keep`; images in `artifacts/caption/`). New references (only when 1.1.4's look is to be re-taken): `npm run build`, then `node scripts/capture-caption-reference.mjs`.
- Release flavour: `npm run build:release` (unsigned NSIS installer; the MC_* overrides are compiled out, so it always uses %APPDATA%\unimux: do not run it for tests).
- W3 check: `npm run build && npm run build:check && node scripts/verify-tauri-w3.mjs` (`--write-fixture` refreshes test/fixtures/electron-local-storage).

- W5 real-app check: `npm run build:check`, then `node scripts/verify-tauri-detached.mjs` (main window offscreen, detached windows hidden).

- cargo directly: `cd src-tauri && node ../scripts/msvc-run.mjs cargo test --workspace` (loads the MSVC environment and the SDK fallback).
- Files panel (G5): `npm run build:check`, then `node scripts/verify-tauri-files.mjs` (`--keep` keeps the scratch dir; screenshots in artifacts/tauri-files/).
- Monitor panel (G8): `npm run build:check`, then `node scripts/verify-tauri-monitor.mjs` (`--keep`; screenshots in artifacts/tauri-monitor/).

3. **Two acceptance items wait for the user**: the SP1 substitute (transport-only shared buffer instead of "Channel p50 < 2 ms into 20 xterm") and SP5 criterion 4 (20 cells x 1 MB/s at 60 fps only partly met; 1.1.4 drops more frames under the same load).
4. Echo under a 19-cell flood is slower than 1.1.4 in the median (SP5 issue 1): input for P2.
5. conpty.dll / OpenConsole.exe still come from node-pty's copy in node_modules (`UNIMUX_CONPTY_DIR`); P1 commits them under src-tauri/resources/conpty/x64.
6. Spike-only dependencies stay as dev-dependencies until their packages: rmcp (M1 removes), redis crate (D9 drops), russh / ssh-key / aes / cbc / des / md5 / ctr / blowfish in unimux-ssh (S2 / S3 make them normal ones).
7. Load-sensitive tests seen by the packages (not at this integration): `unimux-exec output_over_the_cap_is_truncated_and_the_tree_ends`, `unimux-pty job_object_kills_the_whole_tree`, a few vitest timing cases. All passed in the runs above.
8. Branches and worktrees: all 16 package / review worktrees under `.claude/worktrees` were clean or held only a stale pre-fix index (the review fixes were committed from detached worktrees), had no junctions and were removed; `git worktree prune` run. Deleted: tauri-pkg-F8, -F8-fixes, -SP1, -SP2, -SP2-fixes, -SP5, -SP6-fixes, -SP7, -SP8, -SP9 and the worktree-wf_b57fdc79-7b0-* branches (`git branch -d`); `tauri-pkg-SP6` with `-D` because its commit 1d26bd7 is in `tauri-1.2.0` only as the rebased 599fb11 (`git cherry` shows it as applied). This also removed the 1.3 GB `src-tauri/target/sp6` of the SP6 worktree.

## Wave 1 packages: open issues and review fixes

The sections each package wrote on its branch, in package order (moved here at the wave 1 integration; text unchanged).

### P1 open issues (2026-11-06)

1. **macOS not run** (no Mac, CI never ran): the Unix backend passes the full suite on Linux in Docker and compiles + clippy-cleans for aarch64-apple-darwin (`proc_listallpids` session scan, zsh `-il`); the "same suite on macos-latest" item waits for the first CI run of tauri.yml.
2. **Load-sensitive SP5 cases**: with the machine at 100 percent CPU (other agents' builds) `conpty::output_after_exit_is_delivered_before_the_exit` (3000 cmd echoes; the same loop took 70 s into a plain pipe outside any pty at that load) timed out at 30 s and again at the raised 120 s, and `job_object_kills_the_whole_tree` once missed its 5 s exit limit when run in parallel with it; both pass alone and passed in the first full run. The manager suite uses the flood helper instead of cmd loops. Rerun `cargo test -p unimux-pty` on an idle machine before the P1 verification.
3. Windows 50 MB flood throughput depends on the load: 107.8 s in the first run, 27.8 s in the review-fix run, and the reviewer's run at 88-100 percent CPU moved only 8 MB in 183 s (45-250 KB/s), so the old fixed 180 s wait failed there. Since the review fixes the test waits on progress (it fails only when no output arrives for 60 s) and `UNIMUX_P1_FLOOD_MB` sets the size; at that load a full 50 MB run can still take 4-18 minutes. Throughput itself is a P2 / SP5 target; P1 checks pause, resume and completeness.
4. The DA1 answer on kill is an addition to 1.1.4, where such a kill waited out OpenConsole's 3 s DA1 hold. Review fix: it is sent when no DA1 answer (`CSI ? ... c`) was ever written, not when no input at all was written, so an agent cell whose launch command was typed but whose DA1 nobody answered is killed in about 10 ms instead of 2.6-2.8 s (`an_agent_cell_without_a_da1_answer_is_killed_in_time`, fails at 2623 ms with the old check). P2's host-side DA1 answer for terminals without a renderer will make it rare.
5. The pty service (`pty:*` commands, sizes, P2 output engine between the manager and the renderer) is P2 / P3; P1 only sets the conpty resource dir in `unimux-app` main.rs. The SP5 wave 0b items "conpty files from node_modules" and "Unix pty is P1" are resolved by this package.
6. **Difference to 1.1.4 that needs the user's OK: the Job Object kill.** A single-terminal kill (cell closed or respawned) ends everything in the terminal's job, also GUI programs and new console windows started from the cell (`code .` when VS Code was not running, `start "" cmd`, a browser first started by `vite --open`). 1.1.4's kill (node-pty: ClosePseudoConsole + TerminateProcess of the shell) ended only programs attached to the console, and `taskkill /T` ran only in killAll for terminals still running after 3 s; SP5 measured that a `start /b` dev server survived such a kill. The job is what makes the PLAN item "dev-server child tree killed" pass, and there is no reliable way to tell a GUI program from a console one in the job (both are children of the shell; `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_SILENT_BREAKAWAY_OK` would take every child out of the job). Kept as is and recorded in the PLAN P1 row; if the user wants 1.1.4's behaviour, the per-terminal kill drops `TerminateJobObject` (dev servers then outlive their cell again) and kill_all keeps it.
7. Review fixes (2026-11-07): Unix ptys get node-pty's termios (IUTF8, VERASE 0x7f, the c_cc table, B38400; `unix::tests::the_terminal_gets_node_ptys_termios` reads it back); on Unix the chdir and execvp run in the child like node-pty's, so a missing cwd or program is a terminal that exits with code 1 (Linux prints "chdir(2) failed.: ..." / "execvp(3) failed.: ...") instead of a spawn error (Windows still fails the spawn, as node-pty's CreateProcess did); the launch command goes through the slot's early input, so it is typed even when spawn has not put the pty into the slot 150 ms after the first output (`the_launch_command_is_typed_when_spawn_returns_late`, fails with the old code).

### B1 open issues

1. The acceptance "ptyhost.int runs against hostd in CI on both OSes" is wired (tauri.yml step `.int suites on the Rust host`) but not run: nothing is pushed. macOS never ran hostd (no Mac): the Unix backend is compile-checked for aarch64-apple-darwin (check + clippy -D warnings) and ran on Linux in Docker (cargo test 17/17, the vitest rust host 3/3 with bash and zsh). macOS differences to watch in the first CI run: `/bin/zsh -il` startup time, `TIOCSCTTY` / `openpty` from libc on Darwin.
2. The `pty` domain is a stand-in until P1 / P2: no shell integration (`integratedLaunch` prompt marks), no OutputChannel coalescing / FlowGate / acks, no echo fast path, no `setSink` / `setFlowControl`; ptyhost.int does not check them. P1 / P2 point `pty/mod.rs` at their manager so the same suite checks the production code.
3. OpenConsole 1.23 sends `ESC [ c` and holds the output up to about 3 s until it is answered; hostd answers like xterm.js once per terminal. P2 must do the same for a terminal with no renderer attached (restored sessions, detached windows not open yet), or a cell's first output is late by 3 s.
4. Flaky under heavy machine load, on both hosts (the 1.1.4 node host too): the pwsh case of ptyhost.int waits 15 s for `mc-2-ok`, and with other agents building in parallel PSReadLine re-rendered the typed line one character at a time and had not reached Enter after 15 s (captured output). 5 of 14 two-host runs had that case fail on at least one host (on the node host and on the rust host); 9 rust-only runs passed. The suite and its timeout are 1.1.4's and were left unchanged.
5. Only ptyhost.int has a rust host. ssh.int / ssh-remote-list.int / ssh-bar.int (P5), sftp-host.int (T2), db-tunnel.int (D2), db-fake.int (D4), db-mysql-fake.int (D7), db-redis.int (D9) add their hostd domain with their package (HOSTD.md "Adding a domain") and append the suite to `HOSTD_SUITES` in scripts/test-hostd.mjs.
6. Windows: both hosts use node-pty's conpty.dll 1.23 from node_modules (`UNIMUX_CONPTY_DIR`, set by the helper and by tests/stdio.rs); without node_modules hostd falls back to the in-box ConPTY. P1 decides where the shipped app takes the dll from.

### Review fixes: B1 (2026-11-07)

| finding | fix | checked by |
|---|---|---|
| major (reported twice): Unix exit held back until every holder of the slave side closed it (`sleep 600 & exit`, a dev server): no `pty.exit`, `has` true, kill waited its whole timeout; node-pty 1.1.0 reports it at most 200 ms after the shell ended | `pty/unix.rs`: once the waiter has the exit status the reader gets EXIT_DRAIN = 200 ms (DESTROY_SOCKET_TIMEOUT_MS) to reach EOF; after that a wake pipe ends its `select()` (select, not poll: macOS poll does not work on ttys), the master fd is closed and the exit reported (the entry and its `running` record go, so no tree kill reaches a reaped pid) | Linux (Docker rust:1.99-slim, `--rm`): stdio.rs `exit_is_reported_while_a_background_job_holds_the_terminal` (`sleep 30 & exit`, exit < 5 s, `has` false, kill answers at once); ptyhost.int case of the same (both hosts, Unix only) |
| minor: no PWD on Unix (node-pty sets `env.PWD = cwd`): a symlinked cwd showed the physical path | `unix::spawn` sets PWD to the cwd (or the hostd's cwd) after the request env | Linux stdio.rs `pwd_is_the_cwd_as_given` (cwd through a symlink, `$(pwd)` prints the link); ptyhost.int case (both hosts, Unix only) |
| minor: `pty.histories` had no `sizes`; resizes were not recorded | `Tail` ports TailBuffer.size / pruneSizes / replay (absolute UTF-16 positions, MAX_TAIL_SIZES 512, start size at spawn, every resize); answer `{id, data, sizes}`; helper type `TerminalHistory` with `sizes` | unit `tail_sizes_match_the_ts_tail_buffer` (expected values printed by flow.ts TailBuffer for the same calls), `tail_keeps_at_most_max_sizes`; stdio.rs sizes before / after a resize; ptyhost.int replay case checks `sizes` on both hosts |
| minor: Windows `kill_tree` called `Pty::kill` again, which does nothing once a kill is pending (the terminate waits for ClosePseudoConsole) | new `unimux_pty::conpty::Pty::kill_tree`: TerminateProcess + TerminateJobObject at once, then the close; hostd's WinTerm uses it | unimux-pty conpty.rs `kill_tree_ends_the_tree_at_once_and_after_a_kill` (alone and after `kill()`, `start /b ping` grandchild dead) |
| minor: Tail::trim dropped the whole character at a cut inside a surrogate pair (limit - 1 units), against its comment | the character is kept (limit + 1 units; JS keeps a lone low surrogate a String cannot hold); size offsets stay on the same characters | unit `tail_cut_inside_a_surrogate_pair_keeps_the_whole_character` |
| minor: a request with a missing / non-string op or with JSON serde rejects (a lone surrogate escape from JSON.stringify) was answered with `id: null`, so its promise never settled | `protocol::parse` keeps the id for a missing op; JSON is read with `unimux_core::js_json::parse` (lone surrogate escape = U+FFFD, like Node's UTF-8 write of that string); for a line that is not JSON the id is read from its `{"id":N,` start. HostdClient fails every pending request on an `id: null` error, so nothing hangs | unit `a_bad_request_is_answered_under_its_own_id`, `a_lone_surrogate_from_json_stringify_reads_as_a_replacement_character`; stdio.rs `protocol_answers` (ids 1005-1008); nodeHost.test.ts null-id case |
| minor: HostdClient wrote to stdin after close() ended it (uncaught ERR_STREAM_WRITE_AFTER_END) | `closing` flag set by close() (requests then reject with "the host is closing"); `stdin.on('error')` fails the pending requests | nodeHost.test.ts: request during close rejects, no uncaught exception |
| (found while re-verifying) clippy `unnecessary_mut_passed` on Linux for `openpty(..., &mut ws)` (Linux libc takes `*const winsize`, macOS `*mut`) | `&raw mut ws` | clippy -D warnings on Linux (Docker) and aarch64-apple-darwin |

Re-verified 2026-11-07: cargo fmt; clippy -D warnings for unimux-hostd + unimux-pty (Windows, all targets), unimux-hostd for aarch64-apple-darwin and on Linux; cargo test -p unimux-hostd 16 unit + 6 stdio (Windows, real ConPTY) and 16 + 8 on Linux (Docker rust:1.99-slim, removed with `--rm`); unimux-pty conpty kill tests 4/4; `UNIMUX_HOST_TARGETS=node,rust npm run test:hostd` 6 passed + 4 Unix-only skipped on Windows; test/nodeHost.test.ts 8/8; npm run typecheck; npm test 3,960 passed, 12 skipped. The new Unix cases of ptyhost.int were not run under vitest on Linux or macOS (the cargo stdio cases cover the same behavior on Linux); macOS still has never run hostd (open issue 1).

- W5b: macOS not compiled as a whole (no Mac; unimux-app needs a C compiler for objc2 on a cross check). unimux-platform and unimux-ipc pass `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin`; the `cfg(target_os = "macos")` host code (points instead of physical pixels, see the review fixes) is checked by reading only. The rest of the host is platform-neutral Tauri API except the WebView2 browser args / data dir under `cfg(windows)`. Traffic-light position (12,11) of detached windows belongs to W1 / W2 with the caption work.

### B2 open issues

1. **macOS not run** (no Mac, CI never run): the lazy Keychain open, the `safe-storage-*` mark on first use and the round trip with 1.1.4 on macOS are compile-checked only (`cargo clippy -p unimux-oscrypt --target aarch64-apple-darwin`). The acceptance item "cargo fixture round trip on macOS CI" stays open until CI runs; `verify-safestorage.mjs` is Windows-only.
2. The callers themselves are S1 (SSH vault), D1 (DB vault) and A6 (GitHub token): they take `Arc<dyn SafeCipher>` from `AppContext::safe_storage` and must call it off the main thread (the first macOS call may wait for the Keychain prompt). There is no renderer IPC for safeStorage in 1.1.4 (only the stores' `vaultInfo` storage labels, which come with S1 / D1); the `safeStorage` op exists only in the check build's test bridge.
3. `startup.log` gets a new `safe-storage-<origin>` mark that 1.1.4 did not write (PLAN B2 asks for the Origin there); on macOS it appears when a store first asks, so it can be far later than `renderer-boot`.
4. 1.1.4's page sometimes stops answering CDP (`Runtime.enable`) when started with `--inspect` (about one start in three on this machine, more under load), and the main inspector can attach before `process.mainModule` exists; `verify-safestorage.mjs` ends that start (only its own process tree, waits for it to exit) and tries again up to 5 times.
5. Resolves SP2 open issue 3.
6. `MC_SAFE_STORAGE_NAME` is a 17th dev override that 1.1.x did not have (OVERRIDE_NAMES, ARCHITECTURE section 11); PLAN F5 and INVENTORY still list the 16 switches of 1.1.x. A Mac harness run creates one `unimux-check Safe Storage` Keychain item (reused by later runs, not removed).
7. The status stays "in progress" until the macOS CI job has run the oscrypt tests (`cargo test --workspace` on macos-latest); then B2 is done.

### Open issues: B3 (http_fetch, 2026-11-06)

Verified on Windows 11 26200: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -p unimux-net --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test -p unimux-net` (11 unit + 29 integration tests, all green; `UNIMUX_NET_ONLINE=1` adds a real GET to api.github.com over h2 and HTTP/1.1 with the system proxy and the Windows certificate store: 200), `cargo check --workspace`. macOS: `cargo clippy -p unimux-net --target aarch64-apple-darwin --all-targets -D warnings` is clean (run in a throwaway rust:1.99-slim container with clang and stub libc headers for ring's C code; nothing run on a Mac).
Acceptance: the mock server asserts the exact token renewal header set and order on the wire (content-type, user-agent `unimux/<ver>`, content-length, connection: close, host, like Node's http.request in 1.1.4), redirect refusal, the 1 MB cap (with and without Content-Length), timeout, abort, connect-failure classification and direct-then-proxy fallback; the token-transport cases and the request cases of usage-fetchers are ported; update feed / download redirect rules (5 hops, trusted hosts per hop) and the GitHub / changelog header sets are covered; a PAC script served locally is evaluated by WinHTTP (`WinHttpGetProxyForUrlEx`) and the request goes through the proxy it names.
1. **Not met: "a PAC-configured VM fetches a URL through the proxy".** No VM image (SP3 deferred). The local substitute evaluates a real PAC file through WinHTTP and fetches through the proxy, but it passes the PAC URL directly instead of reading it from Internet Options (the user's settings are read only, never changed by a test), and WPAD discovery (DHCP / DNS) is not exercised. To do with the SP3 / U3 VM: set "Use setup script" and "Automatically detect settings" in the VM, run `cargo test -p unimux-net` plus `UNIMUX_NET_ONLINE=1`.
2. Header name case: hyper writes header names in lower case; 1.1.4's Node transport sent the caller's names as given by `Headers` (lower case too) but `Host` capitalised. Order matches; only `host` vs `Host` differs (hyper offers no public way to set the case of one header).
3. WinHTTP drops `SOCKS5` entries of a PAC answer (Chromium would use them); `SOCKS` (v4) entries are kept. Internet Options' manual `socks=` entries are used as SOCKS v4 like Chromium.
4. Proxies that need authentication (407) are reported as connect failures; 1.1.4 (Chromium) could answer NTLM / Negotiate challenges with the logon session. Not implemented; affects only users behind an authenticating proxy.
5. The macOS proxy path (`CFNetworkCopyProxiesForURL`, PAC URL and inline PAC script on a private run loop mode) is compile-checked only.
6. Consumers (A3, A5, A6, G8, U1) build their requests from `unimux_net::calls` (header sets, timeouts, redirect rules and URLs from 1.1.4 in one place); B3 registers no Tauri command of its own.

### Review fixes: B3 (2026-11-07)

All six confirmed findings fixed (1 major, 5 minor; two pairs were duplicates of each other).
1. **Major, WinHTTP callback use-after-free** (proxy/windows.rs): `on_proxy_result` held the parking_lot guard (an `if let` scrutinee temporary) until after `tx.send`, so `auto_ex` could wake, free `Completion` and the callback then unlocked freed memory. The callback now takes the sender out in its own block (lock released) and only then sends; it touches nothing of the context after the send, and `auto_ex` still frees the context only after `recv` returned (leaks it after a timeout, as before).
2. **PAC sees the sanitized URL** (proxy/mod.rs `pac_target`, used by windows.rs `auto` and macos.rs `resolve`): like Chromium's SanitizeUrl, user name, password and fragment are removed, and for https / wss also the path and query (`https://host[:port]/`). New unit test plus a WinHTTP PAC case whose script answers with `url.length` and matches `*secret*` in the URL: https gives `https://path.unimux-b3.test/`, http keeps `/abc?d=1`.
3. **Redirect refusal per caller** (request.rs, client.rs, calls.rs): `Redirect::Error` (usage reads, Electron net.fetch `redirect: 'error'`) refuses only 301 / 302 / 303 / 307 / 308 with a non-empty Location (Chromium's IsRedirect); 300, 304, 305, 306 and a redirect status without Location come back as answers. The new `Redirect::ErrorAny3xx` (token renewal, tokenTransport.ts directFetch) refuses every status from 300 to 399, 304 included. `Redirect::Follow` uses the same non-empty Location rule. `redirects_are_refused_when_asked_to` covers 300 / 302 without Location / 304 / 305 / 399 for the token renewal and 300 / 304 / 305 / 306 / 302 and 307 without Location for the usage reads.
4. **Proxy fallback only while the proxy cannot be used** (connect.rs `ConnectFailure { error, try_next }`, client.rs `send_via`): the next PAC entry (DIRECT included) is tried after TCP or DNS failures of the proxy, TLS to an HTTPS proxy and a broken CONNECT / SOCKS exchange (closed, garbage, no acceptable method). It is not tried after a CONNECT reply the proxy chose (403, 407, 502, ...), a SOCKS refusal code, a SOCKS 4 host without an IPv4 address, or a TLS / certificate failure with the server itself. All of these stay `FetchError::Connect`, so the token renewal's direct-then-system rule (1.1.4's secureConnect test) is unchanged. New tests: `a_proxy_that_answers_with_a_refusal_is_not_bypassed` (403 / 407 / 502 then a working proxy and DIRECT: the second entry gets 0 connections), `an_origin_certificate_error_through_a_proxy_is_final`, and the tunnel unit test checks `try_next` per reply. Judgment call: the two reviews disagreed on SOCKS refusal codes; they are treated like a CONNECT refusal (the proxy answered, its decision is kept). A DIRECT entry that cannot be reached still lets a later entry try, as before.
Verified on Windows 11 26200: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy -p unimux-net --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test -p unimux-net` (12 unit + 31 integration, all green, incl. the WinHTTP PAC case), `UNIMUX_NET_ONLINE=1` online test (api.github.com 200 over h2 and HTTP/1.1), `cargo check --workspace`. The macOS change (two lines in macos.rs: `pac_target` before `cf_url`) was not compile-checked for aarch64-apple-darwin this time. No TypeScript changed, so the Electron build and vitest are unaffected.

### W1 (2026-11-07): review fixes and open issues

Wave 1 (14cd4bb) merged into `tauri-pkg-W1` first (conflicts: commands/window.rs keeps W5's detach:* and W1's window:* in one register(); create_main keeps W3 / W6 and installs the caption after lifecycle::attach_main; test_bridge keeps U2's update-stage and the caption op; Cargo feature lists and package.json as the union; contract, messages and goldens regenerated with the scripts).

Review fixes:
1. **System menu on a right click** (high): DefWindowProc never opened it (its own hit test sees client area over WebView2's drag region). The window's subclass now opens it with TrackPopupMenu (Restore / Move / Size / Minimize / Maximize enabled from IsZoomed / IsIconic, Close the default item) and posts the chosen command, as Windows Terminal's `_OpenSystemMenu`. A check-build seam records the menu and its item states instead of showing it; the CONTEXT_MENU_SEEN logic is gone.
2. **Native layout** (medium, three findings): the hit areas no longer come from the page. caption.rs lays both children out itself on WM_SIZE / WM_DPICHANGED from GetClientRect, GetDpiForWindow and IsZoomed (`layout` = captionLayout + captionHitRects, `top_edge`), so the buttons work before React mounts, after a failed boot, after a W6 renderer crash or reload, while the renderer is busy and right after a maximize / restore / snap / DPI change. The page's rects are compared (inside the client: the page knows the width only to a pixel at 125 / 150 percent) and a difference is logged once per window. The page's oversized-rect sanity check is gone with it (a page can no longer place the hit areas at all).
3. **Maximized state race** (medium): the page asks `isMaximized()` again on every size change (every maximize and restore changes the size) and on every `window:shown`, so a maximize between the first answer and the listener's registration (W5's first_show of a saved-maximized window) cannot leave the maximize glyph; the hit areas follow IsZoomed natively anyway.
4. **Top resize edge** (medium): a second child along the full width answers HTTOPLEFT / HTTOP / HTTOPRIGHT for rows inside Electron's top border (SM_CXSIZEFRAME + SM_CXPADDEDBORDER floored to DIP, corners 12 DIP), hidden when maximized, and posts a press / double-click to the window (SC_SIZE + WMSZ_TOP / TOPLEFT / TOPRIGHT). verify-caption now compares every code of the 1.1.4 map (buttons, HTTOP, HTTOPRIGHT, HTCAPTION, HTCLIENT) at every sampled pixel, all equal.
5. **Window procedure soundness** (medium / low): one `Caption` per top-level window, owned by the parent's subclass (reference data) and freed in its WM_NCDESTROY; only shared references, every field a Cell, so ReleaseCapture / SetWindowPos re-entry never aliases a `&mut`; the handler only queues (an unbounded channel per window drained by one task holding a Weak<AppContext>) and runs inside catch_unwind. Children are created without the state and get the non-owning pointer after a successful CreateWindowExW (no split ownership on a failed attempt).
6. **Handle lifetime** (low): the label map is gone; the caption is found from `window.hwnd()` through the subclass on the UI thread, so a re-created `detached-<id>` gets its own children and no stale HWND is ever dereferenced; the test probe runs on the UI thread (run_on_main_thread + oneshot).
7. **Hover after a click** (low): the capture ends the leave tracking; after a release over a button the leave is tracked again (Windows posts it at once when the pointer is elsewhere), WM_CAPTURECHANGED clears the tracking, and a minimize or hide clears hover and press. Captured points are converted with MapWindowPoints.
8. **Styles** (low): Windows Terminal's exact styles (WS_EX_LAYERED | WS_EX_NOREDIRECTIONBITMAP, no WS_EX_NOACTIVATE), with the non-layered fallback (see open issue 2).
9. **show_without_focus** (low) is SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE only again; the off-screen branches (show_main, W5 host, W6 ask_renderer, spike windows) call `windows::show_offscreen`, which parks the window, turns its DWM transitions off (DWMWA_TRANSITIONS_FORCEDISABLED: a minimize / restore of the check draws no animation on the monitor) and then shows it.
10. **W5 detached windows** (merge): built with `apply_chrome` and `caption::install`. 1.1.4's saved bounds (Electron's getBounds of a frameless window = the visible window) now map to the client rect on Windows (tao's undecorated window has invisible borders left, right and below, outside the visible frame): `outer_rect` reads and `place_native` sets the client rect, so a 1.1.4 state.json opens at the same visible place and size. verify-tauri-detached on the merged tree: all checks passed (a restart reopens both windows at the same physical place).
11. **Accessibility** (medium, the finding's minimum): the tooltip anchors are `role="button"` with `aria-label` (tr()) and an onClick that runs the command (Narrator scan mode / UI Automation Invoke on the WebView2 tree), and the buttons child's window text is the three names (`setCaptionArea(area, labels)`). verify-caption checks the roles, the names in the accessibility tree (CDP Accessibility.getFullAXTree) and the window text; the 6 languages of the three keys are enforced by test/i18n.test.ts.
12. **Harness** (merge): verify-caption is on X1's harness (spawnApp, bridgeCall with blocked-by, our tree only) and in real-app-run's list (Windows, Tauri only, skipped without the wincap tool); `window:*` channels are owned by W1 in real-app-owners; the detached part opens its window through W5's real path (the renderer's "Move to a new window"). Its "off-screen" checks run after the show: they stop a run on a leak, the prevention is the app's own off-screen show.

Rejected / not done:
- Real mouse on a separate Win32 desktop (finding "stand-ins"): SendInput only reaches the input desktop, so a CreateDesktop desktop gets no real cursor without switching to it, which would show it on the user's screen; the SP3 VM was not available to this run. W1 stays "verified with stand-ins".
- A full UI Automation provider (WM_GETOBJECT with three Button fragments) on the buttons child: not done; the minimum of the finding is in. Open issue 4.

The top edge height follows Electron (SM_CXSIZEFRAME + SM_CXPADDEDBORDER at the DPI, floored to DIP); the references were taken on a 96 DPI monitor with a forced device scale factor, which the check reproduces by taking the metrics at the forced scale's DPI. On a real 125 / 150 / 200 percent monitor the metrics come from the window's DPI (same formula); not observed.

### W2 (2026-11-06): macOS window chrome, app menu, WebKit compatibility

Done (code): `mac_chrome.rs` (unimux-app) puts 1.1.4's chrome on the windows: `TitleBarStyle::Overlay`, hidden title, traffic lights (12, 10) main / (12, 11) detached (`WindowKind::Detached` waits for W5), `BackgroundThrottlingPolicy::Disabled`; the menu bar is Electron's `appMenu` + `editMenu` rebuilt as data (labels and accelerators of Electron's role table, no View / Window menu, so Cmd+R / Cmd+W / Cmd+M reach the page), predefined muda items where Tauri has them, own items sending `pasteAsPlainText:` / `delete:` / `startSpeaking:` / `stopSpeaking:` down the responder chain. `unimux-platform::mac`: App Nap off for the process life (`NSProcessInfo beginActivityWithOptions: UserInitiatedAllowingIdleSystemSleep`), WebKit's dash / quote / text replacement / spelling correction / continuous spelling / grammar / link detection defaults registered NO before the first webview (registration domain, nothing written to the preferences file). Renderer: `bridge/webkitCompat.ts` installed first by the bridge (requestIdleCallback polyfill with a 50 ms budget, crypto.randomUUID from getRandomValues, AudioContext subclass resumed on the next gesture, Cmd+V paste guard dropping the menu's `paste:` after a Cmd+V keydown the page handled), report in `window.__MC_COMPAT__`; `bridge/macDrag.ts` (macOS only) replaces `-webkit-app-region: drag`, which WKWebView ignores: a primary press on `.titlebar` outside 1.1.4's no-drag holes starts a native drag (`core:window:allow-start-dragging`), a double click calls `mac_title_double_click`, which follows the user's "Double-click a window's title bar to" setting (`AppleActionOnDoubleClick` / `AppleMiniaturizeOnDoubleClick`: zoom, minimize or nothing), both granted by the macOS-only capability `capabilities/mac-window.json`; `maxWebglContexts(engine)` gives 12 under WebKit (cap 16), 64 on Chromium, `WebglLimit` learning and the DOM fallback unchanged. Eval bridge (`eval_bridge.rs`, `test-bridge` only, `MC_EVAL_PORT`): CDP's Runtime.evaluate over a 127.0.0.1 JSON-lines socket, client `scripts/eval-bridge.mjs`, smoke `scripts/smoke-eval-bridge.mjs` (`npm run smoke:eval-bridge`), CI job `mac-smoke` (macos-latest: `npm run build:check` + the smoke). `scripts/mac-check.mjs` compile-checks the macOS code on Windows (stub C compiler for the Apple target's build scripts; type checking is real, nothing is linked). `sp5_echo.rs` and a few `cfg_attr`s were made to compile for macOS.

Verified (Windows 11 26200, 2026-11-06): `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -D warnings` workspace (default and `dev-overrides,test-bridge`) and unimux-app / -platform / -pty `--release`; `node scripts/mac-check.mjs clippy` (aarch64-apple-darwin, workspace + unimux-app with `dev-overrides,test-bridge`) and `node scripts/mac-check.mjs -- clippy --workspace --all-targets --release -- -D warnings`: all clean. `cargo test` unimux-app 17/17 (menu layout vs Electron's role table, traffic lights, eval-bridge wrapper and delivery), unimux-platform (incl. the title double-click setting), unimux-pty unit + plane. `npm run typecheck`; `test/webkit-compat.test.ts` 15/15 (incl. the drag area against 1.1.4's drag / no-drag CSS rules); `npm run build:check` + `node scripts/smoke-eval-bridge.mjs` 13/13 on WebView2 (offscreen, scratch data dir and home: bridge answers, errors and by-value results, renderer boots, compat report, requestIdleCallback, v4 randomUUID, AudioContext, WebKit-only shims off on Chromium, no app menu on Windows, real profile untouched).

Review fixes (2026-11-07):
1. About panel copyright (minor): the About item's `AboutMetadata` now carries 1.1.4's Info.plist line, `Copyright © <build year> ajzak, azozowsky` (electron-builder's default from package.json `author` when `build.copyright` is unset; the year is the build's, written by build.rs as `UNIMUX_BUILD_YEAR`), instead of falling back to tauri.conf.json's "MIT". Test `about_panel_has_1_1_4s_copyright_line` checks the line against package.json and that the menu code passes it.
2. Acceptance never ran (major): W2 stays "in progress" (not done) until `mac-smoke` and the macOS halves of `checks` / `build` are green on tauri-pkg-W2 or a CI rehearsal branch; the run id goes here then. Nothing can be pushed from the agent, so this needs the user / integration step.
3. Eval bridge vs Runtime.evaluate (minor): requests that do not parse as an expression now run as statements through the host's script execution (`eval_with_callback`: completion value of a `try` block, thrown errors kept in the page and read back by a probe script), so `var el = ...; el.dispatchEvent(...)` and `const a = 1; a` work like CDP and a SyntaxError comes back at once instead of after the timeout. Indirect eval was not used because the page CSP has no 'unsafe-eval'. Known gaps: a promise a statement script ends with is not awaited, `const` / `let` stay local, and on WKWebView a statement script ending in a value NSJSONSerialization cannot take (a `Date`) is not supported. ARCHITECTURE.md and scripts/eval-bridge.mjs say so; smoke +5 checks (statements, `var` reaches the page, undefined -> null, thrown errors, SyntaxError under 3 s).
4. WebKit prewarm (minor): `pickPrewarm` takes the reserve as a parameter, `webglPrewarmReserve(engine, limit)` keeps 1.1.4's MAX_CELLS on Chromium and half of the pool under WebKit (6 of 12), so parked cells on macOS still get contexts in idle time (up to 6; a bigger grid draws the rest through the DOM renderer at first, documented in terminalLimits.ts). TerminalManager uses it; tests cover limit 12, a lowered pool of 8 and the call site. R3: grid-switch smoothness on a Mac.

Verified after the fixes (Windows 11 26200, 2026-11-07): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo test -p unimux-app --features dev-overrides,test-bridge` 19/19 (new: About copyright line, statement wrapper and probe answers); `cargo clippy -p unimux-app --all-targets -D warnings` (default and `dev-overrides,test-bridge`); `node scripts/mac-check.mjs clippy` (aarch64-apple-darwin) clean; `npm run typecheck`; vitest `webgl-pool` + `webkit-compat` + `i18n` 47/47; `npm run build:check` + `node scripts/smoke-eval-bridge.mjs` 18/18 on WebView2 (statement scripts, `var` on the page, thrown errors, SyntaxError in 4 ms). Still not run: anything on macOS (open issue 1).

2. The macOS half was not run (no Mac): the WryWebView wrap, `mc_dropped_paths` and the import on `tauri://localhost` are compile- and clippy-checked only. Matching by name means two dropped files with the same name from different folders get their paths in drop order. W2 / macOS CI must run it.

Pre-existing on `tauri-1.2.0`, not W3 code, found by the macOS check: `update_window.rs` calls `WebviewWindow::hwnd()` without `cfg(windows)` (E0599 on macOS) and `windows.rs::browser_args` is unused on macOS (clippy -D warnings fails). Owner: U2 / W2. The macOS check on Windows also needs a C cross compiler (objc2-exception-helper); there is none on this machine.
4. Detached windows (W5) must call `file_drop::install` on their webviews too; the import script is on the main window only (one import per profile).
5. `test/launch-reset.int.test.ts` (real powershell.exe in node-pty) timed out with empty output in this worktree, alone and in the full run; W3 touches no pty or shell code. An environment issue to look at outside W3.
6. An import not yet confirmed (the app ended before WebView2 wrote the entries and the sentinel to disk, or before the first page reported) is offered again on the next start; stored keys are kept, but a key the user deleted in that run comes back. The sentinel `mc:webview-import` stays in localStorage until a later start confirms the import; if the app is killed between that confirmation and the removal, the key stays (harmless, nothing reads it).
