### Open issues after W5 (2026-11-06)

- W5a: mixed DPI is verified by cargo only (screen.rs: mixed-DPI layout, left / below neighbours, detached monitor). Both monitors of this machine run at 100 percent, so the real-app run moved a window to the second monitor at the same scale. Rerun `verify-tauri-detached.mjs` on a machine (or VM) with 125 / 150 percent on one display.
- W5b: macOS not compiled (no Mac); the host code is platform-neutral Tauri API except the WebView2 browser args / data dir under `cfg(windows)`. Traffic-light position (12,11) of detached windows belongs to W1 / W2 with the caption work.
- W5c: display ids are no longer Electron's (a hash of name + position, see screen.rs). A 1.1.4 registry keeps its `displayId` as data; only `bounds` decide where a window reopens (restoreBounds), so placement is the same. A 1.2.0 id written back is meaningless to 1.1.4 (it also only uses bounds).
- W5d: the hooks later packages plug into `ctx.detached.hooks()` are empty: pty route / connectRenderer / forgetRenderer (P2 / P3), DB and SFTP ports and session routes (D1 / D12 / T1), `is_alive` (P3; until then `detach:returned.alive` is always `[]`). `relay_cell_status` (cell:runtime, P3 / W7), `cell_window` (M4), `cell_waits` (W7 taskbar) and `sender_window` (D1 / T1) exist but have no caller yet. W6 must call `set_quitting(true)` in its quit sequence (`close_all` already runs on ExitRequested and when the main window is destroyed).
- W5e: not ported yet: reload of a detached window after a WebView2 renderer crash (1.1.4 `render-process-gone` -> reload; belongs with the main window's crash handling, W6) and the dev-only `console-message` mirror of detached windows.
- W5f: domains must emit renderer messages through `ctx.events.send(channel, args)` (routed like 1.1.4) and use `emit(Scope::...)` only for per-window events; the F3 `set_cell_owner` hook is replaced by the owner table (`Events::set_owners`).
- W5g: a place save with unchanged bounds makes no state change (1.1.4 counted a revision for it; nothing else differs).
- W5h: under a parallel load the vitest real-shell tests (cli-install-shared, launch-reset.int, ptyhost.int, github-git, context-usage throttle) and the unimux-state broadcast 200 ms timer test time out; all pass when run alone.

### W5 review fixes (2026-11-07)

| finding | fix | proof |
|---|---|---|
| major: the ScreenWin physical-to-DIP model is wrong on macOS with mixed scales (tao scales monitors by their own factor, the cursor by the primary's, windows by their current one) | `screen.rs`: `Monitor::from_scaled`, `unscale_rect` / `unscale_point`, `Screen::from_points` (points are Electron's DIPs on macOS, every conversion the identity); `TauriHost` on macOS builds the screen in points, unscales window rects by the window's factor and the cursor by the primary's, and places with `LogicalPosition` / `LogicalSize`; Windows keeps the ScreenWin layout | cargo `macos_mixed_scales_work_in_points` (Retina 1440x900 pt @2 + external 1920x1080 pt @1 with tao-style values; the old layout of the same values is asserted wrong); `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p unimux-platform -p unimux-ipc` |
| minor: `place_physical` measured the frame before the move to a display with another DPI | `place_native`: moves first, measures the frame on the target display, sets the inner size and the position again, repeats (at most 3 times) until the outer rect is the target | real-app run (single DPI on this machine; the mixed-DPI rerun stays W5a) |
| minor: `window:shown` only on the first page load | `page_finished` sends `window:shown` on every `PageLoadEvent::Finished` (1.1.4 `sendShown` on every did-finish-load); show / maximize stays first-load only. A Tauri listener is registered by an async IPC call (Electron's `ipcRenderer.on` is synchronous), and the real-app run lost the send of a window's first, cold load, so the state is sent again 300 ms and 1.5 s after each load (the renderer ignores a repeated value) | verify-tauri-detached: a hidden detached page hears `shown = false` on the first load and again after `location.reload()` |
| minor: a registry id outside Tauri's label alphabet (`a.b`, a space) failed `InvalidWindowLabel`, was retried forever and its tab could not come back | `route::detached_label` keeps plain ids (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`) as `detached-<id>` and hex-encodes others as `detached-:<hex>` (':' never in a plain id, so no collisions; still matches the `detached-*` capability); `detached_id` decodes only canonical labels; the page URL escapes the id like `encodeURIComponent` (`route::query_escape`, what 1.1.4's `loadFile` query did) | cargo `ids_outside_the_label_alphabet_are_hex_encoded`, `an_id_outside_the_label_alphabet_still_opens_and_comes_back`; verify-tauri-detached: a restart with a registry entry `old.tab 1` opens as `detached-:6f6c642e7461622031` with `?detached=old.tab%201`, owns its cell, and closing it puts the grid back |

2. The 1.1.4 side effects of the `hooks.on(...)` handlers are not wired yet: hookWatch (H2), automation status hints (M3), the context tracker pokes and transcripts (H4), the CLI exit codes `cliEnd` (P3 / M3), `codexHooked` read by codex:bind (H4). They attach through `Hooks::observe` (every signal, before routing) and `Hooks::codex_hooked`. P3 must get the Codex notify URL through `Hooks::prepare_codex(cell)` (not `HookServer::prepare_codex`, so the cell counts as unhooked until the new process reports) and call `Hooks::forget(cell)` from the cell removal path (1.1.4 `forgetCell`).

### Open issues: G1

1. Mapped network drives and UNC shares were not in the canonical-path lab (no share on this machine); the libuv prefix rule (`\\?\UNC\` -> `\\`) is implemented and unit-checked only. A1 / G3 real-app checks on a machine with a share should add one.
2. macOS: `cargo clippy -p unimux-git -p unimux-exec --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin -D warnings` passes (compile-checked); the tests (symlink instead of junction, posix dirname / resolve, realpath(3)) have not run on a Mac.
3. `tests/canonical_paths.rs` needs Node (it runs src/main/gitPaths.ts through `scripts/git-paths-golden.mjs`, Node 22.18+ type stripping) and skips the TS comparison with a note when `node` is missing; the subst-drive part creates and removes a subst drive (`UNIMUX_TEST_NO_SUBST=1` leaves it out). The slow-git test uses a Node stand-in like the TS test.
4. Intended differences from git.ts: a lone UTF-16 half at a `trimGitText` / `relativeInside` cut becomes U+FFFD (a Rust string cannot hold it; the golden reader maps it the same way); duplicate spellings of an environment variable on Windows are merged before the quiet variables are set (Node would hand both to the child); after a normal exit the remaining tree of git is ended by the Job Object (unimux-exec rule; git for Windows runs auto-gc in the foreground, so nothing of git's is lost). A panic inside `Git::shared` work is a `failed` answer for the callers of that run (git.ts has no panics; a rejected promise is the nearest).
5. Not ported in G1 (owners): trust scan / GitTrustStore and the other shared/git.ts parsers (G2), status / log / show / diff / mutate / network auth retry / publish (G3), worktrees / clone (G4); no IPC command is registered yet (G3).
6. The pre-existing load-sensitive test `unimux-exec output_over_the_cap_is_truncated_and_the_tree_ends` failed in all four G1 runs, with the machine at 100 percent CPU (other agents and apps): `cmd` printed less than 256 KiB in its 20 s budget, so `truncated` stayed false. G1 does not change that path (`stop_at_cap` defaults to true, the kill condition is the same); the new `output_over_the_cap_is_dropped_without_stopping_when_asked` passed in 3 of 4 runs (once also starved). Both tests should get a faster output source (e.g. a larger echo per line or a helper binary) so they do not depend on cmd's speed; recheck on an idle machine before merging. Final G1 run: all 9 unimux-exec tests passed, both cap tests included.
7. Shared checks of the final run: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy -p unimux-git -p unimux-exec --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test -p unimux-git -p unimux-exec` (28 + 9) and `npm run typecheck` pass. `npm test`: 3947 passed, 5 failed in real-PowerShell / timing tests (cli-install-shared, context-usage throttle, launch-reset.int); a rerun of those files alone failed a different single launch-reset case on a 15 s powershell.exe timeout. G1 changes no TS source (only scripts/dump-goldens.mjs and the new scripts/git-paths-golden.mjs), so these are load-sensitive and unrelated; recheck on an idle machine.
8. Merge step: `ExecOptions` has a new public field `stop_at_cap`, so a full struct literal without `..Default::default()` stops compiling (E0063). `tauri-pkg-H3` / `tauri-pkg-H3-fixes` has one in `src-tauri/crates/unimux-agents/src/cli/system.rs` (around line 55); whichever of G1 and H3 merges second must add `stop_at_cap: true` there (the old behaviour). No other package branch builds ExecOptions without `..Default::default()` (checked 2026-11-07).
9. Review-fix run (2026-11-07): `output_over_the_cap_is_truncated_and_the_tree_ends` failed again in both runs (machine under load, cmd printed less than 256 KiB in 20 s); the fixes do not touch unimux-exec code, and the item 6 remedy (a faster output source) is still open.

### G5 open issues (2026-11-06)

1. **INT96 nested in a list or struct** keeps the parquet record reader's `Int96::to_millis` (nanoseconds of the day floored); hyparquet truncates the whole nanosecond count toward zero, so a pre-1970 value with a sub-millisecond part shows 1 ms later. Top-level INT96 columns are read raw and match exactly (golden `types.parquet`).
2. **Parquet corpus is written by parquet-rs only** (`cargo run -p unimux-files --example parquet_fixtures -- ../test/golden/parquet`). No files from Spark / pyarrow / DuckDB yet; a legacy LIST whose repeated group has more than one field (not "list like") and the BSON / INTERVAL refusal paths beyond `bson.parquet` are not compared with hyparquet.
3. **macOS**: compile-checked only (`cargo check -p unimux-files --all-targets --no-default-features --target aarch64-apple-darwin` clean). With the `parquet` feature the cross check stops at zstd-sys (no C cross compiler here); a native macOS CI run builds it. The macOS forbidden roots (bundle Resources, ~/Library/WebKit and Caches for both bundle ids) are untested.
4. `test/redis-drafts.test.ts` (2 cases, not G5) timed out once in a full `npm test` run under load and passed alone (6/6).
5. `scripts/goldens-files.mjs` loads src/main/files.ts through a vite SSR server for the Parquet rows (1.1.4 imports hyparquet lazily, which a closed `runnerImport` runner refuses).
6. **Accepted deviation: a Date map key** (a DATE / TIMESTAMP key of a Parquet MAP). 1.1.4 made it `String(date)`, V8's `Date.prototype.toString` in local time with ICU's localized zone name ("Thu Jan 01 1970 01:00:00 GMT+0100 (czas środkowoeuropejski standardowy)"), which depends on the machine's time zone and ICU locale; Rust keeps the ISO text. Other keys follow `String(key)` (bytes '1,2,3', BigInt digits). Not in the goldens (they would be time zone dependent).
7. **Accepted deviation: a LIST / MAP group with a logical type and no converted type.** hyparquet's isListLike / isMapLike look at the converted type only and would assemble such a group as a struct; the parquet crate's record reader assembles it as a list / map. Writers set both (parquet-rs, Arrow C++, parquet-mr), so no fixture. Also a file whose logical and converted types disagree is refused by the parquet crate ("Nie udało się odczytać pliku Parquet.") while hyparquet reads it.

### Open issues: G2

1. Scope split with G3 / G4: G2 also took `consume` (request ids), `grant_trust` / `revoke_trust`, `worktrees` / `resolve_dir` and the gitHandler.ts pipeline, because `git:trust*` cannot run without them. G3 adds its channels to `handler::run` (and uses `require_trust`, `forget_scan`, `consume`); G4 registers `git:worktrees` on `Git::worktrees` and adds creation / removal. G3 / G4 / A6 must use the one engine of `commands/git.rs`: `commands::git::engine(ctx)` returns the same process-wide instance on every call (a `OnceLock`, like the CLI detector of commands/agents.rs), so they share one trust store, generation, scan cache and request-id set.
2. The git-trust cases that need status, stage, commit, fetch, diff or the untracked-diff confinement (`runs nothing on any IPC channel`, `runs what git runs only after the explicit trust`, the write operations, environment and scope cases) are G3's; 5 rows are `partial` for that reason.
3. `verify-git-layout.mjs` still needs G3 (`git:probe`), G4 (`git:worktrees`) and A6 (`github:status`); `git:trust` is now registered. No real-app check was run for G2 (only cargo tests through `handle_git`).
4. Intended differences: a field git.ts reads past the end of an empty status XY field (`x` / `y` undefined in JS) is left out of the JSON exactly like 1.1.4, so `GitFileChange::x` / `y` are `Option`. A lone UTF-16 half (`s[0]` of an emoji) becomes U+FFFD (the project rule). On Windows the hooks folder is walked in the file system's order like libuv; that order only matters when a hook cannot be stat'ed or read (1.1.4 stops the walk there). On POSIX the names are sorted by bytes like libuv's scandir.
5. The trust tests are slow under load (the TS comparison spawns Node + Vite and about 60 git runs: 90 s on this machine with other agents busy). `tests/trust.rs` needs Node and `node_modules/vite` for the comparison and skips it with a note otherwise; the other trust tests use Node only for the marker programs (without Node those programs cannot start, so the "nothing ran" checks still hold).
6. macOS: not compile-checked for aarch64-apple-darwin in this package (no unix-only code beyond `executable()` with PermissionsExt and the byte sort of hook names).
7. Review fixes (2026-11-07, branch `tauri-pkg-G2-fixes` = `tauri-pkg-G2` + merge of tauri-1.2.0 + fixes; the review worktree still had `tauri-pkg-G2` checked out, so the fixes sit on their own branch that fast-forwards it): (a) the credential-helper rule of b39025b (security): `SAFE_CREDENTIAL_HELPER` ported with JS `\s` = `JS_SPACE` and ASCII `\w`; `credential(.*).helper` is flagged when the value is `!` / absolute or not one of manager, manager-core, wincred, osxkeychain, libsecret, gnome-keyring, cache, store with plain `--opt[=value]` arguments (or empty). The goldens were regenerated from the fixed gitTrust.ts (21 random git-trust.jsonl rows changed their answer, e.g. `credential.helper = true` is now flagged; 76 fixed rows added for the helper cases, with `credential.helper` and `credential.<url>.helper`, incl. `manager\ncalc`, U+00A0 / U+3000 / U+0085 / U+FEFF and non-ASCII option names); tests/shared.rs has the vitest cases. (b) `TrustStore::set` / `delete` write git-trust.json while the map lock is held, so concurrent grants / revokes reach the disk in map order (unit test: 60 rounds of 6 threads granting and revoking, the reloaded file equals the map); `grant_trust` / `revoke_trust` run the write in `spawn_blocking` and await it. (c) `commands::git::engine` returns one shared instance (see 1).

### Open issues: G4

Fast mode (2026-11-07): built, not verified. No review round, no real-app / CDP run (verify-git-layout needs G3 and A6 too, verify-safety-ui needs P4), no macOS run.

1. **G3 overlap**: G4 added `Git::network` (A6 sign-in, one retry without it, `remote_auth_refused`) and `Git::busy()` (`BusySet`, the `busy` set of git.ts) because clone needs them. G3's fetch / pull / push / mutate must use these (or the merge must fold G3's own versions into one); the auth comes in as a `RemoteAuth` argument (commands/git.rs `GithubAuth` over A6's `GithubService`), not through `GitOptions`.
2. **Same trust rules as 1.1.4, not stricter**: worktree.ts ran `git worktree add` / `remove` / `branch` without the Git panel's trust check, so a repository's post-checkout hook runs on create, as in 1.1.4. The renderer names the project folder as a path for createWorktree / inspectWorktree / removeWorktree (as in 1.1.4; the channels are guarded to the app windows). Restricting them to the projects in state was left out (a race with a just-added project).

Command failed: ...`; a `git:inspectWorktree` / `removeWorktree` with a malformed worktree answers `{ok: false}` / MC_BAD_ARGS where 1.1.4 threw a TypeError; worktree runs get the runner's global safety arguments (worktree.ts had only `-c core.fsmonitor=false`).
4. `git:installGit` never runs winget / xcode-select under MC_OFFSCREEN (test runs); the real installers were never started on this machine (rule). The macOS `xcode-select --install` path is untested.

8. The one `cargo test -p unimux-git -p unimux-app` run (machine under load): all G4 tests passed. Two tests that G4 did not touch failed: unimux-git `tests/runner.rs::finds_the_root_from_a_subfolder_and_tells_folders_without_a_repository` (it reads the root from the 5 s cache after a rename, so it fails when the steps before took longer than 5 s) and unimux-app `g8::tests::the_monitor_samples_this_process_and_refuses_kills_outside_a_cell` (`queryMs < 20`). Both look load-sensitive; they were not run again.

### G6 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. **macOS**: compile-checked only (`cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p unimux-files --no-default-features --tests`, `-p unimux-platform`). Not run there: case-only rename on APFS, `trash_item` through NSFileManager (changed from the trash crate's Finder / AppleScript default, which needs the Automation permission), Finder reveal of bundles, the umask-applied folder modes of a copy, and the review fix that copies a file with `copyfile(3)` and `COPYFILE_ALL | COPYFILE_EXCL` like libuv, so the data, mode, ACL, BSD flags and extended attributes (com.apple.quarantine, Finder tags, resource forks) survive a copy, a same-folder duplicate and an EXDEV move (test `macos_copies_and_moves_keep_extended_attributes`, compiled with clippy for aarch64-apple-darwin, not run). Other Unix targets copy the data and the mode.
7. **Review fixes (2026-11-07)**: raw Win32 path calls (`CopyFileW` in `fsx::copy_file_excl`, `MoveFileExW` in `fsx::rename`, used by rename, move and the local pane) get the `\\?\` / `\\?\UNC\` extended-length prefix from 248 UTF-16 units on (`fsx::long_path`, Rust std's rule, path resolved like Node's `toNamespacedPath`), so paths past MAX_PATH work as in 1.1.4 without a `longPathAware` manifest; before, they failed with "Plik lub folder już nie istnieje." A folder link whose junction cannot be set (FAT / exFAT) no longer leaves an empty folder in the copy: `fsx::symlink` and `copy_tree` remove the plain empty folder the junction crate made, like libuv's `fs__create_junction`. Both new Windows tests fail without the fix. After the fixes: cargo unimux-files all suites pass (lib 16, fileops 43, fileops_golden 3, files 27, golden 5, pane 5, parquet_panic 1), clippy -D warnings (default, no-default-features, aarch64-apple-darwin no-default-features with tests), `npm run build:check` and `node scripts/verify-tauri-fileops.mjs` 15/15.
2. **`local:reveal` of a folder cannot fail any more**: 1.1.4 awaited `shell.openPath` and answered `failed` on its error text; the os service's desktop opens folders fire-and-forget on a thread (W4), so the pane answers ok once the stat passed. The stat still catches a missing path.
3. **Links on Windows**: a link is what Rust's `FileType::is_symlink` says (name-surrogate reparse points), libuv says symlink or mount point only; other name-surrogate tags would be copied as links instead of being followed / noted. A copied file symlink stores its target as read (no `\\?\` prefix like Node's `toNamespacedPath`); the link resolves the same. Copying a file symlink needs Developer Mode or the privilege, as in 1.1.4 (else a note).
4. The Windows trash (trash 5.3) uses IFileOperation with FOF_NO_UI | FOF_ALLOWUNDO | FOF_WANTNUKEWARNING; Electron passes FOFX_RECYCLEONDELETE and no nuke warning. An item that cannot be recycled (no Recycle Bin on the volume, too large) may ask before a permanent delete instead of failing; not provoked here (would show a dialog). Tests use a folder trash or MC_TRASH_DIR only.
5. Copy speed was not compared with 1.1.4: the real-app run copies about 600 to 1200 entries per second of 32 KiB files on this machine (CopyFileW + lstat + times per file, Defender on), varying with load.
6. The harness sweep table below still lists verify-sftp-edit / -path / -ui as blocked by G6 (`local:roots`); they stay blocked by T1.

### G8 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. **Real cells**: the Monitor lists cells from `Monitor::set_cells` (cell id, shell pid). Until P3 tracks the running pty shells, only the check build's test bridge (`monitorCells`) hands them in, so a normal build shows the app section only. P3 must call `commands::files::g8::monitor()?.set_cells(...)` with its live (cellId, pid) list; the console host matching (ConPTY's OpenConsole / conhost started right before the shell, parent = unimux.exe now that there is no pty host) is covered by `without_a_pty_host_the_console_hosts_are_the_apps_own_children`.

The kinds stay in the TS type for the Electron build until R1.
3. **macOS**: compile-checked only. `cargo clippy -p unimux-files --all-targets --no-default-features --target aarch64-apple-darwin -D warnings` is clean; `node scripts/mac-check.mjs clippy` type-checks unimux-app but stops on 15 dead-code errors in W1's `caption.rs` (not G8). 6. Review fix (2026-11-07): fs:exists called GetFileAttributesW and fs:pathKinds std::fs::metadata on the raw path, so a folder past 260 characters was missing (no longPathAware manifest) and `C:\Windows.` / `C:\Windows ` existed, unlike 1.1.4 (Node namespaces the path before libuv). Both now go through `jspath::to_namespaced_path` (port of Node's win32 toNamespacedPath, cases checked against Node 26). Tests: `to_namespaced_path_like_node`, `a_folder_past_max_path_exists_like_node_access` (over 300 characters, folder and file), `a_trailing_dot_or_space_is_kept_like_node`; `cargo test -p unimux-files` all green, fmt and clippy clean.

### Open issues after H1 (hook server)

1. Until P3 tracks running processes no cell counts as alive (`Hooks::set_alive`), so a SessionStart conversation switch (`cell:session`) and a Codex turn end are dropped like late hooks of an old process. No terminal can run before P3, so nothing is lost; P3 must call `set_alive` with its live set. The check build's test-bridge op `hooks` sets it for the harness.
2. The 1.1.4 side effects of the `hooks.on(...)` handlers are not wired yet: hookWatch (H2), automation status hints (M3), the context tracker pokes and transcripts (H4), the CLI exit codes `cliEnd` (P3 / M3), `codexHooked` read by codex:bind (H4). They attach through `Hooks::observe` (every signal, before routing) and `Hooks::codex_hooked`.
3. writeClaudeSettings, claudeHookSettings, prepareCodex's caller, bypassProxyForHooks and the settings halves of 2 ported cases (main-misc "sends bodies only for notify", status-continuation "SubagentStop hook wiring") are H2. No real Claude Code / Codex has posted to the Rust server yet (that needs H2's settings file and P3's spawn); the HTTP side is proven with Node's http client against the 1.1.4 server and the Rust server on the same 118 requests.
4. Deliberate deviations, same status codes: a 403 / 400 / `/x/` answer is sent after the body was read (Node answers at once and lets the client finish sending), so a large rejected body never ends in a connection reset; the 5 s limit is hyper's header timeout plus a 5 s body deadline from the start of the handler (Node's `requestTimeout` counts both together), so a client that trickles its headers for 4 s and then its body for 4 s gets 204 instead of 408. Idle keep-alive connections follow hyper's defaults, not Node's 5 s `keepAliveTimeout` (Claude Code and curl open one connection per hook).
5. macOS: `cargo check -p unimux-agents --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin` passes; the app crate does not check for macOS from Windows (objc2-exception-helper needs a macOS C toolchain; not H1-specific). No macOS run.

### Open issues after H3 (2026-11-07)

A first run under the same load had both detectors time out the 8 s probe (equal, but not found). The apps half was not rerun (no app code changed; `build:check` too slow on the loaded machine).

### G7 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. **Empty match before an astral character with `u`**: 1.1.4 did `lastIndex++`, which lands inside the surrogate pair; V8 steps back to the pair's start and the same empty match came again forever (the search froze 1.1.4's main process). 1.2.0 skips the whole code point (what `matchAll` does). The only intended difference from 1.1.4 (`tests/search.rs::an_empty_match_before_an_emoji_ends_with_the_u_flag`).
2. **Patched regress**: `src-tauri/vendor/regress` is regress 0.12.0 with unimux patches (`[patch.crates-io]`, marked `unimux patch`): the Annex B case-insensitive class closure (src/unicode.rs, src/parse.rs), a negated class escape inside a class folded before it is inverted with `i` + `u` (`add_class_atom`), surrogate pairs joined in group names without `u` (`next_group_name_code_point`) and MAX_NESTING_DEPTH 10,000 instead of 256 (src/types.rs). None is upstream yet; a regress upgrade must carry them over, and the search goldens catch a lost patch.
6. **Nesting limit**: V8 has no fixed nesting limit (its limits are the capture count, about 32,000, and the compiled code size, which fail at exec time past about 20,000 nested groups). 1.2.0 refuses more than 10,000 nested groups or classes with `Regular expression is too deeply nested`; a pattern with more than 200 `(` or `[` compiles on its own thread with a 512 MiB reserved stack, since the parsers recurse once per level.
3. **Catastrophic backtracking**: regress backtracks like V8's irregexp, so a pattern such as `(a+)+$` on a long line can run for a long time. The cancel flag and the time budget are checked between files (as in 1.1.4), so such a search occupies one blocking-pool thread until the line is done; the UI stays responsive (1.1.4 blocked its main process). A step limit inside the matcher is not available in regress.
4. **macOS**: compile-checked only (`cargo check -p unimux-files --all-targets --no-default-features --target aarch64-apple-darwin`); the real-app check runs on Windows.
5. `scripts/verify-tauri-search.mjs` finds the regex toggle by its text: TooltipHost moves an element's `title` aside while the pointer rests on it, so a `[title=...]` selector fails on the second click (the renderer behaves like 1.1.4).

### H4 open issues (2026-11-07)

Verified on Windows 11 26200: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy -p unimux-agents -p unimux-app --all-targets -- -D warnings` (default and `--features unimux-app/dev-overrides,unimux-app/test-bridge`) and `cargo clippy -p unimux-agents --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p unimux-agents` all green (139 unit, 4 sessions golden, the H1 / H5 suites); `cargo test -p unimux-app agents` 2/2; `npm run gen:check` (ipc contract, messages, full golden corpus up to date with the 4 new files); `node scripts/porting-count.mjs --check` 348 / 2,143; `npm run typecheck`; vitest real-app-harness + sessions + context-usage 41/41 (no TS source changed, so the full `npm test` was not run); `npm run build:check`; `node scripts/verify-tauri-sessions.mjs` 17/17; `npm run smoke:tauri` 34/34 (a first run gave 33/34 while `gen:check` loaded the machine; the rerun passed); `npm run ipc:report` 62 / 250 methods (agents 9/15).

Run: `npm run build:check && node scripts/verify-tauri-sessions.mjs` (scratch data dir and home; compares with 1.1.4's sessions.ts and ContextTracker on the same folders).

1. Accepted deviation: a start time Date.parse cannot read (a broken `timestamp`) is NaN in 1.1.4 and reached the renderer as NaN through Electron's structured clone; the Tauri JSON bridge carries it as `null`. A non-string truthy `timestamp` (never written by Claude Code / Codex) is NaN here where 1.1.4 parsed `String(v)`.
2. Accepted deviation: a title cut by `.slice(0, 140)` inside a surrogate pair ends in U+FFFD instead of the lone surrogate (the goldens hold one such row; js_json reads the TS answer the same way).
3. A non-string Codex `payload.id` (a number) is listed and returned like 1.1.4 does, but only string ids are remembered as claimed by codex:bind (1.1.4 put the number into `claimedCodex`).
4. sessions:list over a session file with a non-string cwd is rejected like 1.1.4's TypeError; the rejection text differs (the renderer only catches it).
5. The branch of a loose worktree folder comes from unimux-git's runner (its global safety arguments added to `rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`); the answer is the same, the environment is the runner's scrubbed one instead of worktree.ts's `gitEnv()`.
6. Hooks for later packages: P3 must call `commands::agents::context_tracker(&ctx)` -> `forget(cell)` in its forgetCell path and keep `Hooks::set_alive` current (context pokes for Codex turn ends and codex:bind need live cells); M4's list_agents reads `current(cell)`.
7. Accepted deviation (review fixes): a complete transcript line of 16 MiB (MAX_CHUNK) or more made 1.1.4's `scanBackwards` read the same chunk forever (the chunk ends with that line's end and holds no other, so `end` never moved): the context meter of that cell hung and the loop kept awaiting reads. The Rust read stops there with what it found (offset at the end of the file). No golden can hold the 1.1.4 answer; tracker.rs `a_complete_line_longer_than_max_chunk_stops_the_backward_read` covers it.
8. macOS / Linux: `fsstat` uses `fs::metadata` (birthtime from `created()`, 0 where the file system has none; the inode still tells a replaced file); compile-checked for aarch64-apple-darwin only, no Mac run. The goldens avoid case- and drive-dependent paths so `gen:check` on macos-latest should produce the same files, but CI has not run.

### H4 review fixes (2026-11-07)

Branch `tauri-pkg-H4-fixes` (from `tauri-pkg-H4` 530d6bf; `tauri-pkg-H4` itself is checked out in the builder's worktree, so the fixes sit one commit on top of it).

1. minor, fixed: a Claude transcript whose session id is the empty string was listed with id "". `claude_info` now drops an empty id after `head.id ?? tail.id` (an empty head id hides the session even with a real id in the tail, as `"" ?? x` is ""), and the tail is asked for an id when the head id is missing or empty (`!head.id`). Golden: sessions-list.jsonl scenario `empty-ids` (empty head id, empty head id plus a tail id, empty first tail id, a non-string tail id before a real one, a normal session).
2. major, fixed: `context_sessions_and_codex_bind_channels_answer_like_main` failed about half the time in a full `cargo test -p unimux-app` because every `register` replaced a process-wide TRACKER. The tracker is now kept per app context (`context_tracker(&ctx)`, a list of weak context / tracker pairs); `register_context_with` returns the tracker its handlers use and takes the CLI folders, so the test uses scratch `claude-config` / `codex-home` folders and never reads the real ~/.claude. The tests that register the agents domain (conv test, H4 test, `every_registration_matches_the_contract`, `w4_channels_are_implemented`) share `commands::REGISTER_LOCK` for the CONV handle.
3. minor, fixed: tests of the limits. context-backward.jsonl +3 recipes: an unfinished last line of 17 MB (scan empty, offset 0), a usage line 90 MB back (never found: no chunk starts past 64 MiB) and 60 MB back (found). tracker.rs: `a_file_that_grew_by_more_than_max_forward_is_read_backwards_again` (growth of exactly 8 MiB reads forwards, 8 MiB + 1 byte backwards, told apart by a usage line rewritten in place) and `a_complete_line_longer_than_max_chunk_stops_the_backward_read`. Writing these showed that 1.1.4 hangs on a complete line of 16 MiB or more (open issue 7); the Rust read now stops there.
4. minor, fixed: `list_sessions` collects the distinct loose worktree folders first and asks git for their branches all at once (up to 16 threads, one lookup per folder, first spelling of its path), like 1.1.4's Promise.all. Test: `asks_git_for_the_branches_of_loose_worktrees_all_at_once_and_once_per_folder` (5 folders, 300 ms lookups, all 5 overlap).

Verified: Windows 11 26200: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy -p unimux-agents -p unimux-app --all-targets -- -D warnings` (default and `--features unimux-app/dev-overrides,unimux-app/test-bridge`) and `cargo clippy -p unimux-agents --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p unimux-agents` all green (142 unit, 4 sessions golden, the H1 / H5 suites); the whole `cargo test -p unimux-app` 72/72 (4 runs), and the four register tests (`w4_channels_are_implemented`, `context_sessions_and_codex_bind`, `every_registration`, `conv_channels`) 40 runs in a row with 0 failures (21 of 40 failed before); `npm run gen:check` (contract, messages, golden corpus up to date); `node scripts/porting-count.mjs --check` 348 / 2,143; `npm run typecheck`; vitest sessions + context-usage 28/28; `npm run build:check`; `node scripts/verify-tauri-sessions.mjs` 17/17.

### H5: bench and open issues (2026-11-07)

`node scripts/bench-convsearch.mjs --rounds 5` (medians in ms, 1.1.4 TS indexer in Node 26 vs Rust release; the machine was shared with other agents' builds, so absolute numbers moved up to 5x between runs; the ratio held in every run after the fixes below):

| measure | fixture TS | fixture Rust | 600 files TS | 600 files Rust |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| cold pass (no cache) | 3.25 | 1.82 | 6,284 | 1,378 |
| rescan, nothing changed | 1.28 | 1.01 | 310 | 75 |
| cache load + pass | 2.59 | 1.35 | 1,136 | 348 |
| transcript preview (largest file) | 1.56 | 0.59 | 34.6 | 10.3 |
| search (7 queries) | 0.01 to 0.05 | 0.01 to 0.02 | 11.7 to 132 | 5.0 to 16.0 |

What made the first Rust version slower than 1.1.4 on passes (fixed in this package): a thread start per pool call (this machine took up to 45 ms per 100 thread starts under load) - now one process-wide pool of 4 to 8 workers (rayon-core) kept between passes, fewer than 8 items run inline; `fs::metadata` opens a handle per file - now GetFileAttributesExW by path (links, folders, long paths and errors still go through `fs::metadata`); the inode was read through a second open - now from the handle the file is read through; the Codex walk was sequential - now 8 folders at a time in the same order; big indexes (1M+ folded code units) are matched on separate search workers, outside the entries lock (same results, tested both ways).

Open:

1. The pass workers stay alive (sleeping) after the 10-minute idle stop; 1.1.4 ended its worker thread. The index memory is freed as before; only the 4 to 8 idle pass threads and the 4 to 8 idle search threads remain.
2. `conversation-index-v2.bin` is about the size of the indexed text in UTF-16 (54 MB for the 160 MB bench set, units below 256 stored as one byte). A first 1.2.0 start reads every transcript once (the v8 file cannot be read); with a large ~/.claude this first pass takes seconds, as a first 1.0.9 run did.
3. The bench numbers come from a machine shared with parallel builds; the 4-file fixture timings are 1 to 3 ms, and a busy moment can flip one of them (the script then fails; run it again). A quiet-machine run before the release is worth recording.
4. macOS: `cargo clippy -p unimux-agents --target aarch64-apple-darwin` is clean (stat through `fs::metadata`, inode from the open file); not run on a Mac.
5. A request that arrives while the idle stop saves waits for the save (at most 20 s) and then starts a fresh index that loads the saved file; in 1.1.4 such a request went to the old worker until it was terminated.
6. Shared checks on 2026-11-07: cargo fmt / clippy -D warnings (unimux-agents, unimux-app incl. test-bridge, unimux-agents for aarch64-apple-darwin), cargo test unimux-agents 20 + 8 golden + 13 index, unimux-app 12, typecheck, gen:check, porting --check, vitest conv-search + conv-export 31/31. The full `npm test` gave 3,949 passed / 3 failed in timing tests H5 does not touch (background-stop passes alone; launch-reset.int with real powershell.exe failed in different cases on each run while the machine ran other agents' builds); no TS source changed in H5. After the review fixes (2026-11-07): cargo fmt --check, clippy -D warnings (unimux-agents incl. aarch64-apple-darwin, unimux-app with test-bridge), cargo test unimux-agents 20 + 8 golden + 13 index + 2 pass-search, unimux-app 7, the same index + pass-search tests in Docker on 4 CPUs, vitest conv-search + conv-export 31/31, `npm run build:check`, verify-tauri-convsearch 27/27, smoke-tauri 34/34.

### D3 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. Unpaired surrogates: a Rust string cannot hold one, so 201 generated rows whose input or answer holds one are left out of the vectors (`dump-goldens` prints the count). Text cut by a helper inside a pair (e.g. `mysqlServerLabel`'s `slice(0, 40)`) becomes U+FFFD in Rust. D1 / D12: renderer strings with lone surrogates never reach these helpers through serde (the IPC layer decides what such a call answers).
2. Shapes the Rust types cannot hold (an `after` entry that is not a string, an edit with an unknown `op` or a missing `set` / `values`) are refused as `invalid` in the vectors' harness, matching where the TS loop throws. D1's IPC validation must refuse them the same way (1.1.4's zod answers `invalid` before the builders run).
3. `values::json_value` answers what `JSON.stringify` writes (NaN / infinities as null) instead of a NaN; it is only used by `rows_to_json`, whose text is identical. The renderer keeps its TS copies of formatDbValue / parseDbInput / the exports; the Rust ports exist for host-side use.
4. `redis_shared::data_display` / `quote_arg` give a string for broken base64 where TS throws (atob): D10 must check `data_bytes(..).is_some()` for renderer argv first (the harness does).
5. test/golden/db-sql.jsonl is 17 MB (normalize-state.jsonl is 11 MB); shrink the random part if the repository size matters.
6. Accepted deviation: `values::format_db_value(pretty)`, `json_value` and `rows_to_json` treat JSON nested deeper than 1000 levels (`PRETTY_DEPTH`) as text: shown unformatted and exported as a JSON string. 1.1.4 (V8) pretty-prints and exports it as a value up to a stack-dependent limit (about 2500 levels in node, different in the renderer) and past that `rowsToJson` throws RangeError and the export fails. A fixed cut-off cannot match a stack-dependent one; 1000 keeps the recursive pretty-printer and serde_json's drop safe on any thread. `values::tests::json_nesting_cut_off` pins depth 1000 (pretty, same text as TS) and 1001 (text).

Review fixes (2026-11-07): `mysql_timeout_sql` (MariaDB) now prints `(ms / 1000).toFixed(3)` from the exact value of the double (round half up at 3 decimals, like V8) for every ms, so huge timeouts (past about 8.8e15 ms, unreachable through DB_LIMITS.statementTimeoutMaxMs) match too; `pg::first_keyword_units` is one linear pass from the end (a 4-byte answer per unit) instead of memoized backtracking (8 MiB of `/**/` or `(` took minutes or about 1.1 GB, now about 0.1 s). New vectors: 1.4k huge / random mysqlTimeoutSql rows and 2.65k firstKeyword backtracking rows (comments that only lead somewhere at a later `*/`), plus `comment_and_parenthesis_heavy_statements_are_checked_in_linear_time` at the 8 MiB limit. Verified: cargo fmt, clippy -D warnings (unimux-db, unimux-core, all targets), cargo test -p unimux-db -p unimux-core, npm run gen:check, npm run typecheck, npm test 3,955 passed (none failed this run).

### D1 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. **db-coordinator cases are partial.** test/db-coordinator.test.ts tests the SshCoordinator (routing `db:` / `sftp:` ids to their hosts, per-host snapshots, `sessionHostCrashed`). D1 has the DB side only: the `DbCoordinator` trait the service calls (`connect_session`, `end_session`, `answer`, `prompts`, `on_host_message`, `session_host_crashed`) and its tests with a fake coordinator; the app runs `NoCoordinator` (a tunnel login is refused with `unsupported`, no tunnel prompts). S3 implements the trait on its coordinator and finishes the three rows in PORTING.md; S3 must also route the coordinator's `db:` session states and prompts to `DbService::on_coordinator_session` / `on_prompt` (1.1.4 index.ts:1114).
2. **No sessions yet.** `UnavailableHost` stands in for the DB Host until D4 / D7 / D9 / D11 (routed by D12): `ensure()` is true, every `dbOpen` ends the session with `db:session {state: 'error', error: 'unsupported'}` (translated "not supported" text in the UI), nothing reaches `connected`, so `db:pg` / `db:redis` answer `notConnected` after their validation. The real host implements `DbHostLink` and must call `host::with_deadline` on Redis ops and use `PendingRequests` (op timeouts, `dbHostCrashed` on a crash) and `CrashCounter` (more than 5 crashes in 60 s); D12 adds the HOSTS wind-down step for the sessions (1.1.4 `dbHost.shutdown()`, 3 s) and calls `DbService::host_crashed`. verify-db-core / -pg-ui / -mysql-ui / -redis-ui / -sqlite-ui / verify-detach-db stay blocked on them (D1's channels are no longer the blocker).
3. **zod 4.6 counts code points.** `z.string().min/max` of zod 4.6.7 measures code points once the UTF-16 length is over the limit (`util.codePointLength`), so an emoji counts 1, not 2 (found by the vectors: 8 of 27k rows differed while the Rust schemas counted UTF-16 units). `unimux_db::ipc::S::Str` counts code points. Other packages that port zod validation (S1/S3 ssh IPC, A-packages, M-packages) must do the same; plain `.length` checks in TS (`a[0].length`, the export name, `DB_HISTORY_LIMITS`, `DB_LIMITS.name` in strictConnection) stay UTF-16.
4. **Accepted deviations.** (a) The query history is saved in the wind-down's SAVE phase ("db history save", after "state save"), i.e. after the renderers' 150 ms grace, where 1.1.4 flushed it at the start of before-quit: a record the page sends in its last moment is kept now (verify-tauri-lifecycle expects the step). (b) A session closed before its deferred start ran opens nothing and takes no tunnel reference (1.1.4 still sent `dbOpen` for it from `setImmediate`). (c) A refused sender gets the router's `MC_REFUSED` instead of the per-channel fallback value (central guard, F2).
5. **Shared helpers.** `unimux_db::files` (readJsonFile / ensurePrivateDir / writePrivateFile of src/main/ssh/files.ts) is a private copy for the DB stores; S1 ports the same helpers for the SSH stores, and one copy should move to unimux-core once both are merged. `unimux_state::normalize::remote::normalize_db_connection` is now `pub` (strictConnection uses it).
6. **W4 file touched.** `commands/os/desktop.rs` got `Desktop::save` (rfd save dialog; the offscreen recorder records `{op: 'save', title, defaultPath, filters, parent}` and answers from the same queue) and `PickRequest::more_filters`; `OsService::pick_file` / `save_file` let other domains show dialogs through the recorder. The DB dialogs belong to the main window like 1.1.4 (`showOpen` / `showSaveDialog(win)`).
7. **Not run here:** macOS (the vault's eager decrypt runs only for DPAPI like 1.1.4, so on a Mac the Keychain is first read when the DB vault warms on its background thread at start, as 1.1.4's constructor did); `cargo check -p unimux-db --target aarch64-apple-darwin` only. test/golden/db-ipc.jsonl is 9 MB (see D3 open issue 5 for the size question).
8. **macOS dialogs with a catch-all filter (review fix, not run on a Mac).** rfd 0.17's macOS panel puts every filter's extensions into one `setAllowedFileTypes` list, so the DB dialogs' `{ name: '*', extensions: ['*'] }` would allow only files ending in a literal `*`. `desktop::dialog_filters` drops all filters on macOS when any has the extension `*` (Electron's file_dialog_mac.mm stops filtering then too); Windows keeps them (rfd gives `*.*`). Checked by a unit test on Windows only; a Mac run of `db:sqlite:pick` / `db:pickCaFile` is still to do.
9. **Timer callbacks get their own id (review fix).** A `TokioTimers` callback that already started waits for the service / history lock and cannot be cancelled any more (clearTimeout in single-threaded JS could). `Timers::after_with_id` passes the timer id to the callback; the idle disconnect acts only while the session's `idle` field still holds that id, the history write only while `inner.timer` does. The prompt timeout is already guarded by its prompt id and the test-connection limit by the one-shot `test` sender. `ManualTimers::fire_late` reproduces the race in `db_main.rs` (`an_idle_timer_replaced_while_it_was_already_firing_does_not_disconnect`, fails on the old check).

### D4 open issues (2026-11-07, fast mode)

1. **Not verified**: hostd `db.*` domain and db-fake.int on the rust host are not done. db-fake.int drives the whole DbHost (runs through PgSession with cursors and statement timeouts), so it needs D5 (runs) and the real DB host (D12 wiring of `DbHostLink`); the hostd domain should be added with them. The Docker PG 13-18 connect / auth matrix (md5, SCRAM incl. the SASLprep password of SP9, TLS modes against a real server, CancelRequest on 13 and 18) was not run.
2. **Nothing uses `unimux_db::pg` yet**: `UnavailableHost` still answers every open. The session (D5) and the host (D12) call `pg::connect::open` with a `Connector` (TcpConnector, or D2's tunnel channel) and a `PasswordPrompt` (dbPasswordPrompt / dbPasswordAnswer), then `cancel::cancel_backend` with the session's password and `PgClient::key()`. Dropping the open future is how a closed session stops a connect.
3. **TLS differences to Node**: rustls has no CBC suites and no TLS 1.0 / 1.1 (Node 22's default minimum is 1.2 too); verify-full without a CA uses the OS trust store (Node: its bundled Mozilla list); a certificate without a DNS SAN is not matched on its CN (Node falls back to the CN); a CA that is an intermediate is accepted as a trust anchor (Node needs the chain to a root). The dbTls message is rustls' text (`TLS: ...`), not OpenSSL's.
4. **Prefer** opens a second connection after the server's `N` (as node-postgres does), instead of going on in plain text on the same socket.
5. **Environment**: as recommended in SP9, nothing is read from PG* variables or ~/.pgpass (1.1.4 took `PGOPTIONS` from the environment); to be named in the 1.2.0 notes.
6. **Status message cuts**: `ConnectError::message` drops a lone high surrogate when the 4,000-unit cut splits a pair (status messages go through serde_json to main); the DB port texts in `pg::port` keep it like JSON.stringify.
7. **macOS**: `cargo check -p unimux-db --target aarch64-apple-darwin` now needs a C compiler for ring (rustls); not available here.

### Open issues after A1

1. A lone UTF-16 surrogate in a string of accounts.json (only possible in a hand-edited file) is read as U+FFFD and written back as that character, where 1.1.4 writes the `\udXXX` escape again: the same rule as state.json (F7, `js_json::parse`). The golden test applies this rule to the one row with such a string; every other row is byte-identical.
2. `acquire_dir_lock` treats ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED from mkdir of the lock path as a held lock (waited for within the timeout) only while the lock path still exists (a lock dir another holder just removed, still delete-pending while a handle is open, where stat fails with access denied too); 1.1.4 threw EPERM there. Found by `many_threads_never_hold_one_lock_at_once`. Access denied with no lock path (no right to create folders in the parent) is returned at once as LockError::Io after 3 immediate tries, as 1.1.4 does. No file or lock path changes, so Claude Code and claude-swap are unaffected.
3. The byte-identical no-op check uses accounts dirs written by 1.1.4's store code run under Node (src/main/accounts is unchanged since v1.1.4), not a dir from an installed 1.1.4 app: there is no 1.1.4 test data on this machine and the user's real data is off limits. Run it once on a copy of real 1.1.4 data when that exists.
4. macOS: compile-checked only (`cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin`); the 0600 mode of the Codex file, the symlink realpath of the legacy lock and the interop test were not run on a Mac.
5. No `accounts:*` commands are registered yet (A2 to A6 call into these stores). tests/lock_interop.rs needs node and node_modules: without them it skips with a note; CI sets `UNIMUX_REQUIRE_LOCK_INTEROP=1` so it fails instead.
6. The store golden test takes 10 to 17 s (every write fsyncs, like 1.1.4); 64 s with a full workspace build running next to it.
7. `js_json::parse` reads nesting up to 1,000 levels; JSON.parse has no fixed limit. A Value is cloned, compared and dropped by serde_json's recursive derived code (5,000 levels overflow a 1 MB stack on clone), so a hand-edited accounts.json deeper than 1,000 levels is still treated as unparsable (Claude store: `.bak` and an empty store; Codex store: read-only), where 1.1.4 drops or keeps the records.
8. Without node or node_modules/proper-lockfile the interop cases return early and cargo lists them as passed (a note goes to stderr); CI sets `UNIMUX_REQUIRE_LOCK_INTEROP=1`, so there they fail instead.

### Open issues after A2 (2026-11-07)

1. No `accounts:*` command is registered yet and the app does not run the start-up recovery: the handlers (`handle_make_main` + `check_activation_target` + `activate_account`, `activate_codex_account` / `check_codex_target`, `handle_adopt_copy`, `handle_remove_account`, `dismiss_account_notice`, `login_block`, `has_pending_swap` for `accounts:swapPending`, `clear_pending_activation` + `clear_pending_swap` for `accounts:clearSwapPending`) and `SwapSlot` are ready, but wiring them needs the renewal (A3 `refreshClaudeProfile` on `ProfileLocks`), the login flags (A4), the usage refresh after a swap (A5) and the `accountsRecovered` / `accountsReady` gates (W7). Whoever registers the accounts domain also adds the start order of 1.1.4 (`recover_activation_with_retry` with 3 tries, 1 s apart, a 3 s lock timeout and the quit as `stopped`; on `legacy` `recover_interrupted_swap`; then `start_activation_recovery_retry` every 30 s) and the quit's swap waits (W6b: `SwapSlot::idle` / `wait_idle`). verify-accounts-ui and the other scripts that name A2 (`accounts:swapPending`) stay blocked until then.
2. Ported from A3's files only what "make main" needs: `renewRule` (refs.ts), `withProfileLock` and `RefreshOutcome` (refresh.ts, as `refresh::ProfileLocks` with `lock` for threads and `lock_async` for the async renewal), `heldTokenHash` (resync.ts, `rules::token_hash`) and cswap.ts whole. 5 PORTING rows are partial because the TS case also runs `reconcileLive` / `reconcileWhenIdle` / `resyncActiveBackup` (A3); 4 cases have no row yet: the cswap external switch and the main re-login own change of accounts-activate (A3 / A5) and the two LoginManager cross-checks of accounts-makemain (A4).
3. Deliberate difference: the swap's temp files are flushed to disk before the rename (1.1.4's `fs.promises.writeFile` did not fsync). File contents, names and order of steps are unchanged; only a power loss right after a swap step can no longer leave an empty credentials file.
4. Like A1 open issue 1: a lone UTF-16 surrogate in a string of Claude Code's `.claude.json` / `.credentials.json` (JSON.stringify writes it as `\udXXX`) is rewritten by a swap as U+FFFD, where 1.1.4 kept the escape. No such text was seen in real files; the golden rows use other unusual strings (U+2028, emoji, numbers past 2^53, index-like keys, 1e21).
5. The renewal and cswap seams are blocking closures (`Fn(&Path) -> RefreshOutcome`, `Fn() -> Result<CswapRoster, ()>`): the whole activation blocks (Claude Code's locks wait up to 9 s each) and must run on `spawn_blocking`; A3 bridges its async renewal with `Handle::block_on` there. A panic in the renewal closure counts as `transient` like a TS throw; the roster closure answers `Err` instead of throwing.
6. Slow on this machine: the activate golden test takes 60 to 145 s and its TS generation about 160 s (file creation in %TEMP%, Defender), `npm run gen:check` about 10 min in total; the kill test about 20 to 30 s.
7. The kill test interrupts the activation before each rename of the swap's own files and at 12 random moments; an interrupted accounts.json write (unimux-core atomic_write, A1) is only reached by the random kills. Such a kill leaves `accounts/accounts.json.<pid>.<base36>.tmp` behind and nothing removes it, exactly like 1.1.4 (atomicWriteFileSync's tempPathFor, no sweep in src/main); accounts.json itself stays whole. The test leaves that one name out of its "no temp file" check. A sweep of dead-pid store temps would be a deviation from 1.1.4 and is not done.
8. Threads: in 1.1.4 `reconcileCodexLive` and `activateCodexAccount` never interleaved (no await, one Node thread). Here they share `SwapSlot`'s Codex files lock: the reconcile only reads while a Codex make main holds it, and a make main waits for a reconcile in progress, then checks its target again. A user's make main is still answered `busy` only while another Codex make main runs, as before.
9. macOS: compile-checked only. "Set as main" of a Claude account answers `unsupported` there as in 1.1.4; the Codex make-main and the 0600 temp files were not run on a Mac.

### Open issues after A3 (2026-11-07, fast mode)

1. Not verified beyond one cargo run: no real-app run, no review round. The start-up recovery (A2) and the migration now run in every real start of the app on the real home (as 1.1.4 does); they were never run in the built app.
2. Still no `accounts:*` / `auth:*` / `usage:*` channel: A5 registers them and calls `ClaudeAccounts::before_poll` (after A4's `retryParkedRestores`), `refresh_claude` (usageDeps.refreshClaude), `refs` / `panel_info` / `panel_notices` (`accountsInfo`, `notices`); A4 sets `set_login_probes` (isActive, isMainActive, holdsProfile), calls `before_main_login` and `mark_own_login_change`. pushSwapPending after a recovery (the `after` hook of `start_recovery_retry`, the `on_accounts_ready` push) is A2/A5's once `accounts:swapPending` exists; the hook is empty now.
3. Deliberate differences: the renewal's temp file is flushed before the rename (like A2's swap writes); the renewal runs as its own task (1.1.4's promise also always ran to the end); `RefreshLimits` keeps a JS Map's insertion order with a Vec. `readProfileIdentity` of the migration reads `<dir>/.claude.json` directly (profileSource never looks at the Keychain on any OS, same result).
4. Retry-After as an HTTP date: `httpdate` accepts the IMF-fixdate, RFC 850 and asctime forms; Date.parse also took other forms (ISO 8601 and more), which now read as no Retry-After (the 429 still gets the exponential wait).
5. A reconcile that finds a `readLiveIdentity` error, a resync error or a read-only store behaves as 1.1.4; a store write error other than read-only (disk full) ends the reconcile with an error the poll ignores, as the TS throw did.
6. PORTING partial: "skips the outgoing resync while a swap holds the slot ..." (the held slot is covered through `reconcile_when_idle` only) and the held case of "marks nothing for a cleared login of another identity ...". Not ported: "sets a second live record of the live account aside without a switchedOut notice", "resolves at once when the backup is already current", the `gateFamilyRefresh` case, the dead-login cases that go through the swap / usage paths (A2 / A5), the token-transport cases of B3's transport.
7. macOS: not compile-checked for unimux-accounts (it depends on unimux-net, whose `ring` needs a C compiler for the Apple target here); `darwin` paths (no resync, refs without profiles) are unit-tested only through flags.

### A6 (2026-11-07): GitHub sign-in, open issues

Real-app check: `npm run build:check && node scripts/verify-tauri-github.mjs` (scratch data dir with the SP2 1.1.4 `github/token.json` + `Local State`, scratch `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, no `GH_TOKEN`: the user's own `gh` sign-in is never read and no request reaches github.com). 12/12 on Windows 11 26200: the 1.1.4 blob decrypts in place through the scratch `Local State` (DPAPI, quick status `sp2-spike`), token.json and Local State stay byte for byte, sign-out removes the file, full status signed out through `gh`, device flow `no-client`, bad / unknown flow ids `cancelled`, real profile markers unchanged. The `gh` the app runs there is a stand-in first on its PATH (a copy of where.exe, exit 1 = signed out): a first run with only a scratch `GH_CONFIG_DIR` showed that a real `gh auth token --hostname github.com` still answers from the Windows Credential Manager, so that run read the machine user's own GitHub CLI token and the app made one `GET https://api.github.com/user` with it (status `source: gh`, nothing stored or written; the same thing 1.1.4 does). The script now never lets the real gh run.

- A6a. The device flow itself never ran against the real github.com: 1.1.4 ships no OAuth App (`GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID = ''`, the dialog asks for `gh auth login`), and the same constant is kept. The flow is covered by the fake-fetch ports and a hyper mock GitHub over the real http_fetch client (code, pending, token, login, file on disk, exact header sets). Registering an OAuth App is a user decision (unchanged from 1.1.4).
- A6b. `gh` lookup: Node's libuv on Windows tries the current directory before PATH; unimux 1.2.0 does not (the app's working directory is no place to pick a program up from). Otherwise the lookup is libuv's (`.com`, `.exe`, the env's PATH sliced like libuv's `search_path`: an entry starting with `"` or `'` runs to its closing quote, so a `;` inside is part of the folder, and one outer quote character at each end is dropped). An empty or quotes-only entry is skipped where libuv would try it as the current directory (same reason). A `gh` found but not executable on POSIX is skipped (libuv would stop with EACCES and answer "no token"); both end without a token unless a later place has gh.
- A6c. Messages GitHub sends are cut at 300 UTF-16 units like `slice(0, 300)`; a surrogate pair cut in half loses its first half in Rust (JS keeps the lone surrogate). Display text only. A `version` written as `1e400` (JSON.parse Infinity) reads as null through `js_json::parse`, so such a hand-edited file is not read-only as in 1.1.4.
- A6d. safeStorage calls (first open of the key, decrypt, encrypt) run on the async worker that handles the command, as 1.1.4 ran them on its main thread; on macOS the first one may wait for the Keychain prompt (B2: lazy Keychain access). macOS: compile-checked only (no Mac); the 0700 / 0600 modes and the `gh` lookup on POSIX ran only as unit tests on Windows (the POSIX test is `cfg(unix)` and did not run).
- A6 review fixes (2026-11-07): (1) the Windows PATH slicing above (a single-quoted entry and a double-quoted folder containing `;` were missed before, while 1.1.4 finds gh there); (2) forgetting a refused stored token is one step under the token store's lock (`GithubTokenStore::clear_if`), so a device-flow `set` landing between the compare and the clear is no longer wiped (1.1.4 cannot interleave there on its one JS thread). Tests: `windows_path_entries_like_libuv_search_path`, both PATH forms in `windows_lookup_like_libuv`, `store_clear_if_keeps_a_token_set_meanwhile` (a decrypt held inside clear_if while a `set` waits for the lock; it shows the new token survives, it cannot replay the old race, which needed a hook between two lock acquisitions).
- A6e. `github:publish` is G3 (it uses `github_service(ctx)`: `auth.token()`, `auth.rejected()`, `api::create_repo`, `git_env::github_git_env`, `remote_auth` / `remote_auth_refused`). Until then the Publish dialog can sign in / out and show the status, and publishing answers MC_NOT_YET. (G3 2026-11-07: `github:publish` is registered.)

### G3 (2026-11-07): open issues

Fast mode: built and unit / integration tested only (see the G3 row); no review round, no real-app run.

1. Not run: verify-git-layout.mjs (also needs G4's `git:worktrees`), the Git panel flow in the real app, `npm run ipc:report`, smoke-tauri (its not-yet probe now asks `sftp:sessions`, T1). The git-shared-reads cases (one status for concurrent identical requests) are not ported; the dedupe is G1's `Inflight` and `status` uses it with the same key as 1.1.4.
2. Merge with G4 (parallel): G4 adds `git:clone` / `init` / `installGit` / `worktrees` / worktree commands on the same engine. Clone in 1.1.4 uses `network(..., cloneTimeout, github)` and the busy key `clone\0<target>`: both exist now (`Git::network` is public, `Git::try_busy` takes a key in the same busy set). If G4 added its own `busy` / `network`, keep one. Both packages add fields / modules to `unimux-git/src/lib.rs` and `commands/git.rs` (G3 made `project_path` / `worktree_paths` `pub(crate)` and registers `git:probe` there).
3. `RemoteAuth` (unimux-git) replaces the `remoteAuth` / `remoteAuthRefused` callbacks; the two refusal regexes stay in unimux-accounts `git_env` and the app's `AppRemoteAuth` calls them. The engine looks the GitHub service up at each network run (`commands::github::service_if_started`), so a fetch before the `github` domain registered runs without the sign-in (it registers at start, so this should not happen).
4. Intended differences: `config_stamp` uses the modification time in nanoseconds (1.1.4 `mtimeMs`), only compared with itself. A dropped (cancelled) mutation request still releases the repository lock and the caches through a drop guard (1.1.4 cannot drop a promise). The catch-all `failed` answer of publish.ts (a thrown exception) has no Rust counterpart; every git failure is a typed `GitFailure`.
5. Partial PORTING rows (see test/PORTING.md): the command-line spy of test/github-git.test.ts (token never on a command line) and the 401 local-server case are not ported; a remote-branch checkout with `--track`, a rejected non-fast-forward push, folder pathspecs and concurrent writers (busy) only partly.
6. macOS: not compile-checked (no unix-only code added).

### M1 review fixes (2026-11-07, branch `tauri-pkg-M1-fixes`, on top of `tauri-pkg-M1`)

`tauri-pkg-M1` is checked out in the M1 worktree, so the fixes are one commit on `tauri-pkg-M1-fixes` (fast-forward of `tauri-pkg-M1`); merge that branch.

1. major, closed sessions kept answering: a DELETE, the 4-per-token trim or a revoke now ends the session's open SSE streams (`SseBody` watches the session's CancellationToken; the dispatch task, progress and elicitation senders queue nothing once the session is closed), so no `cancelled` / `Agent connection has expired.` answer follows the close, as with 1.1.4's transport.close().
2. major, initialization check: `jsonrpc::is_initialize` is the SDK's isInitializeRequest (the whole InitializeRequestSchema: protocolVersion string, ClientCapabilitiesSchema, ImplementationSchema; no id needed). A new-session POST without a valid initialize answers 400 -32000 `Bad Request: Server not initialized` and creates no session; an invalid initialize no longer skips the Mcp-Protocol-Version check; the closed-session 404 comes before the initialization checks (SDK order).
3. minor, zod checks: `schema_issues` covers `initialize` (zod 4 issue list in schema key order, incl. the intersection double issues of `elicitation` / `form`, AssertObjectSchema `custom` issues, icon `invalid_value`) and `tools/list` (`cursor`), so both answer -32603 and a bad tools/list no longer marks the cell connected.
4. minor, `notifications/cancelled`: dropped unless its params pass CancelledNotificationParamsSchema (`reason` a string when present, so `"reason": null` no longer cancels; `requestId` a RequestId).
5. minor, lone surrogates: ids are byte-identical now (`surrogate_ids.rs`: a string literal with an escaped lone surrogate travels as a marker; ids and a cancel's `requestId` keep it and are written back as JSON.stringify writes them, lowercase `\udxxx`). Deliberate deviation, kept: such strings elsewhere (tool arguments, names) still reach the tools as U+FFFD, since a Rust string cannot hold a lone surrogate.
6. major, `issue_token` race: the revoke and the new token happen under one lock (`revoke_locked`), `revoke_cell` removes every token of the cell; 500 rounds of two concurrent issues plus a revoke leave at most one token.
7. minor, panic hook: MCP request, session-call and task futures run marked (`unimux_mcp::in_request()`, a thread-local set per poll), and the app's hook returns early for them, so a request panic gives only the server's 300-char `MCP request failed` line and no `panic:` line or dialog (1.1.4 handle().catch). The check build's tool layer has a `test_bridge_panic` tool (feature `test-bridge` only) for scripts/check-mcp-app.mjs.
8. minor, `seen` check-then-act: `seen_if_live(token, cell)` re-checks liveness under the lock that records last_seen (tools/list and call_outcome).

Verification: cargo test -p unimux-mcp (11 unit, 48 server incl. concurrent issue/revoke, replaced-token call, lone-surrogate cancel; tests/protocol.rs 57 / 57 identical to the TS golden, regenerated with `node scripts/spike-mcp-wire.mjs ts --protocol --golden`, 5 new cases: invalid initialize, tools/list cursor, closed session during a call (DELETE / trim / revoke), `reason: null`, lone-surrogate ids; all 5 fail on the pre-fix code; tests/panic_hook.rs), `node scripts/spike-mcp-wire.mjs --protocol` 57 / 57 same, `--cli` 30 / 30 PASS (real claude 2.1.319 and codex 0.162.1, scratch config dirs), cargo test --workspace (0 failures), cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings and `-p unimux-app --features test-bridge,dev-overrides`, cargo fmt --check, cargo check -p unimux-mcp --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin, `npm run build:check && node scripts/check-mcp-app.mjs` 22/22 (4 new panic checks), smoke:tauri 34/34, typecheck, gen:check, vitest test/mcp (56 passed, 1 skipped).

### M2 (2026-11-07): MCP tool layer, verification and open issues

Verified (Windows 11 26200, 2026-11-07): `cargo test -p unimux-mcp` (14 unit; golden_tools 4: tools/list per role and the 375 instruction variants byte-identical, 26,170 zod rows identical (output or issue list + describeError text), 37 callTool scenarios / 166 steps identical; tools_catalog 8 (mcp-tools-size: token footprint, worker list, portable schemas, advertised = accepted, hints, worker update_agent, zod-schemas cases); tools_http 9 (mcp-tasks-elicit tool cases over HTTP: close_grid confirmation, no elicitation / cancelTasks, agent names and the "which one" elicitation, wait_for as an MCP task past the plain cap; mcp.test connect-and-persist; role-aware instructions; progress every `progress_every`; zod texts over the wire); server 48, protocol 57 / 57 and panic_hook unchanged), `cargo test -p unimux-app` default and `--features dev-overrides,test-bridge` (83, incl. `the_tools_follow_the_app_state_and_the_operations_wait_for_m3`), `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings` for unimux-mcp (with `fake-automation`) and unimux-app (default and `dev-overrides,test-bridge`), `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p unimux-mcp --all-targets --features fake-automation --target aarch64-apple-darwin`, `npm run typecheck`, `node scripts/dump-goldens.mjs --check`, vitest i18n / mcp* / zod-schemas / real-app-harness (86 passed, 1 skipped), `node scripts/porting-count.mjs --check` (+14 rows, 4 partial rows of M1 now ported), `node scripts/verify-mcp-cli.mjs --target=electron` (the TS reference, unchanged behaviour), and on the check build (`npm run build:check`): `node scripts/verify-mcp-cli.mjs` (Tauri half: real claude 2.1.319 and codex-cli 0.162.1 on scratch config dirs, mock models: mcp list connected / revoked 401, claude -p get_context + list_agents (4 agents) + invalid update_task + close_grid elicitation cancelled with nothing closed, codex exec get_context; PASS), `node scripts/check-mcp-app.mjs` 22/22, `npm run smoke:tauri` 34/34 (all offscreen, scratch profile).

1. The automation operations are M3. In the release build every call that reaches the service answers `Not available in this build yet: the automation operation <op> (M3).` (after the zod check, so malformed calls already get the 1.1.4 text). M3 implements `unimux_mcp::Automation` over the StateController (execute parses with `schemas::operation` first, `prompts_shown` is cleared on `runtimeStarted`) and replaces `AppAutomation` in commands/mcp.rs; the event-driven wait_for with the stall / exit events is M4.
2. The check build answers tools over the stand-in of `fake.rs` (feature `fake-automation`, only through `test-bridge`): get_context, list_agents, read_inbox, send_message, create_task, update_task, get_tasks, create_grid, close_grid, wait_for with the 1.1.4 shapes and texts; it edits a copy of the app state, never the app state itself. verify-mcp-cli's Tauri half (and tools_http.rs) therefore prove the tool layer and the wire with the real CLIs, not the real service; M3 / M4 re-run them on it. `MCP_CLI_WAIT` long waits were not run (M4).
3. Correction of the PLAN row: zod 4.6.7 counts string lengths in code points (an emoji is 1), not UTF-16 units; the port does what the reference does (the goldens contain emoji cases at the 128 / 140 limits).
4. Deviations a Rust value cannot avoid: a number beyond the f64 range reaches a schema as null (zod says `received Infinity`); a `clip` (12,000 / 8,000 / 400 / 300 / 200 characters) that cuts inside a surrogate pair ends in U+FFFD where 1.1.4 writes a lone `\udXXX` escape; fields of the wrong type from the service that would throw a TypeError in 1.1.4 (a null `blockedReason` in a wait_for row, a non-array `files`) are passed through instead of failing the call (the 1.1.4 service never returns them).
5. macOS: compile-checked only.

### Review fixes: M2 (2026-11-07)

Both confirmed findings of the M2 review (2 minor) are fixed, none skipped. Branch `tauri-pkg-M2-fixes` (on top of `tauri-pkg-M2`, which another worktree holds checked out).

1. verify-mcp-cli no longer leaves the check build running when it fails to start: `tauriHarness` wraps everything after `spawnApp` (waiting for the window, the CDP socket, the MCP server start) and on a failure closes the socket, runs `killAppTreeAndWait(child)` and rethrows; the harness is now created inside the outer try (`h?.stop()` in the finally), so the scratch dir is removed on every path; the Electron half stops its in-process server when the side grid cannot be created.
2. Which automation calls get the cancellation is now covered. The golden recorder gives callTool a live signal (an AbortController, as the MCP server does), so mcp-calls.jsonl records `signal: true` for the 31 calls 1.1.4 passes it to (wait_for, the task-mode long wait's slices, the legacy names) and `false` for the other 93; golden_tools.rs records `cancel.is_some()` and compares it instead of dropping the field. tools_http.rs wraps the stand-in service to record how each service wait_for ends and adds 3 tests: a revoke ends a waiting plain wait_for, a DELETE of the session ends its waiting wait_for, and tasks/cancel or a revoke end a task-mode long wait (no later slice), each in the service with `{"event":"cancelled"}` within 5 s instead of at its 60 / 170 s timeout. Mutation check: passing `cancel: false` for wait_for in host.rs fails call_tool_over_a_scripted_service (7 mismatches) and all 3 new tests.

Verified after the fixes: see the M2 row.

### Review fixes: S2 (2026-11-07)

All 6 confirmed findings of the S2 review are fixed (the two `\d` findings were the same defect); none was skipped. 1.1.4's answers for the new texts are in test/golden/ssh-keys.json (scripts/golden-ssh-keys.mjs under Electron 45, now 160 text variants x no / the right / a wrong / an empty passphrase); the three traditional DSA PEM files are in test/golden/ssh-keys (`openssl dsa` from the throwaway corpus key pem-dsa1024.key), the OpenSSH variants are built by the script from corpus keys.

| finding | fix | checked by |
|---|---|---|
| major: a traditional PEM DSA key (`BEGIN DSA PRIVATE KEY`) gave notAKey, an encrypted one badPassphrase with the right passphrase; ssh2 reads DSA itself and returns an ssh-dss key (unsupportedType) | `old_private` runs ssh2's asn1 0.2.6 reads on the DSA DER (readSequence, readInt, readString(Integer) for p, q, g, y; JS 32-bit lengths, no SEQUENCE length or tag check) and returns `Loaded::OtherPrivate` (keys.ts: unsupportedType); the DER of a DSA key is no longer cut after its SEQUENCE | golden: plain, AES-128-CBC and DES-EDE3-CBC DSA PEM, one without y, one of bare INTEGERs (all 4 passphrase inputs equal); unit test `dsa_keys_are_checked_with_the_asn1_reads_of_ssh2` (each case checked against asn1's Ber.Reader in Node) |
| minor: OpenSSH security key files (sk-ssh-ed25519, sk-ecdsa-sha2-nistp256) gave unsupportedType; ssh2 errors on any type outside its switch (notAKey / needsPassphrase / badPassphrase) | key types outside ssh2's set (ssh-rsa, ssh-dss, ssh-ed25519, ecdsa-sha2-nistp256/384/521) are `Fail::Other` after russh reads the file, and in the own container walk | golden: sk-ssh-ed25519 plain and encrypted, sk-ecdsa-sha2-nistp256, a 2-key file whose second key is a security key |
| minor (2 findings, one defect): `js_regex` left `\d` as the regex crate's Unicode digit class, so PPK v2 line counts in non-ASCII digits loaded | `\d` / `\D` become `[0-9]` / `[^0-9]` (`0-9` inside a class) | unit test `js_regex_digits_are_ascii_only`; golden: Public-Lines U+0666, Private-Lines in fullwidth digits, encrypted PPK with U+0666 |
| minor: OpenSSH files with a key count other than 1 differed (ssh-key reads one-key files only; ssh2 returns the first key, or no key for 0) | a key count other than 1 goes through `openssh_container`: ssh2's header rules in its order, bcrypt and the cipher through ssh-key's `Kdf` / `Cipher`, every public and private entry walked by ssh2's layout, ssh2's padding rule (any length); count 0 gives `Loaded::Nothing` (notAKey), otherwise the first private entry is read by ssh-key's `KeypairData` (no public / private match check, like ssh2). One-key files keep the russh path the corpus proved | golden: count 0 plain / encrypted, count 2 plain / encrypted, ecdsa then ed25519, public parts of another key, bad and 40-byte padding, a count of 2 with one public entry, two rebuilt one-key controls |
| minor: `is_encrypted` decoded the whole OpenSSH body (an unencrypted key's private part) into a plain Vec | wrapped in `Zeroizing` | code review |

### Open issues: S2 (2026-11-07)

Verification: `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy -p unimux-ssh --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p unimux-ssh` (20 unit, 15 in tests/keys.rs, 3 in tests/sp7_keys.rs), `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `node scripts/golden-ssh-keys.mjs --check`, `node scripts/sp7/golden-keys.mjs --check`, `node scripts/porting-count.mjs --check`, `npm run typecheck`. SP7 open issue 2 (port, anchoring) and 5 (dependencies) are closed by S2: tests/sp7_keys.rs now checks the crate's `parse_private_key` and keeps only the russh-alone GAPS / EXTRA lists.

1. `ssh-keygen -y` ran only with the Windows OpenSSH client (`C:\Windows\System32\OpenSSH\ssh-keygen.exe`); on a machine without ssh-keygen the test is skipped unless `UNIMUX_REQUIRE_SSH_KEYGEN=1` (CI should set it on the Linux / macOS runners). macOS not run (no Mac); the code is platform-neutral.
2. scripts/golden-ssh-keys.mjs and scripts/sp7/golden-keys.mjs need Electron (BoringSSL decides what ssh2 reads) and are not part of `npm run gen:check`; they are run by hand with `--check`.
3. Deliberate, invisible differences from ssh2's generator: the checkint of an unencrypted RSA key is ssh-key's (derived from the key, random for the other types and every encrypted key), and ECDSA private scalars are written as OpenSSH mpints (ssh2 1.17 prefixes a zero byte, the cause of its unreadable keys about 1 in 200 times); both files read back in 1.1.4's parser and in `ssh-keygen -y`.
4. A JS-string case the port cannot hold: a lone UTF-16 surrogate in a pasted text (Rust strings are UTF-8; the IPC layer turns it into U+FFFD before S2 sees it, like `js_json::parse`). The only place 1.1.4 produces one, metaOf's 200-unit cut, is ported (U+FFFD).
5. The IPC handlers (`ssh:keys:*` draftFromText / draftFromFile / unlockDraft / pickKeyFile, generate with the vault) are S4; they call `KeyDrafts`, `generate_key` and the vault (S1).

### U2 open issues (2026-11-06)

1. **VM / real-hardware acceptance not run** (no Windows VM image): a per-machine install whose UAC consent shows in front with no taskbar click, and a real full-screen app staying in front and full screen while an update starts. The harness proves the parts it can (never foreground, never topmost, shown inactive, ShellExecuteExW with the window as owner, `runas` decision unit-tested with an icacls deny ACL). Goes with U4 / the VM.
2. The real productName `unimux` installer (with its Apps & features entry) was not run on this machine on purpose; the payload is the stock Tauri template with the registry writes commented out. U4 / VM.
3. `work/shown` later than 1.1.4's 4 s SHOWN_WAIT still happens under load (5.2-5.6 s in 2 of about 12 case runs; the WebView2 start). The update never depends on it; the harness reports it instead of failing. If it matters, U1 can wait longer before the wind-down.
4. The page does not always render `preparing` (it lasts about 1.5 s and WebView2 may take longer to come up; the page then starts at the current stage, as `init` returns the last state); a hidden window may batch `launching` and `done`. updater.log always lists all five stages in order; the never-hidden webview case shows installing, launching and done on the page.
5. macOS: `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p unimux-core -p unimux-platform -p unimux-update` passes; `-p unimux-app` cannot be checked here (objc2-exception-helper's build script needs an Apple C toolchain), so the window mode is Windows-tested only (U5 / R3, CI on macos-latest).
6. Session end: tao exits the process on WM_ENDSESSION, so the window never holds a logout; a supervised install interrupted by a logout is not resumed (as in 1.1.4).
7. `verify-update-window.mjs --build`: a failed build exits before its temporary tauri config in %TEMP% is removed (harmless).
