| major: `check_folder` accepted a folder where `EBWebView` cannot be created, and an existing unwritable `EBWebView` | `check_folder` creates `EBWebView` when absent (removes it again when it made it) and creates and removes a probe file inside it; refusals read "EBWebView cannot be created (...)" / "EBWebView not writable (...)" | 2 new unit tests with real ACLs (icacls deny `*S-1-1-0:(W)` on EBWebView, deny `(AD)` on the folder): both refused, accepted again once the deny is removed, nothing left behind; no WebView2 started on them |
| minor (twice): the 1.1.4 config golden claimed "order" but its checks ignored key order; the struct wrote `fromVersion` before `version` | `UpdateSplashConfig` fields in 1.1.4's order (`version, fromVersion, ...`); the golden compares the key order at every level and the compact JSON byte for byte | `a_config_written_by_1_1_4_reads_unchanged` (fails with the old field order); the unit test of the key order updated |
| minor (twice): the window's `%TEMP%\unimux-updater\webview2-<ms>` fallback was never swept | `unimux_update::window::temp_webview_root()` is the one source of that path (used by `choose_webview_dir`); `stage_runtime` and `prestage` sweep it as well as the updater root (once when both are the same folder), same older-than-a-day rule | `the_temp_fallback_folders_of_killed_windows_are_swept_too` (stale removed in both roots, fresh kept) |

Verification (machine at 100 percent CPU from other agents): cargo fmt --check clean; clippy -D warnings (workspace all targets, default and all features; aarch64-apple-darwin core / platform / update) clean; `cargo test -p unimux-platform -p unimux-update` green; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` (features) 256 passed + load-flaky failures only in untouched crates (exec `output_over_the_cap_is_truncated_and_the_tree_ends`, pty conpty timing, state broadcast timer; pty and state green on a serial rerun, exec's 20 s cmd loop keeps timing out under this load); npm run typecheck and gen:check clean; npm test 3,951 passed + `cli-install-shared` / `context-usage` timeouts, both files green on rerun. `node scripts/verify-update-window.mjs --build`: build and the webview case passed, main / guard / failed lost the old app's DevTools connection under load in that run. Reruns: `--case=main` 34/34; `--case=guard,failed,webview` 63/64 (webview "window survived the old app exiting": the process snapshot sampler missed the window, updater.log shows it ran to `done`); `--case=webview` 30/30 on its next run; `--prestage --case=main` 35/35 on its third run (the first two lost process snapshots / the DevTools connection, same load pattern). The main app's `%TEMP%` fallback and the window's fallback pass with the new EBWebView probe in the webview case.

### U5 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. **No Mac**: nothing of U5 ran on macOS. The real `ditto` / `xattr` / `plutil` / `codesign` / `open` calls, a real `.app` swap under `/Applications`, Gatekeeper / quarantine after the swap, App Management (macOS 13+) and translocation are unverified; they go to R3 (manual, arm64 + x64) and CI on macos-latest. What ran instead: the engine and swap tests on Windows and on Linux (Docker `rust:1.99-slim`, the `cfg(unix)` paths: permissions, exec bits, owner uid, EROFS; 56 + 3 golden passed), and the update window's swap path on Windows on a scratch bundle (`verify-update-window.mjs --case=swap,rollback`, the executable started directly instead of `open`).
2. **aarch64-apple-darwin check no longer possible here for `unimux-update`**: U5 adds `unimux-net` (the B3 HTTP client, rustls + ring), whose `ring` build script needs a C compiler for the Apple target (none on this machine). U2 open issue 5 listed `-p unimux-update` as checkable; now only `unimux-core` / `unimux-platform` are. macOS CI compiles it.
3. **U1 wiring left to U1**: the engine is a library (`MacUpdater::check` / `download` / `install` / `abandon_install`); U1 calls them from its coordinator (newest target wins, the 30 min schedule, the gate), passes `MacInstall::Ready(plan)` as `HandOver::swap`, maps `MacInstall::Refused(reason)` to the download-page offer. The start step already runs where 1.1.4 had it (setup, before the gate's place, only when updates are active): a `Failed` outcome is written to `update-gate.json` at once (1.1.4's `recordUpdateFailure` minus the in-memory block), so the gate's 6 h cooldown applies on that start; U1 reads `mac::outcome_at_start()` for its in-memory block of that version and the status bar text (`mac::messages::ROLLED_BACK`). `show_main` only writes `updates/mac/ready` (markReady), also only when updates are active.
4. The swap is instant, so the window goes closing -> launching without an `installing` stage (updater.log: preparing > closing > launching > done). As in 1.1.4 (no window at all there), not a parity issue; the progress model is unchanged.
5. `launch_new` / the rollback's restart run inside the 250 ms supervision loop; a slow start (Windows: 2-4.5 s for a never-seen exe under the antivirus) freezes the bar for that long. `open` on macOS returns at once.
6. The real-app harness sometimes loses the old app's DevTools page while it boots under load ("DevTools connection closed" before the hand-over, 0 checks); the case then reports nothing. Same load pattern as the U2 notes; a rerun passes.
7. The `rolled-back` flag is written with the target version as in 1.1.4; the downloaded archive and the extraction folder stay after a rollback and are removed by `finish_previous` (`clean_staging_of`) on the next start.

### Review fixes: U5 (2026-11-07)

All 6 confirmed findings of the U5 review (all minor) are fixed; none was skipped. Branch: `tauri-pkg-U5` was checked out by the original U5 worktree, so the fixes are committed on `tauri-pkg-U5-fixes` (= `tauri-pkg-U5` + the fix commit; fast-forward `tauri-pkg-U5` to it).

| finding | fix | checked by |
|---|---|---|
| the macOS start bookkeeping (`finish_previous`, `mark_ready`) ran in every build, so a dev build on the real profile consumed the installed app's `rolled-back` / `pending.json` and removed its `.old-*` backup | both run only when updates are active: `startup::updates_active` = `unimux_core::PACKAGED` or a dev feed (`MC_UPDATE_FEED`), 1.1.4's `updatesActive()`; `mac::previous_update_at_start(.., updates_active, ..)` returns `None` and touches nothing otherwise | `the_start_step_touches_nothing_unless_updates_are_active_and_records_a_failure_for_the_gate` (flag, marker stay; no gate record) |
| `finish_previous` ran in `show_main`, after the gate, and a failure only reached crash.log while its evidence was consumed | `startup::previous_mac_update` runs in setup before the gate's place (1.1.4: before `runUpdateGate`); a `Failed` outcome is written to `update-gate.json` (`{version, at}`, 1.1.4's `writeGateRecord`) right away and kept for U1 (`mac::outcome_at_start()`); `show_main` keeps only `signal_ready` | same test (record keys in order, version, time; `outcome_at_start`); the macOS-only call sites compile-checked on Windows with their cfg switched temporarily (clippy -D warnings clean), as `-p unimux-app` cannot be checked for aarch64-apple-darwin here |
| a cancel was not checked during staging (unpack, copy next to the app, deep codesign) | `stage_archive_until(version, archive, cancelled)`; `download` passes the token; checks after the unpack and before the install target check / `.new-*` removal / copy, as the TS `stop()` calls; `stage_archive` keeps its signature | `a_cancel_during_staging_stops_after_the_unpack_and_before_the_copy` (no tool call after a cancel in the unpack; no copy, no `--deep`, an older `.new-*` untouched after a cancel in the quick check) |
| "did not report ready within N seconds" was logged when the window ended in the lingering hand-off tick | `ready_timed_out` only when `elapsed > SUPERVISE_MAX_MS`; otherwise `swap::handed_off` logs "the window ends after starting the new app; its markReady removes the previous version at ..." | swap unit test of both lines; real-app swap case asserts no "did not report ready" in `updates/mac/update.log` |
| no test of the 15 min total download cap | test `Env` gets `total`; a server that trickles 512 B every 100 ms (stall 2 s, total 500 ms) | `the_total_download_time_is_capped_even_when_bytes_keep_coming` (STALLED in 0.45-1.9 s, no `.part` left) |
| non-macOS `RealSwapOps::open` started the first file `read_dir` listed in `Contents/MacOS` | `SwapOps::open(bundle, executable)`; non-macOS starts `<bundle>/Contents/MacOS/<plan.executable>` or fails with NotFound; macOS `open` unchanged | `the_real_ops_start_the_executable_the_plan_names_not_the_first_file`; verify-update-window.mjs puts `aaa-sidecar.exe` (not an executable, listed first) in both scratch bundles, so the swap and rollback cases fail if anything but `unimux.exe` is started |

Verification: cargo fmt; clippy -D warnings `-p unimux-app -p unimux-update --all-targets` default and with `dev-overrides,test-bridge` clean; the macOS-only call sites (main.rs setup, startup.rs, windows.rs show_main) compiled and clippy-clean on Windows with their `target_os = "macos"` cfg switched to `windows` for that check only (files restored afterwards); `cargo test -p unimux-update` 60 unit + 3 golden on Windows; on Linux (Docker `rust:1.99-slim`, read-only mount copied into the container, removed with --rm) the 60 unit tests pass (the 3 golden tests need the repo's `test/` folder, which the copy of `src-tauri` did not include); `cargo test -p unimux-app --features dev-overrides,test-bridge` 82 passed; `npm run typecheck` clean; `node scripts/verify-update-window.mjs --build` **137/137** in one run (main 31, guard 18, failed 16, webview 27, swap 23, rollback 19, plus 3 global). Not covered by a real-app run: the lingering hand-off tick of finding 4 (no harness case closes the card before the old app exits); the branch is covered by the swap unit test of both log lines.

### X1: real-app scripts (2026-11-07)

Harness: `scripts/real-app.mjs` (target = the Tauri check build; `MC_APP_TARGET=electron` or `--target=electron` = Electron's out/, the 1.1.4 reference, until R1), `scripts/real-app-owners.mjs` (channel -> package), `scripts/real-app-run.mjs` (runs them all; `--write-progress` rewrites the table below). A failed script prints `BLOCKED by <package> (<commands>)` and exits with 2 only for what it exercised itself (`classifyRun` in real-app-owners.mjs): a test-bridge op a later package adds, a not-yet command outside the startup baseline (the not-yet calls of a bare launch, scripts/real-app-baseline.mjs, cached per binary in artifacts/real-app/baseline-tauri.json; the runner takes it once before the sweep), or a baseline command of a package the script tests (`SCRIPT_PACKAGES`). Any other failure is a `fail` (exit 1) and lists the boot-time calls as "not-yet seen", so a regression in a landed package is never hidden as blocked. Measurements (perf-*, bench-*, measure-startup) that pass are still blocked by every not-yet call (their numbers mean nothing until the app is complete). Re-run after a package lands: `npm run build:check && node scripts/real-app-run.mjs --write-progress`.

#### X1 open issues

1. A "blocked by" list names only what the script exercised beyond the startup baseline plus the baseline packages listed for it in `SCRIPT_PACKAGES`. A boot-time not-yet call can still make a script fail without being its cause (e.g. a script whose UI waits on cli:status but does not list H3): such a run is reported as `fail (not-yet seen: ...)`; add the package to `SCRIPT_PACKAGES` when that is the real reason. A not-yet call the UI makes later than 5 s after a bare launch counts as exercised.
2. Harness contracts later packages must fill (written into their PLAN rows): W4 test-bridge `toasts` / `toastAction` and honouring `MC_TEST_TOASTS=1` (every harness launch sets it: no toast may reach the desktop during a check; `toasts {stub: true}` keeps the toasts since launch, like the Electron target); W7 `taskbar`; P4 `unimux --launch-line` (verify-cli-contract, a release gate, is blocked by P4 until then); M2 the Tauri half of verify-mcp-cli (blocked by M2: it runs the TS reference server in-process today).
3. W6: test-bridge `quit` is `app.exit(0)` (no wind-down); `quitApp` / `app.quit()` of the scripts use it for "a normal quit". W6 decides whether it goes through the wind-down like a user's quit. `requestClose` is `Window::close()` (CloseRequested first), the user's close button.
4. Not ported (Electron-internal, exit with SKIP on the Tauri target, R1 removes or ports them): perf-check.mjs (getAppMetrics budgets per process type), perf-renders.mjs and bench-perf.mjs (main-process IPC hooks), smoke-ssh.mjs (ssh2 under Electron's Node). R2 measures with perf-grid, perf-hidden, perf-latency, perf-profile (process tree of our own pid: unimux.exe + msedgewebview2.exe) and bench-jank. A Tauri version of perf-check's budgets would sit on `processTree()` or G8's monitor:sample.
5. smoke-start on Tauri waits for `app-ready` + `page-loaded`; P3 should add a pty mark to startup.log and smoke-start should wait for it (1.1.4 waited for `pty-host-started`). It refuses a non-check exe without `--ci` (a release exe ignores MC_DATA_DIR); `npm run test:start` stays on the Electron package until U4.
6. bench-jank `--gpu-cache` copies WebView2's caches from `data\webview2\EBWebView` (an assumption, not measured yet).
7. verify-release-build.mjs only runs in release.yml (it reads a CI run of a tag); the runner reports it as skip.
8. The real-app sweep is not in CI (it needs a desktop session; the Windows runner could run it offscreen): U4 / R2.
9. scripts/release.mjs on this branch builds the check flavour (`npm run build:check`) and then runs the two release gates against that binary (also in `--dry-run`); they are blocked until P4 and the boot-time packages land, so a 1.2.0 release cannot pass before that (intended). main's release.mjs (1.1.x) is unchanged.

10. Electron target, OS notifications: `scripts/electron-test-toasts.cjs` is loaded into Electron's main process with `NODE_OPTIONS=--require` (put back to the script's value before the app starts anything) and swaps `Notification.prototype.show` for a list the first time the app requires `electron`, so no toast of a check reaches the desktop, also one shown before the main inspector is attached (1.1.4's mainInspector stubbed it only after the launch). Before this fix, the first Electron sweep of this session ran verify-auto-switch-ui with the late stub and its account-switch toast may have reached the desktop once.
11. Electron reference failures that are not caused by the migration (the pre-X1 script bodies from b30f0c2 fail the same way on the same build): smoke-automation ("one terminal setting per row"), capture-readme `--verify` (verify-workspace-ui "return from settings to file"), verify-ssh-bar (the uptime tooltip "Czas działania serwera: 14 dni 6 h"), verify-db-redis-ui ("SSH password prompt in the tab"). They need a look before R1 removes the reference; the Tauri versions are blocked by their packages anyway.
12. Load: with other agents' cargo builds at 100 percent CPU, Electron's first `Runtime.enable` sometimes never answered. ssh-ui-lib's launchApp now reconnects to the page up to 3 times (20 s each) and its CDP timeout is 45 s (`MC_CDP_TIMEOUT_MS`); the scratch folder removal at the end of 15 verify scripts goes through `removeScratch` (logs, never fails a passed check). Timing checks failed once under that load and passed on the rerun: verify-team-start (crashed worker restart), verify-agent-prompt (queue), verify-auto-switch-ui (slider saved mid-drag). smoke-automation and capture-readme have their own CDP clients and keep a 10 / 15 s timeout.
13. verify-tauri-normalize (F8) had a race: the renderer edits the state (the `shell` of started terminals, the strip order) before the peek, so revision > 0. Fixed for good in the review: the check build keeps the revision-0 snapshot (test-bridge `loaded`) and both the first-load and the restart checks compare it exactly; 3 runs passed.
14. smoke-start on the Electron target needs the packaged app of `npm run dist:win`; the runner reports it as skip without it.
15. About 50 `_tmp-ssh-*` scratch folders from earlier runs (X1 runs of 2026-11-07 included) are still in D:\Projekty; launchApp no longer leaves new ones on a failed launch. They were not removed here (other agents may be using some); delete them when no check runs.

### W7 (2026-11-07): verification and open issues

Verified (Windows 11 26200, machine shared with other agents' builds): cargo fmt; clippy -D warnings for unimux-app, unimux-platform, unimux-core (default and `--features unimux-app/dev-overrides,unimux-app/test-bridge`); cargo test of the three crates with the features (unimux-app 101 + new W7 tests, unimux-platform 65 incl. `a_pass_reads_its_deps_without_holding_the_lock`, unimux-core 57 + golden 2); `node scripts/verify-tauri-cells.mjs` (new, 21 checks: gates open, startup.log marks, hooks wired, first renderer mark wins, forgetCell keeps the nonce of a running cell, a closed team cell loses its MCP token, taskbar stub records no overlay / progress, Windows view '', cell:runtime answered, reportError in crash.log and limited to 20 a minute, app:notice toast, quit, real profile untouched); verify-tauri-hooks all passed; verify-tauri-lifecycle 33/33 (3 runs; two earlier runs during a concurrent cargo build failed the "relaunch while quitting" step on connectPage timeouts, the base binary passed 1/1 in the same period); vitest real-app-harness / ipc-guard / startup-gates / taskbar; porting-count --check 324 / 2,143 (+3 startup-gates rows); on the final check build: verify-tauri-cells all passed, smoke-tauri 34/34, verify-tauri-os --quick all passed, `npm run ipc:report` 60 / 250 methods on 60 / 220 channels (app 5/5, pty 1/5 = cell:runtime), typecheck, `npm test` 266 files 4,014 passed, gen-ipc-contract / gen-messages --check up to date. `node scripts/verify-caption.mjs`: 107/108, the caption probe reports `layered: true` (exStyle 0x280000) at every scale and the new "hit-test child is layered" check passes; the one failure, "200% maximized: the top right pixel is the close button" (hits the WebView2 `Intermediate D3D Window`, the window maximized onto the real monitor at [-8,-8]), happens the same with the base binary of fca544d (`--scale=200`: 38/42 on the base, 38/41 on W7, both also failing "system menu offers Restore" in the solo run), so it is not caused by W7. First attempt without `SetLayeredWindowAttributes`: the layered child was not hit-testable (91/108), fixed.


Review fixes (2026-11-07, `tauri-pkg-W7-fix`): new test `overlapping_passes_finish_in_the_order_they_read` (fails without the pass mutex: the stale pass wrote last), `the_account_gates_open_in_order` checks `accounts-recovered` before `accounts-migrated` and its presence without a recovery step, `the_taskbar_follows_reports_reloads_removals_and_mutes` now uses the app's `taskbar_cells` and turns the badge setting off / on and a profile into the shell and back; duplicate `cell:runtime` assertion removed. Verified: cargo fmt; clippy -D warnings (unimux-app, unimux-platform, default and with the features); cargo test unimux-app 101, unimux-platform 66, unimux-core 57 + 2; vitest taskbar / real-app-harness; `npm run build:check`, then verify-tauri-cells all passed (24 checks, incl. `accounts-recovered` in startup.log, `w7-bogus` dropped, counts {attention 1, working 0}, then a non-running agent ignored {attention 0, working 1}), smoke-tauri 34/34.

Open issues:

- W7a. **macOS not run** (no Mac): the Dock badge path (`set_badge_label`) and reapply on show are compile-checked with the rest of unimux-app only (unimux-app cannot be cross-checked here).
- W7b. Slots filled by later packages: `alive` (P3 `set_alive` / `clear_alive`; until then only the test bridge sets it, so the taskbar count and the runtime observers see no running cell in a real run), `startup_steps.set_cell_file_prune` (H2; no `cell-files-pruned` mark until then), `set_account_recovery` / `set_account_migration` / `on_accounts_ready` (A1 / A2 / A5; the account gates open at once now), `on_projects_settled` (M3 cancelOrphans), `on_forget_unless_alive` for the context tracker (H4), cell files (H2), automation (M3), `on_forget` for verifiedCodex (H4 / P3), `on_state_changed` for ssh / db / limit alerts / usage reschedule (S3, D1, A5), `on_runtime` for hookWatch (H2), cliEnd (P3 / M3), automation statuses (M3), `gates.before_spawn` in cell:spawn (P4), `accounts_ready.wait()` in the account / auth / usage channels (A1 / A2 / A4 / A5), `app_notice` for the HTTP hook fallback (H2).
- W7c. `cell:runtime` moved from P3 to W7 (owners map); P3 must not register it again, it plugs in through `on_runtime` and `set_alive`.
- W7d. Projects are compared by value (`!=` on the JSON) where 1.1.4 compared references; the result is the same except that an edit which rebuilds identical projects no longer queues cancelOrphans (nothing could be orphaned by it).
- W7e. The manifest's Windows 10/11 entry is app-wide (also the update window mode of the same exe). Snap layouts flyout with the now layered child still needs a person or a VM (W1 open issue 1).
- W7f. verify-caption at 200 percent (full run and `--scale=200`, base and W7 alike) maximizes the window onto the real monitor and, in the solo run, finds the detached window on screen ("not off-screen ([104,104,...])"): a window of the check briefly appears on the desktop. Pre-existing in W1's script, reported here because it breaks the "nothing on the desktop" rule; W1 should keep the maximized and detached cases off screen.
- W7g. verify-notify-actions (the taskbar contract's user) stays blocked by P3 / P4 (needs a running agent terminal); the `taskbar` op is covered by verify-tauri-cells meanwhile.

Integration fixes (fix: commits):

1. 47801df: detached windows (W5) get the W2 macOS chrome (`WindowKind::Detached`), the W3 file-drop paths (`file_drop::install`), the W6 renderer watch (`lifecycle::watch_renderer`; they always reload) and the main window's WebView2 folder (`windows::webview_dir`, so U2's %TEMP% fallback cannot give two windows different folders, which WebView2 refuses). W6's wind-down sets `detached.set_quitting(true)` in STOP_SERVICES and runs `close_all` in CLOSE_DETACHED.
2. 12acb6a: the H1 hook server stops in the wind-down's HOOKS phase (and again in RunEvent::Exit for a forced exit); the M1 merge registers the MCP server's stop in the MCP phase the same way. RunEvent::Exit also runs W4's `os::on_exit` (copied secret) and H5's `agents::on_exit` (conversation index save) before W6's `after_exit`; H3's `app_started` (CLI scan) runs after the main window.
3. 490ccbd: `dump-goldens.mjs` had lost the update / detached / git / files / hook goldens in the H5 merge resolution; put back (no golden file changed).
4. 5b63d7d: one `regex` entry in the workspace manifest (M1, G1 and S2 all added it), lock regenerated.
5. 4f0a816, 6f9076e: verify-safestorage (B2) keeps its own 1.1.4 main inspector and pins `MC_APP_TARGET=electron` for its 1.1.4 side (X1 removed `mainInspector` from ssh-ui-lib and made the target global); ssh-ui-lib's `launchApp` takes `options.onSpawn`; `real-app-run.mjs` lists the wave 1 acceptance scripts (verify-tauri-convsearch / -detached / -files / -hooks / -lifecycle / -os / -w3, verify-cli-detect, verify-safestorage, verify-update-window), which `test/real-app-harness.test.ts` requires.

### Merged into `tauri-1.2.0` (with `--no-ff`)

No conflicts: W1 already carried 14cd4bb, and the open-path branch touches only `unimux-platform` (`shell.rs`, `Cargo.toml`) and a doc comment in `commands/os.rs`.

Integration fixes (fix: commits):

1. 1e4df62: `verify-tauri-lifecycle.mjs` expected W6's four wind-down steps only; wave 1 added W5's `detached: quitting` / `close detached windows` and the M1 / H1 `mcp stop` / `hooks stop` steps (integration fixes 1 and 2 of wave 1). The check now expects the full plan in phase order and passes 33/33 (W1 reported 32/33 for this).
2. 7ef45ca: `unimux-ipc` `embedded_contract_has_the_known_size` and `fallbacks_answer_but_count_as_not_implemented` still counted 215 channels; W1's `window:*` caption members make the contract 220 channels (178 invoke + 42 send), 250 methods + 3 sync members, 45 events.

### Open-path hardening (`tauri-fix-openpath`)

Five gaps of the second review of W4's openPath, all in `unimux-platform/src/shell.rs` (`launches_program`, the one rule used by the `shell:openPath` decision in `commands/os.rs`, the last check before ShellExecute / `open` and the openInEditor fallback):

1. Windows asks `AssocIsDangerous` on top of the fixed list and PATHEXT; the fixed list is a minimum the registry cannot lower (`win::assoc_is_dangerous`).
2. The Windows refusal list adds msu, ppkg, wsb, jnlp, the Access types (mdb, mde, accdb, accde, accdr, accda, accdu, ade, adp, mda, mam, maf, maq, mar, mat, maw), iso, img, vhd, vhdx and cab; the refusal test names each, plus the appx / msix types, appinstaller, library-ms, search-ms and searchconnector-ms.
3. macOS reveals instead of opening a regular file with any execute bit, a file starting with `#!` or a Mach-O / universal-binary header, a folder holding `Contents/Info.plist`, and anything that is neither a regular file nor a folder (a FIFO is never read). The extension list adds xpc, appex, framework, service, systemextension, mdimporter, wdgt, dext, osax, component and dmg.
4. Links: the path the renderer sends and its resolved target are both judged; either being a program is enough to reveal.
5. `objc2-foundation` gets the `NSBundle` feature (unimux-platform did not build alone for aarch64-apple-darwin, `mac_notify.rs` imports it).

Tests: `AssocIsDangerous` flags `.exe` and not `.txt` / `.png`, and refuses types this machine flags outside both lists (`.xnk`, `.grp`, `.mcf`, `.ops`, `.prg`, `.ksh`, `.fxp`); header and content checks on temp files run on every OS; the Unix-only test (chmod 755 file, renamed Mach-O, bundles, symlinks to programs) and the Windows symlink test (skips without the symlink privilege, skipped here) are not run.

## Wave 2 packages: open issues

### P3 open issues (2026-11-07, fast mode)

Built and unit-tested only (cargo fmt, clippy -D warnings for unimux-pty and unimux-app with and without the check features, cargo test of both crates; tests/service.rs runs on real ConPTY terminals). Not run: the real-app checks of the acceptance list (a pwsh cell types and resizes, renderer reload with replay, a detached cell keeps its output, the injected panic flow in the real app), verify-tauri-lifecycle (its expected wind-down plan now has `terminals shutdown` in the hosts phase), macOS (compile-check only through CI).

1. `cell:spawn` stays MC_NOT_YET until P4; P4 builds the SpawnRequest and calls `commands::pty::service().spawn(req, false)` after `ctx.cells.set_alive` / `hooks.queue_prompt`, compares `host().kill_count(id)` before and after its awaits, checks `service().is_fatal()` and sets `host().set_verified_codex`.
2. Hooks for later packages (registered on `commands::pty::host()`): `on_prefix("install:")` (H6: tap, interrupt, exit of the install window), `on_prefix("auth:")` (A4), `intercept` + `on_killed` (P5 SSH sessions), `on_cell` (M3 automation statuses and noteExit), `on_crashed` (A4 login.failActive, H6 install.failActive, P5 ssh.hostCrashed, the SSH app-start claim). Until they register, `install:` / `auth:` terminals are just kept out of the cells.
3. A crash is a panic on the service's actor thread or in the output path on a reader thread (ARCHITECTURE section 3). A panic while a renderer message of the pty port is handled is logged (rate-limited) and the terminals keep running, like an uncaught exception in the 1.1.4 host. The crash.log line reads `pty host exited (panic)` plus the panic text (1.1.4: the exit code and the stderr tail).
4. Tap output is the raw read of the terminal (before the output engine coalesces it), decoded as UTF-8 across reads; 1.1.4 tapped after node-pty's decoder. H6's marker parsing should not notice.
5. The check build's test bridge `pty spawn` still spawns through the engine directly (synchronous pid for the scripts) and reports the spawn to the bookkeeping itself; `pty kill` counts as a user kill.
6. `PtyHost` and the service are process-wide statics like P2's engine: in the unit tests every `register` points the host at the newest context.
7. test/pty-host-client.test.ts imports the client dynamically, so porting-count does not see it as main-side and its cases have no PORTING rows; they are covered by unimux-pty tests/service.rs (restart queue, fatal, restart after fatal) and supervisor::tests (queue bound, stop). The stdio case is obsolete (no host process).
8. One `cargo test -p unimux-pty -p unimux-app` run on the loaded machine: unimux-app 117/117, unimux-pty tests/service.rs 8/8, engine 7/7, manager 16/16, plane 2/2; two load-sensitive failures in code P3 did not change: `batch::tests::bulk_after_a_quiet_window_leaves_at_once_then_waits_for_the_window` (leading edge took 1.49 s, limit 100 ms) and tests/conpty.rs `output_after_exit_is_delivered_before_the_exit` (no exit seen in round 1). Not rerun (fast mode).

### P2: verification, measurements and open issues (2026-11-07)

Branch `tauri-pkg-P2` (from `tauri-1.2.0` at fca544d). Machine: Windows 11 26200, WebView2 156, shared with about 14 other agents' builds (CPU at 100 percent for most of the runs).

#### Verified

- cargo fmt --all --check; clippy -D warnings (all targets) for unimux-pty, unimux-ipc, unimux-app, default and `dev-overrides,test-bridge`; unimux-pty + unimux-ipc for `aarch64-apple-darwin` (unimux-app cannot be cross-checked here: objc2-exception-helper needs a C compiler for the target).
- cargo test -p unimux-pty: lib 86 (the ports of test/flow.test.ts, output-channel.test.ts, pty-flow.test.ts, replay-sizes.test.ts and the early-size half of host-router.test.ts on the fake clock, plus batch, routes, timers, frame), tests/engine.rs 5 on real ConPTY (replay at recorded sizes incl. an early size, host-side DA1 at once without a window, FlowGate pause / resume through byte acks with the unacked bytes bounded and the exit frame after the output, routing to a detached window and refusing messages from the old owner, in-flight cap with a slow page, reload replaces the window and flow control follows the main window), P1 suites conpty 14, manager 14, plane 2. unimux-ipc 31, unimux-app 82 (with features).
- vitest test/pty-frames.test.ts 13 (decoder against frame.rs's own test vectors, characters split across reads and messages, replay byte offsets to character offsets, exit flush, JSON frames, the bridge port: queue until TerminalManager listens, release acks, page-side in-flight count, shared-buffer copy and release, one pull in flight); npm run typecheck; test/real-app-harness lists verify-tauri-pty.mjs.
- Real app (`node scripts/verify-tauri-pty.mjs`, check build, offscreen, scratch data; terminals started through the test bridge `pty` op because `cell:spawn` is P3 / P4):
  - replay: a cmd cell at 75 cols, narrowed to 32 and widened to 81 (3 recorded sizes, at bytes 0 / 544 / 744); after `Page.reload` the replay draws the same 6 marker lines; transport `sharedBuffer`; the host sent the replay tails.
  - grid (20 cmd cells, unimux-pty-flood 1 MB/s each, 15 s): every run met the P2 bounds: shared buffers posted and not released counted page side at most 1, host side at most 3 (cap 2, 3 with an echo), unacked bytes per terminal at most 1,051,199 (FlowGate 1 MiB + one flush; typical 75 to 430 KB).

#### Measurements (interleaved with 1.1.4 = the Electron build of this tree, `scripts/sp5/electron-grid.mjs`)

Echo, keystroke -> drawn, median / p95 ms (perf-latency.mjs probe, cmd cell):

| run | Tauri P2 spaced | Tauri P2 burst | 1.1.4 spaced | 1.1.4 burst |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| idle, pair 1 | 3.7 / 4.7 | 3.6 / 8.3 | 6.5 / 44.1 | 6.7 / 15.8 |
| idle, pair 2 | 5.9 / 8.3 | 6.8 / 8.6 | 6.4 / 23.2 | 6.6 / 23.1 |
| idle (later, single) | 2.5 / 4.6 | 2.0 / 7.3 | | |
| 19 cells x 1 MB/s, pair 1 | 7.7 / 24.6 | 8.0 / 27.0 | 7.7 / 22.3 | 8.7 / 15.9 |
| 19 cells x 1 MB/s, pair 2 | 6.1 / 16.5 | 7.2 / 20.2 | 7.3 / 46.9 | 6.9 / 17.2 |
| 19 cells, after the echo slot, pair 3 | 11.9 / 36.2 | 7.5 / 24.0 | 7.1 / 66.5 | 20.6 / 309.5 |
| 19 cells, pair 4 (machine saturated) | 14.7 / 117.9 | 122.7 / 623.1 | 10.6 / 167.5 | 36.9 / 146.3 |
| 19 cells, single runs | 12.2 / 23.1, 12.9 / 22.2, 10.9 / 560.7 | 10.9 / 20.9, 8.0 / 17.6, 8.1 / 26.0 | (two 1.1.4 runs failed: no echo recognized in 10 rounds) | |

20 cells x 1 MB/s through ConPTY (what reached xterm, fps, p95 / max frame ms, frames over 33 ms):

| app / transport | MB/s into xterm | fps | p95 / max | > 33 ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tauri sharedBuffer | 20.3 / 20.6 / 20.0 / 20.0 | 98 / 42 / 145 / 184 | 29.2 / 116.8, 62.6 / 108.4, 16.6 / 29.3, 12.4 / 20.9 | 58 / 109 / 0 / 0 |
| Tauri channel | 20.0 | 181 | 12.4 / 37.4 | 2 |
| Tauri pull | 5.2 / 20.0 | 171 / 214 | 16.6 / 75.0, 8.3 / 20.9 | 8 / 0 |
| 1.1.4 | 8.7 / 20.9 / 17.0 | 194 / 165 / 144 | 12.5 / 83.3, 16.6 / 108.4, 12.5 / 120.9 | 3 / 12 / 64 |

Reading: idle echo is at or below 1.1.4 (median and p95) in every pair. Under the flood the medians are at 1.1.4's level when the machine is not saturated (pairs 1-2: 0.79x to 1.04x), far from the SP5 spike's 13 to 28 ms; the p95 under load is within noise of 1.1.4 but not provably within 1.1x (burst p95 1.17x and 1.7x worse in pairs 1-2, better in pairs 3-4). Frame times at about 20 MB/s into xterm: Tauri equal or better than 1.1.4 in the runs on a less loaded machine (145 to 214 fps, no frame over 33 ms with the shared buffer), worse in the first two runs at full load. Transport decision (SP5 rule "pull if the shared buffer does not beat it"): pull had the best single grid run (214 fps) but the worst echo under load (15.3 / 30.3 and 12.9 / 24.4 vs 12.9 / 22.2 and 8.0 / 17.6 with the shared buffer, same hour); the shared buffer stays the Windows default, pull is the macOS default and selectable with `MC_PTY_TRANSPORT=pull` for the Mac / rerun comparison.

#### Open issues

1. **SP5 performance targets not proven within 1.1x under load** (see above): every comparison ran on a machine at 100 percent CPU from other agents' builds, where both apps swing by 5x between runs (two 1.1.4 runs did not even echo). Rerun `node scripts/verify-tauri-pty.mjs latency --load` / `grid` against `MC_APP_TARGET=electron node scripts/sp5/electron-grid.mjs 20 1000000 15 [--latency=60]` on an idle machine (or the Windows VM) before calling P2 verified.
2. **Echo slot**: a keystroke's echo may use a third in-flight message (MAX_IN_FLIGHT_ECHO) so it does not wait for a release round trip behind two bulk messages; it did not measurably change the loaded echo (the wait is in the renderer's xterm write queues, as SP5 found). SP5's other idea (smaller unacked window for unfocused terminals) was not tried: 1.1.4 has the same 1 MiB per terminal and the medians are at parity now.
3. **macOS**: the pull transport is the default there but has not run on WKWebView (no Mac); `mc_port_pull` returns raw bytes, which WKWebView's IPC supports in Tauri 2.12.5 according to SP1, unverified. unimux-app was not cross-compiled for aarch64-apple-darwin (needs a cc for the target).
4. **Not in P2** (P3 / P4 / P5): `cell:*` commands, the routing table from the StateController (`route` is called by the test bridge only), tap / interrupt for `install:` / `auth:`, `ptyhost:crashed` / supervision around the engine (the engine's PtyManager is not wrapped in P1's supervised actor yet), a wind-down step that kills the terminals (1.1.4's killAll; process exit closes the pseudoconsoles), sshList / sshStats messages (P5, `post_json` is ready for their answers), exit notifications to main (`PtyEngine::on_exit` is ready).
5. **Units changed by the plan**: replay tail and flow control count bytes (1.1.4: UTF-16 characters), so a replay of mostly non-ASCII output holds fewer characters than in 1.1.4 (512 KiB of UTF-8 instead of 512 Ki characters); the renderer's pending queue (PENDING_OUTPUT_CHARS) still counts characters.
6. npm test: a first full run on the saturated machine had 17 load-sensitive failures outside P2 (PowerShell / cmd timeouts in cli-install-shared, launch-reset.int, ptyhost.int node host, git, mcp timing); the rerun on a quieter machine passed completely (267 files, 4,027 tests). test/real-app-harness needed verify-tauri-pty.mjs in scripts/real-app-run.mjs (added, Tauri and Windows only, skipped without the flood helper).
7. `scripts/sp5/run.mjs` and the check-build `spike_pty*` commands still use the SP5 plane (`plane.rs`); they stay for the SP5 comparison and go with R1.

#### Review fixes (2026-11-07)

All 9 confirmed findings fixed (2 critical, 2 major, 5 minor; the branch `tauri-pkg-P2` was checked out in the builder's worktree, so the fixes are on `tauri-pkg-P2-fixes` = `tauri-pkg-P2` + one commit).

1. **Critical, coalescing / watchdog timer fired before its id was stored** (output.rs): the task looked its id up in a slot filled after `Scheduler::after` returned, so a timer that fired while the arming thread was preempted returned without flushing and left `st.timer` set for good (a quiet cell froze until 128 KiB or a keystroke). Now every arm bumps a generation in the channel state; the task takes the state lock first (held by `arm` until the id is stored) and runs when its generation is current. Same for the pause watchdog. Test `a_timer_that_fires_before_its_arm_returned_still_flushes` (a scheduler whose task runs 30 ms before `after` returns).
2. **Critical, window frozen after a reload** (ptyPort.ts, batch.rs, pty_transport.rs): the page released by its own count, and shared buffers of the old attach (still posting until the new page's attach stopped its batcher) put that count ahead of the new batcher's, which ignored every release from then on. Now the page passes an attach id to `mc_port_attach` (`attach`, the page session UUID; plain characters only), the shared buffer's additional data is `{"mcPort":"pty","a":<attach id>,"n":<seq>}`, the page drops (and gives back) buffers of another attach and releases `upto: n` (the host's number; pull and Channel are per attach, so their count equals the host's and they keep it). A failed post is released at its own number (was `released + 1`), and a reached cap without any release for `CAP_TIMEOUT` (3 s, like the pause watchdog) is lifted and counted (`capTimeouts` in the stats), so a lost release can no longer freeze a window. Tests: vitest (release by `n`, stale buffers dropped and released), batch.rs (`a_cap_without_any_release_is_lifted_after_the_timeout`, `a_lost_post_does_not_take_an_in_flight_slot`), pty_transport.rs (additional data, attach id check), real app `verify-tauri-pty.mjs reload` (below).
3. **Major, resize during a spawn lost** (engine.rs, manager.rs): a size posted while PtyManager::spawn ran reached neither the pty nor the early-size table but was recorded in the replay tail. The engine keeps it on the terminal (`Spawning`) and applies it when spawn returned; PtyManager's slot keeps a size that comes before its pty is set (like input and pauses) and applies it in `set_pty`. Tests `a_resize_during_the_spawn_reaches_the_pty` (engine and manager, `mode con` reports 100 x 40 with a held spawn).
4. **Major, last output of a paused terminal lost at its exit** (unix.rs, conpty.rs, manager.rs): the reader now opens at the child's exit and is never paused again (Unix `Gate::drain` before the 200 ms exit drain, which used to stop a paused reader with the output unread; Windows `set_closing` in the waiter, where a paused terminal with the conpty.dll never reported its exit until an ack or the watchdog). PtyManager keeps the slot in `entries` while the sink handles the exit, so the engine's drain can still resume it. Test `a_paused_terminal_that_ends_delivers_its_last_output_before_the_exit` (manager, 3 rounds; fails without the waiter change: no exit at all on Windows). The Unix half is compile-checked only (no Mac).
5. **Minor, replay frame not atomic with live output** (engine.rs, output.rs): `OutputChannel::replay_with` queues the replay frame inside the channel's state lock, so output flushed after the snapshot always follows the replay.
6. **Minor, stale UTF-8 decoder after a kill / respawn and a dropped BOM** (ptyFrames.ts, frame.rs, engine.rs, TerminalManager.ts): new frame kind 5 `reset` (empty payload), sent by the engine when it spawns a terminal under an id a window shows, before any of its output; TerminalManager forgets the decoder of a disposed cell; the output and replay decoders use `ignoreBOM: true` (a BOM stays U+FEFF as with node-pty's StringDecoder). Tests: vitest (reset, forget, BOM in output and replay), engine `a_respawn_starts_with_a_reset_frame`.
7. **Minor, flaky in-flight cap test** (batch.rs): the first messages are posted one by one and waited for, then the cap is asserted; `exit_frames_keep_their_place_behind_the_output` waits for the exit frame instead of a 200 ms quiet gap (it failed once on the loaded machine).
8. **Minor, lock-order cycle in `stats_json`** (engine.rs): the terminal list is collected first, the channels are read without the `terms` lock (as `set_flow_control` does).
9. **Minor, duplicate `allow-mc-port-pull`** in capabilities/main.json: removed.

Verification of the fixes: see the P2 row.
### Review fixes: U1 (2026-11-07)

All 3 confirmed findings of the U1 review are fixed; none was skipped. Committed on `tauri-pkg-U1-review` (made from `tauri-pkg-U1`, which is checked out in another worktree; fast-forward `tauri-pkg-U1` to it when merging).

| finding | fix | checked by |
|---|---|---|
| major: a download whose connection stalls after the headers waited forever; the 2 min watchdog only set the error, the hung entry stayed, every retry joined it and never sent a new request | `NetBackend::stream_to` waits at most `DOWNLOAD_STALL` (60 s, electron-updater's socket inactivity timeout) for the response headers and for each body chunk; when it fires the download fails with `NO_RESPONSE`, the partial file is removed, the coordinator clears the entry and the next check / install downloads again. `NetBackendOptions::download_stall` (tests use 500 ms). The coordinator watchdog is unchanged (1.1.4 did not cancel either) | new `tests/net_backend.rs` case: server sends `Content-Length` + 10 bytes then goes silent; the download ends with `Error` + `NO_RESPONSE` within the deadline, no partial file, then `check` + `download` sends a second request and ends `Ready` with the verified payload |
| minor: the 30 min background check used `interval` with Burst, so a resume after sleep / hibernate ran one check per missed half hour back to back | `set_missed_tick_behavior(MissedTickBehavior::Delay)`: one check after a resume, like `setInterval` | code review (tokio documents Delay as one tick then the period from now) |
| minor: the status bar install window could not be created while the startup gate's hidden window (same label `update-gate`) waited for its disposal, so the click did nothing; the late disposal would also have destroyed the new window | the startup gate's window is kept in `UpdateService::lingering`; `open_install_window` takes it, disposes it and waits (up to 5 s) until the label is free before creating its own window; `dispose_after_main` disposes only what is still in `lingering`, so it never touches the install window | clippy / build; the gate and running-app cases of `verify-update-gate.mjs` (the click inside that second is not driven by the harness) |

Verification: cargo fmt --check clean; clippy -D warnings for unimux-update + unimux-app, all targets, with dev-overrides,test-bridge and with default features, clean; `cargo test -p unimux-update` 95 unit + 3 + 6 `net_backend` (new stalled-download case) green; `cargo test -p unimux-app --features dev-overrides,test-bridge` 83 passed; `node scripts/verify-update-gate.mjs --build` **27/27**, then a second run without rebuilding **27/27**. No TS changed.

### U1 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. **verify-update-wait still blocked**: update:get answers now; the script stops at `cell:runtime` / `cell:spawn` (P3, P4): the detached grid's agent is not counted. Rerun when P3 / P4 land.
2. **macOS**: no in-place engine until U5, so the Mac offers every update as the download page (status `available` + `portable: true`, like a 1.1.4 app that cannot replace itself); `hand_over` refuses on macOS. `cargo clippy --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p unimux-update` no longer builds on this machine: unimux-update now depends on unimux-net (ring needs a C cross compiler, the same gap as `-p unimux-app`, U2 open issue 5). macOS CI must build it.
3. **No bare-installer fallback**: 1.1.4 installed without its window when the window could not start; the Tauri NSIS installer cannot relaunch the app (no `--force-run` until U3's hooks), so a window that cannot start fails the hand-over with the status error (retry possible). Revisit with U3.
4. Gate window: a page that fails to load or a WebView2 renderer that dies does not cancel the gate (1.1.4 `did-fail-load` / `render-process-gone`); the gate still fails open through its own timeouts and a destroyed window cancels it. The full-screen rule (shown without focus) uses SHQueryUserNotificationState; with a real full-screen app it is unverified (VM / person, like U2 open issue 1).
5. The gate window is hidden, not destroyed, by the flow and disposed 1 s after the main window shows: destroying the only webview first let the WebView2 browser process shut down and the main window then never got a page (seen in the harness).
6. `update:busy` answers `{swap: false, login: false}` until A2 / A3 register their probes (`UpdateService::add_busy_probe`).
7. Portable detection keeps 1.1.4's `PORTABLE_EXECUTABLE_DIR / _FILE`; 1.2.0 ships no portable build, so it is always false today.
8. The prestage (runtime copy + warm start when the download is verified) and the hand-over share the staging folder of this process (`runtime-<ver>.partial-<pid>`): they are serialized by a lock in `UpdateService` (a race made one harness run fail before the fix).
9. U2's own harness (`verify-update-window.mjs`) was not rerun (it needs its own payload build); the U1 gate case runs the same window process end to end (installer, relaunch, ready).

### S1 open issues (2026-11-07)

1. IPC: only the read-only channels and the history store's own channels are registered (`ssh:vault:info`, `ssh:storage:status`, `ssh:knownHosts:list`, `ssh:history:*`). The key, password and identity-password channels, `ssh:vault:changed`, `ssh:passphrases:forget` and the connect / prompt channels are S4; pruning the passwords of deleted hosts and identities after every state change is the coordinator's `stateChanged` (S3). S3 / S4 take the stores from `ctx.ssh` (`vault().await`, `known_hosts().await`, `history()`), lock them only for one call and run writes on the blocking pool.
2. Unpaired surrogates: a label cut by `.slice(0, 80)` inside an emoji is a lone surrogate in 1.1.4 (written as `\udXXX`); a Rust string holds U+FFFD there. Only reachable outside the IPC limits (zod caps the label at 80 UTF-16 units before the vault slices it). The golden test maps such escapes to U+FFFD on both sides.
3. `frecency` uses Rust's `f64::log2` (the platform libm) where V8 uses its own ieee754 port; a last-ulp difference could only change which of two exactly tied entries is evicted at the 2,000-entry limit. All 735 rule rows (frecency values included) match on this machine.
4. `parse_keeping_infinity` marks Infinity with the strings `"﷐Infinity"` / `"﷐-Infinity"`; a store file holding exactly that string would read it as a number (U+FDD0 is a noncharacter; the app never writes it).
5. macOS: the vault's open asks the cipher for its storage like the 1.1.4 constructor (startSsh), so on a Mac the Keychain is read when the stores open after the main window (as in 1.1.4); there is no eager decrypt for the Keychain. The POSIX 0700 / 0600 tests (`json_file::private_modes_on_posix`, `stores::creates_the_ssh_directory_0700_and_the_files_0600`) are `cfg(unix)` and have not run: the macOS CI job must run `cargo test -p unimux-core -p unimux-ssh`.
6. No real-app run of the check build in this package: the Tauri verify-ssh-* scripts stay blocked by S3 (`ssh:sessions`, `ssh:connect`); the wiring is covered by the app unit test `commands::ssh::tests` and verify-tauri-lifecycle's plan check (updated, not re-run here).
7. The history batch timer is a short-lived thread per batch holding a weak reference (like an unref'd setTimeout); the quit flush runs in the wind-down's BEFORE_QUIT phase on the blocking pool.

### S3 open issues (2026-11-07, fast mode)

Verification run: `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy -p unimux-ssh -p unimux-app --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p unimux-ssh -p unimux-app` (all green, one run). Not run (fast mode): the Docker SP7 matrix with the real login core (`node scripts/sp7/matrix.mjs`), any login against a real or in-process SSH server, real-app checks, macOS.

1. **No login against a server yet.** The login core was only tested against a closed port, a silent server and an abort; the auth order, the timers and the control are unit-tested. The acceptance (same outcome, SshErrorCode, methods and algorithms as 1.1.4 in the SP7 Docker matrix; host key base64 equal to 1.1.4 known_hosts entries) is open: run scripts/sp7/matrix.mjs with `login::login` instead of the probe (or S4's in-process russh test server).
2. **No owner yet.** `HostRouter`'s slots are empty until P5 (cells), T1 (SFTP) and D2 (DB tunnels) plug in (`ctx.ssh_link.owners.set(...)`, then `LoginRegistry::login` / `answer`, and their `sshStatus` / `exit` messages go to `coordinator.on_host_message`). Until then `ssh:connect` ends with `ptyHostCrashed` and a DB tunnel with `dbHostCrashed`, like 1.1.4 with no host running. The SFTP event route (`ctx.ssh_link.events.sftp`) is set by T1. `killed` (cell:kill, MCP stopCells) and `host_crashed` are P3 / P4 / P5 callers; nothing calls them yet.
3. **Error table**: the SP7 `error_code` table is kept as measured (a russh `Disconnect` / `HUP` maps to `unknown`, as ssh2's DISCONNECT error does); a server that closes without an error message reads `unknown` from the login, while 1.1.4's owners report `reset` for a close without any error event. Check in the matrix.
4. **Deliberate differences**: a keyboard-interactive exchange stops after 64 info requests (ssh2 has no limit); a key the login cannot read is skipped before anything is sent (1.1.4 never reaches that, main checked the key when it was stored); a cancelled keyboard question ends the login at once instead of sending an empty answer first (same outcome, `cancelled`); a re-key must show the host key accepted at login (ssh2 asks only once); the client id is russh's (`SSH-2.0-russh_0.65.1`), not ssh2's.
5. **Debug lines** (MC_SSH_DEBUG=1): only one handshake line (kex, host key, cipher, MAC) is reported; ssh2 printed its own packet log up to USERAUTH. Nothing consumes `LoginEvents::debug` yet (the owners decide).
6. Not ported (PORTING.md `partial` rows): a saved passphrase checked again after the key changed, the debug-on connect, the not-remembered half of the 3-cell D7 case, a failing trust write, a failing password write / prune, the identity password save and its prune. `ssh:passphrases:forget` and the other key / password channels stay S4's.
7. macOS: platform-neutral code, not cross-checked (unimux-ssh pulls ring, like wave 2 noted).

| M2 | `tauri-pkg-M2-fixes` | 0f953b6 | verified (Windows; automation answers MC_NOT_YET until M3) |
| U5 | `tauri-pkg-U5-fixes` | 855a0c3 | done on Windows / Linux; macOS never run |

Before the merges the main tree held the uncommitted second security review of the credential helper rule (TS side) and a files-path golden fix; committed first as 78ab8ce.

Conflicts were in the docs (PROGRESS / PLAN / ARCHITECTURE / PORTING rows, merged per package; the P2 and U1 notes now sit under "Wave 2 packages: open issues"), in `scripts/dump-goldens.mjs` (every package added goldens), `scripts/real-app-run.mjs` (the union of the acceptance script lists), `scripts/verify-tauri-lifecycle.mjs` (the wind-down plan now has S1's `ssh history flush` and D1's `db history save`), the Cargo manifests (unimux-files: G6 + G7 + G8 dependencies; unimux-accounts: A2 + A6; unimux-update: U1 + U5; one duplicate `minisign-verify` key in the workspace manifest removed; the lock regenerated by cargo) and in unimux-app:

- `main.rs`: U1 moved the window start into `start_main_app` (behind the startup gate); W7's `cells::startup::start(hooks, mcp)` and S1's `commands::ssh::app_started` went there, U5's `previous_mac_update` runs before the gate.
- `hooks.rs` / `context.rs` (H2 against W7): H2's cell file prune is registered as W7's prune step (`set_cell_file_prune`, W7 marks `cell-files-pruned`), H2's `Hooks::new(data_dir)` is built next to W7's `cells`; W7's forget-unless-alive hook now calls `Hooks::forget_files` (hook nonce + H2's cell files), forgetCell's codexHooked half stays W7's.
- `unimux-update/src/version.rs` (added by both U1 and U5): U5's `compare_versions` (BOM-aware trim, no NaN) with U1's `is_newer` and both test sets.
- `commands/files.rs` / `unimux-files`: G6, G7 and G8 registrations, modules and `jspath` helpers side by side.

Integration fixes (fix: commits):

1. 78ab8ce: gitTrust.ts allows only a space or tab between credential helper arguments (second security review of G2); the files-path goldens skip drive-relative paths to another drive (they depend on the run's `=C:` folder).
2. 33a2587: unimux-git's SAFE_CREDENTIAL_HELPER uses `[ \t]+` like gitTrust.ts (was JS `\s`, which let a NBSP or U+2028 through); test and test/golden/git-trust.jsonl regenerated (12 rows change to "needs trust").
3. 0e049d5: W7's forgetCell also drops H4's context tracker state of a removed cell once no process of it runs (index.ts forgetCell); verify-tauri-cells expects the `contextTracker` hook.
4. fb391d6: G8's note export picker fills D1's new `PickRequest::more_filters`.
5. 342400f: the Windows hand-over fills U5's new `HandOver::swap` (None; the Mac bundle swap is not wired into the coordinator yet).
6. 232585f: `startup_marks_are_the_fixed_renderer_marks_and_the_first_one_wins` failed once in the workspace run: the stores that open safeStorage in the background (S1 / D1 / A6) add a `safe-storage-*` mark at any moment; the test compares the other marks only.
7. f4b320e: `real-app-run.mjs` lists verify-update-gate.mjs (skipped without its payload installer) and verify-tauri-github.mjs, which test/real-app-harness.test.ts requires.
8. 38d21d7: smoke-tauri's raw-argument check expects `notes:image:save` to resolve (G8 implements it; it was the MC_NOT_YET probe).

### Open after wave 2

1. **No Mac and no CI run** (branch not pushed): the macOS halves of all wave 2 packages are compile-checked at most; unimux-app, unimux-update (ring through unimux-net) and unimux-ssh cannot be cross-checked for aarch64-apple-darwin here. U5 (bundle swap, Gatekeeper, translocation) and P2's pull transport on WKWebView have never run.
2. **P3 / P4 are the next blockers**: cell:* commands, the routing table from the StateController, the supervised PtyManager, the terminals' wind-down step, exit notifications, Monitor `set_cells`, `Hooks::claude_spawn` / prompts from cell:spawn, H4's `current()` for M4; verify-update-wait, verify-notify-actions and verify-git-layout stay blocked.
3. **Wiring still open**: accounts:* commands and the start-up recovery (A2, with A3 / A4 / A5), `update:busy` probes (A2 / A3), the macOS update engine in U1's coordinator (U5's `MacUpdater`), the DB coordinator and real DB hosts (S3, D4 / D7 / D9 / D11 / D12), github:publish (G3), the MCP automation (M3 / M4).
4. **P2 performance**: the SP5 echo / frame targets under a 19-cell flood are not proven within 1.1x of 1.1.4; rerun on an idle machine or the VM.
5. Two save-dialog paths: G8's note export has its own `show_save_dialog`, D1 added a save dialog to W4's desktop service; fold G8's into D1's when either is touched again.
6. **`dump-goldens.mjs --check` needs about 12 GB of heap** now (80 MB of goldens; with 4 GB it aborted with "JavaScript heap out of memory"). `npm run gen:check` as written will fail the same way in CI: write each golden file as it is made (or check it file by file) before CI runs gen:check.
7. Load-sensitive tests under a full parallel run: A2's kill test, P1's dev-server-tree kill test (release), and the vitest ConPTY cases the packages reported; all pass alone.
8. Branches and worktrees: the merged worktrees under `.claude/worktrees` were removed (none had a node_modules junction; -7 and -13 held only the stale pre-fix index of a branch whose fixes were committed from another worktree, removed with `--force` after checking the working tree matched that index) and `git worktree prune` run. Deleted with `git branch -d`: every merged `tauri-pkg-*` branch of this wave (P2, P2-fixes, U1, U1-review, W7, W7-fix, G2, G2-fixes, G6, G6-fix, G7, G7-fixes, G8, H2, H4, H4-fixes, S1, D1, D1-fixes, A2, A2-review, A6, M2, M2-fixes, U5, U5-fixes) and the merged `worktree-wf_3188d96a-*` branches. Kept: worktree `.claude/worktrees/wf_3188d96a-67a-38` with `tauri-pkg-A2-fixes` (= tauri-pkg-A2, merged): it holds an uncommitted earlier draft of A2's Codex lock fix (superseded by the committed ceca254); delete it when nobody needs the draft.

## Do zainstalowania / do decyzji użytkownika

### Decyzje podjęte 2026-11-06

1. Wszystko naraz: 1.2.0 wychodzi dopiero, gdy cała aplikacja jest w Rust + Tauri v2 i działa jak 1.1.4. Bez sidecara Node.
2. Wygląd zostaje: przyciski okna na Windows rysowane tak, żeby wyglądały jak dziś; własne okno aktualizacji (postęp, etapy, restart) także przy aktualizacjach między wersjami Tauri.
3. latest.yml i latest-mac.yml publikowane przy każdym wydaniu (aktualizacja z Electron 1.1.x do 1.2.0).
4. Klucz podpisywania aktualizacji wygenerowany, sekrety GitHub ustawione.

Szacunek: około 290 dni pracy agentów + 20-25 procent na integrację, czyli realnie 11-13 tygodni przy 6 agentach równolegle. Wersja testowa do sprawdzenia przed wydaniem.

### Decyzje podjęte 2026-11-07

1. **Bez wydania 1.1.5.** Poprawka reguły pomocnika poświadczeń git (credential.helper, dwa przeglądy bezpieczeństwa) trafia tylko do 1.2.0 na Tauri; 1.1.5 na Electronie nie wychodzi.
2. Gałąź integracyjna `tauri-1.2.0` jest na GitHubie.

### Nadal otwarte

1. **macOS: identyfikator com.ajzakomator.app zostaje** (zachowuje uprawnienia systemowe i zgodność z aktualizatorem 1.1.x). Po aktualizacji macOS raz zapyta o dostęp do pęku kluczy ("Zawsze zezwalaj"). Akceptujesz?
2. **Plan awaryjny.** Jeśli testy przejścia (SP3/SP4) nie wyjdą, potrzebne będzie wydanie pomostowe 1.1.5 na Electronie. Zgoda?
3. **Kopia testowych danych 1.1.4** (KOPIA, nie oryginał `%APPDATA%\unimux`) albo zgoda, żebym utworzył syntetyczne dane w 1.1.4 pod `MC_DATA_DIR` z testowymi hasłami SSH/DB i tokenem GitHub. Przyda się też F8: zanonimizowane prawdziwe pliki state.json można dopisać jako ziarna do `test/golden/normalize-seeds.jsonl` i uruchomić `npm run gen:goldens` (opcjonalnie).
4. **SP1: zastępcze kryterium akceptacji.** "Channel p50 < 2 ms przy 20 x 1 MB/s do 20 terminali xterm" nie spełnia żaden transport (sam renderer xterm nie wyrabia 60 fps przy takim obciążeniu). Propozycja: liczyć sam transport (wspólny bufor WebView2: p50 0,2-0,3 ms, spełnione), kryterium "do xterm" przenieść do SP5, a sprawdzenie WKWebView do W2 / pierwszego uruchomienia na Macu. Zgoda? Do tego czasu SP1 ma status "done", nie "verified".
5. **SP5: kryterium 4 spełnione częściowo.** 20 terminali po 1 MB/s trzyma 60 fps przy syntetycznym 20 MB/s i w większości przebiegów ConPTY, ale gdy ConPTY przepuszcza 13-20 MB/s, 45-72 klatki trwają ponad 50 ms (1.1.4 przy tym samym obciążeniu gubi ich więcej). Akceptujesz SP5 tak jak jest, czy zostawiamy to jako cel wydajności dla P2? Przy okazji: echo przy 19 zalewanych terminalach jest w medianie wolniejsze niż w 1.1.4 (13-28 ms wobec 9-13 ms); to zadanie dla P2.
6. **P1: zamknięcie komórki kończy wszystko, co z niej uruchomiono (Windows).** W 1.2.0 zamknięcie lub restart terminala kończy całe drzewo procesów (serwery deweloperskie agentów nie zostają w tle), ale też programy z oknem uruchomione z tej komórki (np. `code .`, gdy VS Code nie był otwarty, nowe okno `start "" cmd`, przeglądarka otwarta pierwszy raz przez `vite --open`). W 1.1.4 ginęły tylko programy podpięte do konsoli, a serwery uruchomione w tle często zostawały. Zostawiamy nowe zachowanie?
7. **X1: cztery skrypty na starej wersji Electron nie przechodzą** (smoke-automation, capture-readme --verify, verify-ssh-bar, verify-db-redis-ui; nie przechodzą też ze skryptami sprzed X1, więc to nie wina migracji). Naprawiamy je przed R1 (usunięciem Electrona), czy akceptujemy?
8. **D3: plik wektorów testowych 17 MB** (test/golden/db-sql.jsonl, obok 11 MB normalize-state.jsonl) jest w repozytorium. Zostawiamy, czy zmniejszyć losową część?
9. **D1 i M2: kolejne duże pliki wektorów testowych** (test/golden/db-ipc.jsonl 9,8 MB, test/golden/mcp-zod.jsonl 9,3 MB; razem ze wszystkimi wektorami test/golden ma teraz około 80 MB). Zostawiamy w repozytorium, czy zmniejszyć losowe części? Przy okazji: sprawdzenie wektorów (`npm run gen:check`) potrzebuje teraz około 12 GB pamięci; zanim CI je uruchomi, skrypt trzeba przerobić (zapis plik po pliku).
10. **W7f: verify-caption przy 200 procentach** (skrypt W1) na chwilę pokazuje okno na prawdziwym pulpicie (wbrew zasadzie "nic na pulpicie"). Poprawiamy w W1 (trzymać te przypadki poza ekranem)?
11. **A6: aplikacja OAuth GitHub z device flow.** Nadal wyłączona, jak w 1.1.4. Rejestrujemy ją (pozwoli na prawdziwy test logowania urządzeniem), czy zostaje bez niej?
12. **A2: kto rejestruje komendy accounts:* i odzyskiwanie przy starcie** - A5, czy osobny mały krok po A3, A4, A5 i W7?

### Do zrobienia po Twojej stronie (gdy będzie potrzebne)

1. **Obraz Windows dla VirtualBox** (np. oficjalna maszyna deweloperska Windows 11 albo ISO z licencją) do testu aktualizacji 1.1.4 -> 1.2.0 (spike SP3, potem U3). Nigdy na Twoim prawdziwym profilu.

4. ~~Brakuje Windows 11 SDK~~ zainstalowany (10.0.28000, sprawdzone 2026-11-06), buildy go używają. Można usunąć folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\unimux-build` (kopia SDK z NuGet, już niepotrzebna).

7. **Opcjonalnie: powtórzyć pomiar SP1 na bezczynnym komputerze** (bez gry i ciężkich programów): `npm run build:check && node scripts/spike-sp1.mjs --long`. Pomiary wave 0b szły przy 94-100 procent obciążenia CPU; kolejność transportów była zawsze ta sama, ale liczby bezwzględne warto powtórzyć.

Gdy zechcesz, wypchnij `tauri-1.2.0` (albo zgódź się na próbę CI na osobnej gałęzi): dopiero przebieg `tauri.yml` na windows-latest i macos-latest zamyka akceptację P1, B1 (`.int` na hoście Rust), B2 (oscrypt na macOS), W2 (mac-smoke) i resztę połówek macOS.

## User feedback 2026-11-06

- Update window: the text arrow looked wrong (fixed in resources/update-splash: drawn chevron), the window must not be always-on-top (fixed in src/updatesplash for Electron; required for U2), and the UAC prompt must come to the front (U2 requirement).
- Tauri check build: the system title bar shows above unimux's own title bar; W1 must remove it (decorations: false, own drag region, caption buttons like 1.1.4).
- 2026-11-06: an Edge dialog "cannot create data directory ...\unimux-sp6-*\headless\unimux-updater\webview2-broken\EBWebView" popped up on the user's desktop several times. Source: scripts/spike-update-window.mjs (SP6 fixes, worktree -22) deliberately hands WebView2 a file as its data folder. Gated at the wave 0b integration: the headless case runs only with UNIMUX_SPIKE_BROKEN_WEBVIEW=1 (commit "fix: SP6 spike runs the broken-WebView2 case only ..."). Still to do in U2: the writable-folder pre-check in the updater and the main app (PLAN.md U2/SP6).
